# Module notes — the long-form reference behind CLAUDE.md

**What this file is.** Until 2026-09-21 every one of these entries lived in `CLAUDE.md`,
which had grown to ~400 KB (~100k tokens — the whole context budget of the agent it was
written for). The operational map moved back to a short `CLAUDE.md`; the per-module
essays, the protocol payload table and the config-knob essay moved HERE, verbatim, so
nothing was lost and every rationale is still greppable. Each entry records *why* a
module is shaped the way it is, dated to the change that shaped it.

**How to use it.** Read `CLAUDE.md` first (the coding-agent map in the private checkout;
the public copy has `DEPLOY.md` for running things) — it names every module in one line
each and links here. Come here when the one-liner is not enough: before changing a module's
contract, when a comment cites a design decision you do not recognize, or when a
protocol message's payload matters.

**How to maintain it.** This is a reference, not a changelog. Dated rationale for a new
change belongs in `documentation/AVA_CHANGELOG.md` (with the commit hash) and in the
module's own docstring. Add or rewrite an entry here only when a module's *contract*
changes — what it reads, what it produces, what it refuses — and keep the entry
describing the current code, not the history of getting there. Precedence on conflict is
unchanged: **code > `AVA_STATUS.md` > `AVA_DESIGN.md` > this file**.

---

## Directory structure

```
client/          Cross-platform PyQt6 UI — no ML dependencies
  main.py        Entry point; accepts --server URL flag
  ui/            PyQt6 widgets (chat/chat-review/sleep/prompt/encounter/gossip/debug/persona/facts/
                 migrate/modules tabs, main_window; persona+facts share memory_editor.py)
  core/          backend_client.py (WebSocket client only)
  requirements.txt

server/          Linux-only GPU server (two-layer)
  server_config.json  Box configuration shared by inference, training, and recovery
  data/          chats/ (transcripts + sidecars), til/ (sources + protocols + wander),
                 assoc/ (library + access history), graph/ (tree + hand-edited aliases)
  watchdog.py    Thin, never-upgraded process supervisor: launches inference as a subprocess,
                 HTTP management API (port 8766) = launch/kill/status/restart + `git pull`
                 bootstrap + a GENERIC offline-job runner (POST /job/<name>) driven by the
                 git-tracked watchdog_jobs.json. Holds no project-specific logic (data layout,
                 config schema, training params) — those live in the inference server / repo
                 scripts so they upgrade with `git pull` (the watchdog is the process-tree root:
                 restarting it needs SSH, so it must almost never change).
  watchdog_jobs.json  Git-tracked manifest of GPU-exclusive / model-unloaded offline jobs
                 (train / wipe). Re-read per request, so adding a job is a repo edit +
                 pull, not a watchdog change. Each job = argv + optional progress-journal/log/sync.
  convert_moe_bnb4bit.py  One-time GPU CLI: stream a bf16 Qwen3.5-MoE checkpoint into a local
                 per-expert bitsandbytes NF4 checkpoint (unsloth's gpt-oss layout: `experts.
                 gate_up_projs.<i>` / `down_projs.<i>` as pre-quantized `Linear4bit`, every other
                 Linear the loader would quantize pre-quantized too, `mtp.*` dropped, the skip list
                 written into `quantization_config` + a `unsloth_perexpert_bnb4bit_experts` marker
                 the loader keys on). Exists because transformers' bnb path quantizes `nn.Linear`
                 only and Qwen3.5's experts are fused 3-D parameters. `--plan` is GPU-free.
  wipe_state.py  CLI the watchdog's `wipe` job invokes: deletes regenerable reflection state
                 (+ optional chats/review archive), repoints the base model; prints a JSON result.
  server.log     Inference server output (created at runtime)
  watchdog.log   Watchdog process output (created at runtime)
  config_schema.py  **The ONE schema of server_config.json** (2026-09-17): every documented
                 knob as a `Field` (dotted path, kind, in-code default, tooltip) grouped into
                 `Tab`s, plus the dotted-path helpers (`dget`/`dhas`/`dset`) and
                 `backfill_defaults(config)`. PyQt-free / stdlib-only, so it has two consumers
                 that cannot drift: `settings.py` renders it, and the inference server's boot
                 back-fill (`server.py::_backfill_server_config`, loaded by path since
                 `server/` is not on sys.path) walks it and writes every knob ABSENT from the
                 box's config into the file with its default — so an operator finds each new
                 setting in the JSON after the next run instead of in the code that reads it.
                 Behaviour-preserving by two contracts: a value written is the one the reader
                 would have used anyway (each default must match its reader's — the self-test
                 cross-checks decay / deliberation / activity_log / chat_worklog), and a null
                 that means something other than absence is never written
                 (`chat_repetition_penalty` absent ⇒ 1.1, null ⇒ off; `write_null` marks the
                 readers that treat null like absent — `chat_degen.*`, `graph.til_max_age_days`).
                 `backfill=False` marks box STATE never written (`model_id`, `adapter_id`);
                 `derive` computes `reflect_context_length == context_length`. A key present at
                 any value is left alone. Self-test: `python config_schema.py`.
  settings.py    PyQt6 GUI settings editor over server_config.json — surfaces the FULL
                 documented knob set (base model_id + base_quant, adapter_id, context/reflect
                 lengths, chat degeneration guards, offline-SFT params, the wall_clock
                 consolidation/decay curve, checkin, reach-out pacing, deliberation, the
                 reflection/retrieval kill-switches, the facts tree, the associative library,
                 gossip, the public API, logging), not just the handful a fresh config
                 writes. Schema-driven — the schema is `config_schema.py` (imported, not a
                 second copy), each Field with an in-code default matching the server's own +
                 a doc-cited tooltip. Round-trip safe: loads the whole
                 JSON, writes back ONLY managed keys (nested groups merged via dotted-path
                 dget/dset), so unknown/unmanaged keys — incl. nested ones like
                 consolidation.dialogue/fact — are preserved verbatim; validates on Save, writes a
                 timestamped .bak first. No restart is triggered — edits apply on the next server
                 reload, exactly as a hand-edit would. Needs PyQt6 (run with the client venv:
                 `source ../.venv/bin/activate && python settings.py [--config PATH]`).
  reflection_run.py  Headless CLI: run the server-side reflection loop with no client connected
  snapshot_state.py  GPU-free CLI/copy/tar exporter and persona-bundle producer. Captures
                 inference data (minus scratch/activity/persona-history), chats, TIL,
                 prompts, config, current digest, active adapter, and linked forensic
                 training snapshot when available. Portable adapter_id; code commit +
                 dirty status and base/MiniLM repository IDs in MANIFEST.json.
                 CURRENT LIMITS: no server/data/assoc/ (generated protocols, relations,
                 access history), no graph aliases, no associative embedder/revision or
                 environment pin, and no transaction across changing live roots.
                 Export/import is built and exercised; full causal closure is not.
  inference/     Inference server role
    server.py    Entry point; WebSocket server (port 8765)
    core/        inference_backend, llm_shared, chat_logger, rag_engine, reflection_writer, tension,
                 reflection_runner (server-side consolidation/revision/branch loop),
                 reflection_chunking, reflection_source, reflection_config, reflection_prompts,
                 reflection_staging, reflection_memory, user_digest (per-person user portraits),
                 state_wipe (destructive reset helpers),
                 chat_sidecar (also the ONE definition of "this file is a transcript" —
                 `is_chat_session_json` over `SIDECAR_SUFFIXES`; a chat's stem owns several
                 files and ~a dozen sites enumerate the dir looking for transcripts, so a
                 suffix missing there gets a sidecar indexed into chat RAG as something Ava
                 said), chat_facts, branch_replay, model_family
                 (per-family CoT/chat profiles), runtime_state (typed ModelRuntime/Session/
                 EncounterState owners for server.py's shared singleton state), til_wander
                 (TIL/wander self-directed learning subsystem, configure()-wired),
                 encounter_run (Ava-meets-AI encounter loop, configure()-wired),
                 unified_memory (DGX Spark / GB10: detect that CUDA's pool IS system RAM and pin
                 `device_map={"": 0}` — accelerate plans from MemFree, which excludes the page
                 cache, and offloads to a "CPU" that is the same memory; since 2026-09-17 ANY
                 single-GPU box is pinned too, because the planner's estimate refused a
                 gemma-4-31B that fit with 28 GiB free — `AVA_LOAD_PLACEMENT=planner` opts
                 out), fast_load (clone a
                 tensor out of the safetensors mmap before the device copy — 0.16 GB/s from
                 file-backed pages on this box vs 58 GB/s from the heap — and `HF_HUB_OFFLINE`
                 for an on-disk model; both patch transformers' loader from OUR side, applied
                 on unified memory only), sdpa_gqa (wraps transformers' `use_gqa_in_sdpa` to
                 say no when head_dim > 256: flash is the only SDPA kernel that takes
                 `enable_gqa`, so Gemma-4's head_dim-512 global layers otherwise run the math
                 backend with an fp32 32 x L^2 score matrix; installed by both the inference
                 loader and train_cycle, every box), moe_bnb_experts (per-expert `nn.Linear` experts class
                 for Qwen3.5-MoE swapped into transformers before a converted bnb-4bit
                 checkpoint is built — the class unsloth ships for gpt-oss, for a family it
                 does not ship it for),
                 reflection_service (Sleep-tab reflection-run handlers/lifecycle,
                 configure()-wired), generation (model-facing chat/branch/reflect-generate
                 layer, configure()-wired), session_ops (chat-session CRUD handlers,
                 configure()-wired), mgmt_http (inference-side HTTP sidecar, port 8767:
                 artifacts/export/chats-sync/precision/training-review — the read-mostly,
                 data-layout-coupled endpoints moved off the watchdog so they upgrade with
                 `git pull`), api_http (public OpenAI-compatible API for external tools,
                 its OWN port 8000, enabled by default via `api.enabled`; requests are never logged),
                 training_review (the Training review tab's entry list: the
                 latest build's render projected to the reviewed final turn, duplicate rows
                 collapsed, live locked targets patched over the frozen copy, plus the
                 `models/` adapter lineage (`list_adapters`) feeding the regenerate bar's
                 Adapter dropdown — pure stdlib,
                 served by the sidecar so the tab can run from a remote UI box, and loaded
                 by path by that client when it has no connection, so there is ONE
                 definition of the projection),
                 idle_scheduler (generic autonomous-idle-job scheduler: owns the shared idle
                 clock, crash-safe wake-lock, and the GPU asyncio.Lock; outreach/synthesis/
                 checkin/wander timers register only outside `sole` mode; upkeep jobs
                 (background_reflection/worklog_sweep/graph_rebuild and others) keep their OWN interval_s
                 — different events at different frequencies. Each job runs as its OWN
                 supervising task: no shared tick, no registration-order priority, so co-due
                 jobs ALL fire and are serialized only by the GPU lock. A job's ready/consumed
                 policy may read only its own state — cross-job policy goes at the point of
                 effect (see reachout_gate), never in a gate; configure()-wired),
                 worklog (first-person episodic worklog: a leaf sink like activity_log —
                 Ava's durable, keep-forever, first-person record of what she DID, one
                 entry per meaningful episode, recorded at each episode-close; read by
                 the deliberation executive to choose what she does next),
                 modules (the module workbench: a pass described as DATA — what it reads,
                 what conditions it, what it produces — and runnable on its own against one
                 chosen chat, RETURNING the produced value instead of writing it, which is
                 what makes a pass experimentable and, later, chainable; v0 = chat_facts,
                 nothing injected, no sink. `chat_facts`/`chat_summary`/`til_facts` mirror
                 passes that already run; `fact_fetch` mirrors the live facts-tree fallback, while `assoc_fetch`
                 exercises the default associative retrieval channel), ...
    prompts/     chat_prompt.txt, api_client_system_prompt.txt, rag_prompt.txt,
                 rag_memory_prompt.txt, rag_wander_prompt.txt,
                 surface_prompt.txt, sleep_prompt.txt, revision_prompt.txt, branch_prompt.txt,
                 exchange_anchor_prompt.txt (per-exchange ABOUT + TAGS, in the exchange's
                 own language — see core/exchange_anchor.py)
    data/        Reflection state and journals, organized by lifetime (gitignored):
      hot/         Working set still moving through consolidation:
        memory/      Decisions Ava recalls: rag_memory.jsonl, weights_persona.jsonl
        consolidation/  Bookkeeping: consolidation_anchors.jsonl
      scratch/     Disposable: sft_render.jsonl + sft_quarantine.jsonl (regenerated per cycle)
    requirements.txt
  training/      Offline from-scratch consolidation→SFT build (GPU; runs while the server is idle)
    build_dataset.py From-scratch corpus assembler (pure/GPU-free): frozen bundles → one row/exchange, WALL-CLOCK age + per-row LR ramp, chronological oldest-first, per-host FACT injection (persona injection retired — persona reaches CoT implicitly), cap-age user contamination; `include_fresh` additionally emits untrained PREVIEW rows (`BuildRow.preview`, lr 0) for not-yet-eligible chats (inside `rag_only_window_h`, or `chat_reflected`-only) so they reach the review render without entering the trained corpus
    build_history.py builds.jsonl — append-only build log (built_at/seed/outcome/snapshot_dir; age_of is instrumentation only now)
    preview_build.py GPU-free preview snapshot of the NEXT build's corpus (models/snapshots/preview-<ts>/: sft_render.jsonl with include_fresh + build_meta outcome "preview") — written by the inference process at the end of a training-lite reflection run when Sleep's "Include fresh chats" is on, so the Training review→repair pass can precede the real cycle; only the newest preview is kept, real build-* snapshots untouched
    build_snapshot.py Immutable per-build forensic snapshot (models/snapshots/<build_id>/: render + wander + persona digest + config)
    train_cycle.py   Entry point: build corpus → fresh LoRA on frozen base (never resumed) → validation DISABLED so promote unconditionally → save/repoint adapter + write snapshot + builds.jsonl line
    decay.py         Wall-clock decay: WallClockConfig + pure helpers (wall_clock_age_hours / lr_multiplier_hours / verbatim_rag_weight_hours / gist_rag_weight_hours / rag_weight_hours); configured via server_config.json → consolidation.wall_clock
    validation_switch.py  Master switch for the retained probe (VALIDATION_ENABLED = False; deliberate policy)
    ledger.py        Append-only anchor ledger (consolidation_anchors.jsonl); read for persona/fact hosts (stage fold retained but no longer advances)
    render.py        Inference-parity renderer for a build row (`render_example_text` / `build_messages` / `assert_parity`), reused by `build_dataset` (§5a). ONE resolved target per exchange — the decay-driven verbatim-variant copies AND the IDEAL prior-preservation regularizer were both retired. Removed reworded-variant / regularizer designs: documentation/AVA_DESIGN_LEGACY.md
    label_policy.py  Final-turn-only loss masking + the per-row user-turn unmask (cap-age contamination)
    migrate.py       One-shot, idempotent artifact migrations
    selftest.py      GPU-free pipeline self-test
    DESIGN.md        Consolidation/decay design notes
  reflections/   Reflection archive tree (gitignored except README) — reviewable, revertable
    README.md        Layout doc (the only tracked file)
    <run_id>/        Per-completed-reflection bundle: run/ (run-log snapshot), artifacts/
                     (memory·ledger·sidecar deltas the run committed), persona/ (snapshot of
                     the live persona digest as of this run — the sole product of an
                     all-already-reflected run), users/ (snapshot of the per-person user
                     portraits as of this run — a portrait is prompt-injected on every turn
                     with that person, so "what was she working from when she answered like
                     that?" needs an answer that survives the next regeneration; all
                     portraits are copied, not only ones this run rewrote, since the
                     archive's contract is the live state at commit time), adapter/ (copy of
                     the produced LoRA adapter,
                     only when the train stage ran). No manifest or replay index is stored.
                     Written by inference/core/reflection_archive.py

  graph/         Offline facts-tree fold (GPU-free; the consumer both fact-protocol lanes
                 were written for). Reads the immutable `<stem>.facts.json` protocols and
                 folds them into node → facet → claim → occurrence, with entity mentions as
                 edges. **Derived and disposable — never a store**: the protocols stay the
                 sources, so a better resolver is a rebuild, not a migration. See
                 FACTS_TREE.md (design + staging) and graph/DESIGN.md (implementation).
    read.py      The AUTHORITATIVE read set — `data/chats/` + `data/til/snippets/<kind>/`
                 only. Load-bearing: 38 of the 61 `.facts.json` on this box are copies
                 (persona lineage, reflection checkpoint, review archive), and a naive glob
                 triple-counts facts and reports the duplication as corroboration
    nodes.py     Typed node ids (`person:` / `entity:` / `topic:` reserved / `unknown:`),
                 which is where the two lanes' incompatible subject namespaces are
                 reconciled — a chat subject is a person key, a TIL subject an entity
                 mention, and a flat space is how `nvidia` becomes a person. Stage-1 tiers
                 1–2 (exact + the hand-edited alias table); the residue is typed `unknown:`
                 and COUNTED rather than guessed (`chat_facts.UNSPECIFIED`'s rule). A casing
                 heuristic was declined — it fails on the Russian half of the corpus
    fold.py      `(lane, fact_class)` → facet, which repairs a real cross-lane collision
                 (TIL writes reported events as `stated`, meaning "the text asserts this",
                 not "someone holds this view") and makes the corpus's dominant bucket
                 structurally unreadable as knowledge: 73% of claims are `position`
    store.py     `data/graph/tree.json` write/read + the consumer-facing read API. No
                 migration path by construction; `read_tree` returns None for missing,
                 corrupt AND unrecognised — all three mean *rebuild*
    blob.py      The tree's READ side: what a retrieval pass may choose from
                 (`node_index`), how its answer is parsed (`parse_selection`), and what the
                 tree is allowed to say back (`render_blob`). FACTS_TREE.md §10 **consumer
                 5**, the channel the doc lists last and marks "off by default … here to be
                 argued about"; its only caller so far is the `fact_fetch` workbench module,
                 which injects nothing. Two rules are enforced HERE rather than in a prompt,
                 both safety properties: `person:_self` yields nothing ever (§10) — a pass
                 picking it is *reported*, not silently filtered — and the blob carries
                 `property`+`event` only, with `position`/`report` returned separately,
                 counted, and rendered attributed (`attribution_line`). **The retrieval axis
                 is the mention edge, not node ownership**, forced by the data: `chat_facts`
                 files a fact under its person, so 3 nodes of 107 own any claim while 106
                 carry only mention edges, and every topic a message is about owns nothing
    build.py     CLI: `python -m graph.build [--nodes|--node ID|--find|--unresolved|--json]`.
                 Browse flags are read-only. `--unresolved` is the one to read first
    selftest.py  `python -m graph.selftest` — every module plus the seams, sealed from the
                 real corpus

assoc/           **The associative facts library — a NEW project (ASSOCIATIVE_MEMORY.md), not part of
                 Ava's runtime.** Milestone 1 (retrieval tier) built 2026-09-09: `store.py` (documents
                 with keys + versions, append-only chats, chunk ids structural and shared across
                 versions), `kinds/` (registry: chat / tech_doc / news / article / structured / code —
                 Markdown splitter, tree-sitter splitter + parser witness, structure witness),
                 `protocol.py` (fact-line grammar + parser ported from Ava's chat_facts), `witness.py`
                 (deferred LLM witness: chunk-marked groups, anchoring + grounding, ingest report),
                 `lex.py` / `glossary.py` (L1: pymorphy3 + simplemma lemmas, BM25), `dense.py` (BGE-M3 +
                 FAISS, hash stand-in for offline benches), `codebook.py` (L2 cells over term
                 embeddings — milestone 2, 2026-09-09: query-time placement of unseen cue words,
                 same-word exclusion, coverage; tier 2 beats tier 1 on the ladder), `dedup.py`
                 (tiers 2–3 + the equivalence check → merge / contests / supersedes),
                 `authority.py` (families + damped bipartite PageRank), `relations.py` / `graph.py` /
                 `activation.py` / `spread.py` / `aha.py` (milestone 3, 2026-09-09: typed relations, the
                 edge table, L4 activation in sqlite, the beam spread, standing needs + the aha judge;
                 gate verdict: tier 3 even with tier 2 cold → default tier 2, `Library(tier=3)` the knob),
                 `senses.py` / `infer.py` (milestone 4, 2026-09-09: sense induction + the three pivot
                 bridges via `Library.pivot`, learned `mentions` strength, `Activation(variable_d=True)`,
                 inferred edges off by default), `fold.py` (exact-tier claims, versioned supersession), `budget.py` (per-script token estimate, fit stamps, oversize split),
                 `puller.py` (tier-1 lexical + dense over three grains, scope-filtered, explainable),
                 `selection.py` (policy, three-grain catalogue, fast path, ordinal picks, budget spend),
                 `render.py`, `rebuild.py` (manifest, staleness, atomic swap), `library.py` (the §1.2
                 API), `facade.py` (OpenAI-compatible chat + context endpoints, prefix-matched
                 sessions). Benches under `assoc/bench/` (`server/.venv/bin/python -m pytest
                 assoc/bench -q`; `ASSOC_MODEL=1` adds the gemma-4-31B in-process harness). `ava_import.py`
                 imports an Ava snapshot export (chats + TIL, her protocols as the documents' protocols) and
                 `bench/measure_real.py` measures the tier ladder on it with labels by construction (real-corpus
                 numbers 2026-09-10 in ASSOCIATIVE_MEMORY.md §12: tier 2 beats tier 1, tier 3 even). The
                 library never loads a model — every generation is an injected `generate_fn`.
tools/           Standalone CLI utilities
  deploy.py         HTTP client for the watchdog API (pull/restart/status/logs)
  publish_public.py Export HEAD as the public copy (~/ava-public): drops CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md,
                    applies stand-ins + hostname redactions, leak gate, rsync; `--scan` is the
                    pre-commit private-data check. PRIVATE-ONLY: holds the redaction list and
                    excludes itself from every export
  requirements.txt  (no external dependencies — stdlib only)

config.json      Repo root — {selected_model, server_url}

README.md        Unified philosophy and conceptual document (the "what")
readme_ru.md     Russian translation of the README
documentation/   Maintained design documentation, split by rate of change (see its README.md
                 for the full contract): AVA_DESIGN.md (stable architecture baseline),
                 AVA_STATUS.md (implementation truth table — Built/Partial/Logged-only/
                 Pending/Removed), AVA_CHANGELOG.md (curated technical history),

                 AVA_DESIGN_LEGACY.md — the frozen legacy design document (formerly the

SPARK_LOADING.md **READ BEFORE TOUCHING THE LOADER.** Why a model load on the DGX Spark takes
                 minutes instead of seconds (mmap→CUDA copies at 0.16 GB/s; CUDA "free"
                 excludes the page cache; bnb never quantizes fused MoE experts), the fixes,
                 the 30-second benchmark that re-verifies the numbers

                 box freezes (memory, not heat)
GOSSIP.md        Same-user gossip: serving + driver + both-side logging built; residual plans marked
FACTS_TREE.md    Implementation brief for the facts tree — the offline fold over the two
                 fact-protocol lanes (`chat_facts` / `til_facts`) and the consumer design
                 they were both written for (implemented; see current status in FACTS_TREE.md). Grounded in a census of
                 the live corpus: node → facet → claim → occurrence, a typed node id space
                 (person/entity/topic) resolved in tiers, and a facet level that keeps the
                 corpus's dominant bucket (80% of chat facts are two speakers' `stated`
                 positions) from ever being read back as knowledge.
PROMPT_REWRITE.md  Implementation brief (2026-09-18, designed, nothing built) for the autonomous
                 standing-prompt rewrite: the prompt-mutation pass as locator (extended to
                 `keep` exchanges with high baseline-normalized CoT tension), deltas clustered
                 into patterns whose tension-weighted recurrence is the budget, a `rewrite_prompt`
                 deliberation action (five samples → consensus → one final draft → blind choice
                 incl. the incumbent) landing in the experiment tier, 24 h between attempts,
                 nothing spent on a decline. Curiosity tokens stay wander-only.
SCRATCHPAD.md    Raw brainstorming sandbox for future design cycles
```


## Architecture overview

The project is split into a **client** (pure UI) and a **server** (all model/data concerns).
Communication is over WebSocket using a JSON message protocol.

### Client module responsibilities

- **`client/main.py`** — Entry point. Parses `--server` flag, launches PyQt6 app.
- **`client/ui/main_window.py`** — `MainWindow`: top-level window, menu, font controls, status bar (polls every 2 s for VRAM, model state, context token usage).
- **`client/ui/chat_widget.py`** — `ChatWidget`: all chat UI. `ChatWorker(QThread)` streams generation requests to the server. While a reply is active, a Stop button sends a discard-cancel, removes the aborted partial turn, and restores the exact prompt so the operator can adjust Temperature and retry; the server rolls the turn back rather than logging it. After a reply *lands* (e.g. a collapsed/degenerate one), a **Retry** button is the completed-turn sibling of Stop: it asks the server to roll the latest completed exchange off the active transcript + conversation (`retry_last_exchange` → `session_ops.handle_retry_last_exchange`, refused on a reflection run / already-reflected session / synthetic Ava opener), excises that turn from the chat log, and restores its prompt so the operator can adjust Temperature and send again — so the discarded reply never reaches reflection or training. The active session is RAG-fenced, so the removed reply was never indexed. Beside the chat list, **Re-reflect chat** (`ResetReflectionWorker` → `reset_session_reflection`) sends the selected chat(s) back to the reflection backlog by deleting their `.state.json` sidecar — the one act that drops the reflect-once freeze along with the verdicts, trainable targets, consolidation summary and anchors derived from that chat, so the next Sleep run re-reads the (untouched) transcript under the current persona; it needs a connection but **not** a loaded model, and confirms first because human-`locked` Training-review repairs live in that same file. After a successful reply, **Meta…** lets the user save/edit one notification-only reflection comment on that latest reply; the window closes when a newer reply lands, while the stored note stays on the exchange for revision/persona reflection and later revisits. A **Debug** checkbox renders, above each reply, the full prompt that turn conditions on, **colour-coded by segment** (`prompt_debug` → `_on_prompt_debug`): blue = the system framing (base prompt, identity line, temporal anchor, persona + user portraits, surfaced questions), green = the injected RAG block, red = the user turn, muted grey = assistant history. Those are the three things an operator is separating when a turn goes wrong — most immediately whether the RAG block is carrying near-duplicate lines — and the former flat one-colour dump (which this replaces) made the block indistinguishable from the prompt around it. It renders into the chat log rather than a side panel, so scrolling back through a session shows what each turn was actually conditioned on. The facts fetch runs **before** the RAG query rather than after it (2026-08-13): its picks are an INPUT to retrieval, not just a block beside it — the conversations behind the picked facts are nominated to the past-chat channel (`rag_engine._query_nominated`). Above every reply — **not** behind that checkbox — sits the **facts block** (`facts_block` → `_on_facts_block`): what the facts-tree fetch pass picked for this message, in the Debug view's `rag` green so an operator with both views on reads it as the same thing in both. It is separate from the prompt dump because it answers a different question — the dump shows everything the turn conditioned on, this shows the one block that was *chosen for* the message — and because a channel whose only surface is opt-in is a channel whose silence nobody notices (it was dead for a day for exactly that reason). A fetch that ran and found nothing still prints its counts; a fetch that BROKE prints red. `ModelOpWorker(QThread)` handles blocking load/unload off the main thread.
- **`client/ui/chat_review_widget.py`** — `ChatReviewWidget`: the **Chat review tab** — one past transcript rendered as the *model* saw it, where the Chat tab's preview renders it as a *reader* does. Loosely mimics the Chat tab's shape (chat list + substring filter on the left, conversation on the right) but chats nowhere. Per exchange it shows the exact assembled `system_content` (base prompt + identity/temporal anchors + the injected RAG block + any first-turn surfaced questions — logged verbatim per turn precisely because it cannot be reconstructed later; a turn repeating the previous message byte-for-byte collapses to a back-reference), the raw retrieved `rag_context`, `assistant_cot` next to the answer, and the per-turn bookkeeping: `generation_params`, `input_tokens`, `think_open_prob`, the `tension` per-segment stats (peak/median entropy, median/p10 margin, contested fraction) with the contested-token trace and its road-not-taken `alt_token`, `reflection_feedback`, the `corrupt_cot`/`corrupt_response` flags, and the schema-v5 `rewrite_history` (prior CoT/reply + the dropped-tension note). The header carries the session-level facts (model, adapter, schema version, `continued_from`/`initiated_by`/`interlocutor`, `initiated_ask`, notes) plus the base `system_prompt`. An Ava-initiated exchange 0 is labelled as the **synthetic impulse** (no user turn, masked from training) instead of being shown as something the user said. It adds no protocol message — it drives the existing read RPCs (`list_sessions` / `get_session`) off the GUI thread by reusing the Chat tab's `SessionsWorker`/`PreviewSessionWorker`, needs a connection but **not** a loaded model, and never mutates server state. **Three-channel colouring (2026-09-22):** the CoT and the reply are painted in the Chat tab's channels (red = tension, green = relief, blue = pain) when `get_session` could decode the exchange's stored `tension` series — `generation.stored_tension_spans` resolves a tokenizer for the block's `model_id` (the loaded one when it matches, else `AutoTokenizer` by id, cached, offline first) and attaches render-only `tension_spans` to the exchange; it runs in the executor, and a model whose tokenizer is not on the box, or a pre-tension exchange, simply renders plain. The painting itself (`_insert_colored_spans`, `_append_channel_means`, `paint_black_pane`) is called on the Chat tab's instance so its Gain knob applies to both views. Six section toggles (System message / RAG block / CoT / Tension / Params+meta / Channels) re-render the *cached* session dict, so collapsing the bulky system message costs no refetch. Scope: the **transcript** (`chats/<ts>.json`); the reflection sidecar (verdicts + trainable targets) remains the Training review tab's surface. Refreshed when the tab is opened (`main_window._on_tab_changed`) and via a Refresh button.
- **`client/ui/sleep_widget.py`** — `SleepWidget`: reflection UI. It **triggers and monitors** the reflection run; the run itself executes server-side (see `reflection_runner.py`). Split-panel layout: a past sessions list on the left (extended selection to reflect on specific chats) and a text event log on the right, with granular stage checkboxes (Reflection, Merge RAG, Commit Training, Train (LoRA)) and a workspace setting (Continue Staging) in the footer. A **Train (LoRA)** footer checkbox (**on** by default) chooses how the run mints its Ava version: checked appends a `train` stage that, after committing, hands LoRA production off to the watchdog's generic job runner (`POST /job/train`) — the inference server stops to free the GPU, runs the offline `train_cycle`, then relaunches with the new adapter (expect a brief restart). **Unchecked = training-lite:** the run instead sends a `persona` stage — reflection still runs in full and still COMMITS (memory, facts, sidecar targets, anchors, user portraits all land in live memory), the inference server is never stopped, the adapter stays as it is, and the run produces + activates `data/persona/<run_id>/` so her refreshed self-portrait actually reaches a live chat turn (chat reads the digest through the active-persona pointer, not the live dir a run writes to — see the protocol table's `start_reflection_run` entry). Skip-validation greys out with it off, gating an adapter promotion that no longer happens. The **Skip validation** control is retained, but `training.validation_switch.VALIDATION_ENABLED=False` force-skips the behavioral probe and baselines regardless of it. This is deliberate; technical row checks remain active. A **Regen persona** footer checkbox (**off by default**) forwards `overrides.force_persona_digest`, forcing the end-of-run persona-digest pass to regenerate even when the persona evidence fingerprint is unchanged (the digest is otherwise a no-op on an unchanged run) — useful after a prompt or token-budget change. An **Apply judge** footer checkbox (**on by default** — the kill-switch for the criterion flip) forwards `overrides.apply_branch_judge`: the clean-base persona judge sets the trainable target (overrides the blind chooser) when its pick differs, but only when the digest clears the weighted-recurrence maturity gate (so it's dormant on a thin, stale, or contested corpus — and on any digest written before 2026-08-23, which carries no weighted recurrence). Uncheck to fall back to logged-only. Judge overrides do not re-enable validation while the master switch is off; the hand-off reports the skipped probe. A **Revisit old chat** button (`_begin_revisit_run`) starts a `revisit` run with no session selection — the server picks a random chat ≥ `revisit.min_age_days` (default 7) **that was not itself revisited within `revisit.min_revisit_days` (default 7)** — the anti-fixation gate, folded from the append-only `data/hot/revisit/revisited.jsonl` log so the random pick rotates instead of fixating (mirrors synthesis's `min_resynth_days`; the pick is recorded regardless of run outcome) — and re-reflects it under the current persona to re-derive its target, suppressing persona formation + prompt modification and skipping ingestion + the training hand-off, reusing the same event-log/stats/poll path (the run is Reflection→Merge RAG→Commit Training only). A **Dedup facts** button (`_on_fact_dedup`, 2026-08-04) manually runs the clean-base semantic `[fact]` merge — the same pass a normal run now performs at the end of its clean-base window (`reflection_runner._run_clean_base_fact_dedup`). It **always previews first** and asks before applying: the run's version writes to staging (discarded with a discarded run) while this one writes straight to live memory, and append-only reversibility is not a reason to skip the confirmation. Reuses the Debug tab's `DedupFactsWorker` + `dedup_facts` RPC rather than restating them; `_any_job_running()` collects the busy-worker OR-chain each handler previously assembled inline. The grouping is dozens of sequential clean-base calls over a real store, so it **streams**: `_on_fact_dedup_progress` renders the subject blocking then one line per block, and is the SAME renderer the reflection run's `fact_dedup_progress` events go through (identical payload, one definition — the run stages the merge, the button writes it live). It sends `start_reflection_run` with the selected sessions + overrides (prompts, sampling, stages), then streams progress by polling `reflection_run_events` / `reflection_run_status`, and can issue `stop_reflection_run`. It no longer runs the consolidation/revision/branch loops itself, builds judge context, or chunks sessions — all of that moved to the server. Between the status label and the event log it renders a compact **live stats panel** (`_render_stats_panel`) from each status poll's `stats` block — elapsed · rough ETA · global exchange x/y · peak VRAM · discards · tok/s — the run's heartbeat during the long silent branch/judge phases; and at run end it appends a **RUN STATISTICS** section (`_render_detailed_stats`) from the summary's `detailed_report` (outcomes/chosen breakdown/categorized discards/per-phase timing + the VRAM "fits in 24 GB?" readout). Both are driven by `reflection_stats.RunStats` server-side. **When the `train` stage is selected**, the run completes over the socket and then the watchdog stops this server to train — so the reflection event stream is dead for the training window. The widget detects the train hand-off and switches a `TrainPollWorker` to poll the watchdog's `GET /job/progress` over HTTP, rendering live training stages + validation-tier replies into the same event log, until training finishes (then prompts the operator to reconnect from the Chat tab, mirroring the Restart-server UX). The hand-off is detected **two ways** (mutually guarded against double-start), because the "completed" status rides the same socket the watchdog is about to kill and is easily missed in the sub-second window before the server dies: the normal path catches `reflection_run_status == "completed"`; the robust fallback fires when `ReflectionPollWorker` finds the inference socket persistently down (≥2 failed reconnects) **and** confirms via the watchdog's `GET /job/status` that a job is `running` (the watchdog flips that flag synchronously on accepting `POST /job/train`, before it stops inference — so it is a race-free signal, and a transient blip that reconnects never trips it). Without this fallback the poller would spin on reconnect until inference came back, showing no training progress. Stop during training only detaches the poll — the watchdog has no mid-cycle halt.
- **`client/ui/prompt_widget.py`** — `PromptWidget`: the **Prompt tab** — the one surface for Ava's live standing prompt, holding an editable copy of it above the three ways to change it. It exists because the two prompt controls used to sit in the Sleep tab with no view of the text they acted on: an operator could ask Ava to rewrite her prompt and revert it, but never *read* what was live, let alone edit it. The editbox opens on the **live** prompt — the experimental one when an experiment is active, else the base `chat_prompt.txt` — fetched per refresh (`prompt_experiment_status`, which now returns `prompt`/`base_prompt` text, not just a char count), never remembered client-side, so a restart or an experiment activated elsewhere shows honestly. Three buttons, all landing in the SAME temporary tier (`data/hot/prompt/experiment.json`) and all undone by the same revert, so `chat_prompt.txt` can never be written from this tab: **Update prompt** (`SetPromptWorker` → `set_prompt`) applies the operator's own edited text — the hand-written sibling of a generated experiment, deliberately allowed to *replace* an active one (editing and re-applying is the point) while carrying the original `base_prompt` forward so a later revert still restores the pre-experiment prompt rather than the previous experiment; **Prompt experiment** (`PromptExperimentWorker`, moved here verbatim from `sleep_widget`) hands Ava the current prompt to rewrite freely, streaming her process into the log panel below and loading her result into the editbox on success (so it is immediately hand-editable); **Revert prompt** (`RevertPromptWorker`) drops the experiment and reloads the base. Every RPC runs off the GUI thread. Unsaved edits are never silently lost: a background refresh (tab opened, socket connected) leaves a dirty box alone, and an explicit Refresh — or an experiment, whose result would overwrite it — confirms first. Needs a connection; only the experiment needs a loaded model. The Sleep tab keeps no prompt state at all now. **A third pane (2026-09-19) is the autonomous rewrite's window** (`RewriteStatusWorker` → `prompt_rewrite_status`, refreshed with the tab and independent of unsaved edits, since it never touches the box): the **currency gate** condition by condition — offered or not and the FIRST failing reason (`disabled` / `no_model` / `no_mature_pattern` / `gap` with the hours left / `no_new_deltas` / `continue` for an interrupted event), the settings in force, the pattern budget as the last fold left it (patterns with scope, tension-weighted recurrence, chats, mature or forming; how many are still unspent; unspent deltas; deltas since the last attempt) and the exact line the executive would be shown — and the **attempt log**, newest first: timestamp, event id, outcome (CHANGED / STAYED / DECLINED), the chosen candidate, her `WHY`, the patterns weighed and keys consumed, and for the newest attempt every candidate's full text with the chosen one marked. The gate is walked past the model check server-side (`offer(check_model=False)`), so the chain is readable with nothing loaded.
- **`client/ui/activity_widget.py`** — `ActivityWidget`: the **Activity tab** — one always-live, read-only scrolling view of the server's unified activity journal (`core/activity_log.py`), so what Ava is doing on the box (wander/outreach/synthesis/check-in/background reflection + a reflection mirror at full Sleep-tab detail for EVERY run, operator or background) is visible regardless of whether the operator triggered anything. An `ActivityPollWorker(QThread)` polls `activity_events` every ~2 s from the moment the client connects (independent of any run, started/stopped by the chat widget's `connected`/`disconnected` signals — same lifecycle contract as the Sleep tab), tracking ONE server cursor so a disconnect/reconnect resumes cleanly with no gap. It merges TWO sources into one log: the WebSocket stream while inference is up, and — the one honest seam — the watchdog's `GET /job/progress` over HTTP while the socket is DOWN for the Sleep `train` hand-off (which stops inference), rendering training into the same timeline (mirroring `sleep_widget.TrainPollWorker`). A **Hide routine skips** checkbox filters the hourly "nothing to do" lines, and a **Hide reflection detail** checkbox collapses each reflection event to its headline (the indented pass output — distilled items / verdicts / anchors — is shown by default, since watching it is the point of the tab; the filter keys on the server's 4-space body indentation, so it needs no event-shape knowledge); the live "current activity" is surfaced both as a header chip and, via `current_activity_text()`, in the main-window status bar (`main_window._update_status_bar`). Read-only — it never mutates server state. **Since 2026-08-11 it is the box's reporting view, not just a lifecycle ticker:** each entry may carry a `level` (`event`/`body`/`stream`/`raw`) and a `text` body rendered indented under the headline — a pass's verbatim CoT + output when it finishes, a live heartbeat's rolling tail while it runs, and raw stdout (including unsloth's, during a training cycle). The level drives the gutter glyph (`✎` body, `⋯` heartbeat, `│` raw) because those are three different things to be scanning for. **Hide pass output** (the renamed *Hide reflection detail*) folds every indented body back to headlines, and **Hide raw output** drops the stdout lane; both key on the same 4-space indent rule, so they need no event-shape knowledge. A `gap` on the batch — the cursor fell past what the server can still serve — is rendered as an explicit line rather than silently skipped.
- **`client/ui/worklog_widget.py`** — `WorklogWidget`: the **Worklog tab** — a read-only live preview of Ava's **first-person episodic worklog** (`core/worklog.py`), the durable record of what she *did* in her own voice (distinct from the machine-phrased Activity journal). A `WorklogPollWorker(QThread)` polls `get_worklog` every ~2 s with ONE id cursor from the moment the client connects (independent of any run, started/stopped by the chat widget's `connected`/`disconnected` signals — same lifecycle as Activity/Sleep). Because the worklog is small (one entry per episode, not per token), it keeps every entry in memory and rerenders the whole view each poll: an **open threads** section (the `opens`/`closes` fold) above the chronological episode log, each entry showing its kind glyph, first-person `summary`, any open/closed loop, and `refs`. It exists to *watch entries being produced during a real run*. It also hosts a **Deliberate** button (`DeliberateWorker` → `deliberate_now`) — a **dry run** of the deliberation pass (the worklog read side): Ava reads her recent worklog + open threads and decides what she would do next (wander / synthesize / reach out / revisit / nothing), streaming her decision CoT into a bottom panel and reporting the action she WOULD take. **Nothing is executed** — dispatch is a later task. Read-only — it never mutates server state.
- **`client/ui/modules_widget.py`** — `ModulesWidget`: the **Modules tab** — the workbench for ONE pass (`server/inference/core/modules.py`). **The input list follows the module (2026-08-10):** a module declares the input KIND it reads (`ModuleSpec.source`) and the tab asks the server what that kind offers (`list_module_inputs` → `ModuleInputsWorker`) rather than knowing per module — so `til_facts` lists fetched articles + news digests where the chat modules list transcripts, and a fourth lane needs no change in this file. The `chat` lane is the deliberate exception: it keeps using the Chat tab's own `SessionsWorker` + `ChatReviewWidget._label_for`, so the chat list here cannot drift from the one beside it. Both are normalized to `{id, label, sub}` so ONE renderer draws every lane. The chosen input is remembered **per lane** (a chat filename is not something the til module could run on), and the result header names the unit the server reports (`unit_label` — exchanges for a transcript, characters for an article) instead of assuming exchanges. Every generation on the box is the same shape (something is injected, something is read, something is produced) but each pass hardcodes all three inside the run it belongs to, so a pass can be neither inspected nor tried on its own; this tab makes one runnable in isolation. Layout: the **chat list** on the left (the module's INPUT — the same list the Chat / Chat-review tabs show, reusing `SessionsWorker` and `ChatReviewWidget._label_for` rather than a second copy that could drift), and on the right a module picker, the **injected-context** line, and a vertical splitter with the **prompt** box over the **result** box. The prompt box opens on the module's on-disk prompt (`ModulesListWorker` → `list_modules`) and is freely editable — an edit applies to the next **Simulate** only and NEVER writes the file; a background refresh leaves a dirty box alone (same discipline as the Prompt tab) and *Reset to file* confirms before discarding edits, while switching module deliberately replaces the text, which belongs to the module that was selected. **Simulate** (`SimulateWorker` → `run_module`) runs the module against the selected chat on the GPU and streams the generation into the result box (detached-cursor append, so an operator scrolled up is not yanked to the tail); on completion the stream is replaced by the parsed report — the produced records, token accounting, and a **WARNING line distinguishing a pass cut inside its `<think>` (no answer region, so an empty result means the pass FAILED) from one that simply found nothing**. It **writes nothing**: the server returns the value rather than committing it, so a facts simulation never touches `<stem>.facts.json`. (That detachment now has its first real consumer — `background_reflection`'s sidecar backfill calls `run_module_blocking` and supplies its OWN sink, so a pass runs in one place and two callers decide independently whether the value is persisted.) Needs a connection AND a loaded model. **`ModuleSpec.source` (2026-08-10)** names the input KIND a pass reads — which list it can run against and how one is loaded — because the third module arrived reading something else: `til_facts` takes a fetched article or news digest out of `data/til/snippets/<kind>/`, which has no exchanges and is measured in characters, while everything above the loader (prompt override, block loop, streaming, refusals, the write-nothing contract) is identical. So *what a pass reads* became data like the rest of it (`_INPUT_SOURCES`: load-one + list-all per source), and the client asks the server what a module can run on (`list_module_inputs`) instead of knowing per module — a fourth lane is an entry in that dict plus `source=` on the spec. **Scope so far:** four modules (`chat_facts`, `chat_summary`, `til_facts`, `fact_fetch`), nothing injected — the injected-context line says so explicitly, and a selection would be refused server-side rather than silently ignored. `fact_fetch` (2026-08-11) is the first that mirrors no existing pass — a proposed **stage 1 of a two-stage chat turn** (pick which facts-tree nodes to look up before a message is answered; the blob is rendered from the tree by `graph.blob`), built here BECAUSE this tab writes nothing, so the channel FACTS_TREE.md §10 defers can be argued about with real output in front of it. It needed **no change in this file**: it reuses the `chat` lane, and its report reaches the operator through the same module-agnostic `lines`/`counts` path — which is the property the registry was built for. Result rendering is module-agnostic (the server sends display `lines` produced by the module's own `finish`), so a new module needs no client change; a chunked module reports `part i of n` in the status line and marks the seams in the streamed text, since several generations joined into one value otherwise read as a single runaway.
- **`client/ui/gossip_widget.py`** — `GossipWidget(EncounterWidget)`: the **Gossip tab** — Ava talks to *another Ava instance* (`GOSSIP.md`). Gossip drives the same server-side encounter loop as Encounter (`start_encounter`), so this subclasses `EncounterWidget` and reuses all of its poll/render/start-stop machinery, overriding only `_build_ui` (gossip defaults: peer name, peer sidecar URL `:8767/v1/chat/completions`, `model=ava` cosmetic label, gossip intro) and `_on_start`. Its reason for existing beyond UX is training safety: the Encounter *default* framing calls the peer a "non-subjective helpful assistant," and because the **driver logs and reflects on** the transcript (the framing becomes exchange 0's `user_prompt` and rides every `system_content`), that lie would poison reflection/training. So the tab pre-fills the **peer-aware** framing (`_GOSSIP_FRAMING`, mirroring `prompts/gossip_prompt.txt`) and `_on_start` restores it if blanked, so a gossip run can never fall back to the poison default. The peer box must have `gossip.enabled` + its model loaded. Tested end-to-end between the two boxes (the main box ↔ a second box — standing up the second box was done for this).
- **`client/ui/debug_widget.py`** — `DebugWidget`: read-only inspection tab. Fetches Ava's current live reflection RAG artifacts via `get_rag_artifacts` (a `RagArtifactsWorker` does the blocking RPC off-thread) and dumps them into a textbox grouped by type (`[persona]` / `[fact]` / `[ask]`, each annotated with `ask_kind`/`trigger`/`from_weights`/surface count/source session). Above the artifact list it renders Ava's current **persona self-portrait** and, directly beneath it, the **outside view** (`rag_artifacts.outside_view` ← `core/self_portrait.py`) — what her transcripts show about her to a reader who never saw her `<think>`. The two sit together because they are independent readings of the same conversations and the thing worth looking at is where they disagree; and this tab is the outside view's PRIMARY surface, since nothing injects that portrait into a prompt by design. Auto-refreshes when the tab is opened and via a Refresh button; the read path never mutates server state. The header also carries a **Wipe…** button — a destructive disaster-recovery flow that, through a guided sequence of dialogs (confirm regenerable delete → ask whether to also delete chats + the review archive → choose a base model → final confirm), hands off to the watchdog's generic job runner (`POST /job/wipe`, a synchronous job invoking `wipe_state.py`; via `BackendClient.wipe_state`; a `WipeWorker` runs the blocking RPC off-thread). The watchdog stops the inference server, `wipe_state.py` deletes all regenerable state (adapters, RAG, reflection memory/ledger/runs/staging — chat sidecars included, **except human-validated `locked` exchange records, which are hand-authored and preserved** when chats are kept), optionally also the raw transcripts + reflection review archive, repoints the base model (clears `adapter_id`, optional new `model_id`), and the watchdog relaunches — the operator reconnects from the Chat tab once the model reloads (mirroring the Restart UX). Unlike training it is synchronous (no progress poll): file deletion is fast, and the slow model load happens in the relaunched subprocess.
- **`client/ui/memory_editor.py`** — `MemoryEditorWidget`: the shared editor behind the **Persona** and **Facts** tabs. Both curate one *kind* of the same folded `rag_memory.jsonl` view, so the machinery is one widget: fetch the live rows of a kind, extended-select and remove them locally (portable Delete/Backspace plus a mouse Delete button), then Upload the retained set explicitly. Upload sends the fetched baseline keys + retained keys over WebSocket, and the server rejects a stale baseline (a reflection landed since the fetch) rather than applying a stale editor view. A subclass supplies only what differs: the artifact `KIND`, the nouns the status line uses, how a row renders (`format_row`), the confirmation body (`eviction_warning`), and the upload RPC (`upload_rpc`, called off the GUI thread).
- **`client/ui/persona_widget.py`** — `PersonaWidget(MemoryEditorWidget)`: operator cleanup surface for the connected server's live `[persona]` evidence. Upload appends persona `evict` tombstones to live RAG and the consolidation ledger, then refreshes RAG. This prepares the next reflection/digest/training fold only: it never reads or mutates runnable snapshots, reflection archives, adapter weights, or the current digest artifact. The one deliberate exception is the **Regen persona…** button (2026-08-23, `RegenPersonaWorker` → `regen_persona`): the natural next act after curating the evidence — re-derive the digest from the live `[persona]` set exactly as a Sleep run does (plan forced; cluster on the CLEAN base; synthesize + WRITE on the ADAPTER through `reflection_digest.synthesize_digest`, the run's own seam) and then mint + **activate** the persona version via `snapshot_state.produce_persona(run_id, activate=True)` — the same call the Sleep run's `persona` stage makes, so the refreshed self-portrait reaches the very next chat turn (chat reads it through the active-persona pointer). Adapter unchanged; the previous persona snapshot stays on disk as rollback. Confirm-first; refuses to run over unsaved local removals (upload first, so the digest folds the evidence the operator means); streams the clustering progress + portrait tokens into a non-modal `RegenPersonaDialog` (closing it does not stop the server-side pass, and says so).
- **`client/ui/facts_widget.py`** — `FactsWidget(MemoryEditorWidget)`: the `[fact]` counterpart of the Persona tab, same store and same discipline (local delete → explicit Upload → baseline fence → `evict` tombstones to live RAG + ledger → RAG refresh; snapshots/archives/weights/digest untouched). It differs only in what an operator must see before evicting a row: the fact's **attribution** — `— about X`, or `— about X, per Y` for hearsay, mirroring `rag_engine._attribution_label` so the row reads as recall does — and its **trigger**, which is what actually decides when the fact comes back (clipped at 80 chars). Evicting a fact drops it from chat recall at once, from the "I know that …" line injected into its host exchange's CoT at the next build (the ledger tombstone drops the anchor from `live_anchors`), and from the next standing portrait of the person it is about (folded from live memory). A hearsay fact never raised a ledger anchor, so its tombstone there is inert.
- **`client/ui/migrate_widget.py`** — `MigrateWidget`: clones a **still-running Ava** onto this box so one host can run "production" reflection cycles while this one debugs. On show/Refresh it inspects the connected source via the watchdog `GET /status` (hostname/data_dir) + the chat status (model_id/adapter_id/base_quant) and a **same-box guard** (refuses a clone onto its own checkout). A precision selector (16 / 8 / 4-bit) picks the base-load quant. **Migrate…** hands off to a `MigrateWorker` that streams the inference sidecar's `GET /export` clone bundle (weights-inclusive, uncompressed tar; port 8767) straight through a streaming tar reader into a staging dir, atomically swaps the managed roots (`inference/data` = reflection working state; `data` = the ordered `server/data/` home — chat corpus + til/wander + persona lineage incl. the `current.json` pointer; `reflections` / `models`) + `server_config.json` into the local `server/` (same mirror discipline as *Fetch artifacts*), then **rewrites the migrated config** — repoints the absolute `adapter_id` at the local `models/` copy (basename preserved by copying the whole lineage) and sets `base_quant` from the selector, keeping `model_id`/`context_length`/`consolidation` verbatim (tokenizer + LoRA compat). Each cloned persona's own config carries a *relative* `adapter_id` self-contained under its persona dir, so it needs no rewrite. It only *prepares* the box; the operator then starts the local watchdog, connects to `ws://localhost:8765`, and loads the model (the migrated config drives model_id + adapter + precision). The tab also carries a **Merge chats…** button (`ChatSyncWorker`): a two-way sync of chat transcripts with the connected server — reads the local chat stems **+ per-stem fingerprints** (hot + archive) off disk, fetches the server's via the inference sidecar's `GET /chats/manifest`, **pulls** stems the server has but this checkout lacks (`GET /chats/export` → local `hot/chats`) and **pushes** stems this checkout has but the server lacks (`POST /chats/import` → server `hot/chats`). A chat present on **both** sides that GREW on one box (e.g. a reply appended to an Ava-initiated session after an earlier merge) **updates** the shorter copy: the manifest carries a **fingerprint** per stem (the transcript's ordered per-exchange token list — `exchange_id`, else a content hash), and when one side's fingerprint is a strict prefix of the other's, the longer side is a pure append and wins (the update overwrites the shorter copy and drops its now-stale `.state.json` sidecar). A chat appended to on both boxes **independently** is a **divergence** that can't be merged — left untouched on both sides and reported as a conflict; a frozen (archive-only) chat is never updated. The server re-verifies the prefix relation on import (authoritative + race-safe), so a push only updates a genuinely-shorter server copy. Only raw transcripts move (not weights/RAG), so chats new to **or updated on** a box land in its `hot/chats` and must be **re-reflected** there (a Sleep pass). Same-box guard disables it. Finally a **Fetch snapshot…** button (`FetchSnapshotWorker`): pulls the source's **runnable snapshot** — the *current Ava personality*, i.e. the active LoRA adapter **plus its RAG sources** (reflection memory + consolidation, the chat corpus, the wander corpus, the persona digest), prompts, and config — and **overwrites this checkout** so the local server boots as current Ava. It reads `GET /snapshot/manifest`, streams `GET /snapshot/export` (which delegates to `snapshot_state.stream_snapshot`, the same scope as the CLI export) into staging, then maps the portable **bundle** layout into the local **checkout** layout (the ordered `server/data/` homes) as it atomically swaps each member in (`data/`→`inference/data`, `chats/`→`data/chats`, `til/`→`data/til`, `prompts/`→`inference/prompts`, `models/<adapter>`→`models/<adapter>`, `training/<build_id>`→`models/snapshots/<build_id>`, `server_config.json`→`server/server_config.json` with `adapter_id` rewritten absolute (and a pre-move legacy `inference/server_config.json` deleted, so the box has ONE config); the bundle's top-level `digest.json` is provenance-only, not restored). Snapshot-scoped: unlike Migrate it skips the `reflections/` archive and old adapter lineage (lighter); other local adapters are left untouched. The whole config is the source's, so adapter+base+config are consistent by construction (no compat guard). Same-box guard disables it; the operator starts/reloads the local server from the Chat tab to boot as current Ava. Beside it a **Fetch adapter…** button (`FetchAdapterWorker`) is the fourth transfer path and the first deliberately **hybrid** one: it pulls ONLY the source's active LoRA (`GET /adapter/manifest` + `GET /adapter/export` → `snapshot_state.plan_adapter_manifest`/`stream_adapter`, an adapter-only scope defined beside the snapshot scope it narrows; the tar carries `models/<adapter>/` — the same arcname `stream_snapshot` uses, so the member→destination mapping is shared — plus `MANIFEST.json`) and repoints **`adapter_id` only**, leaving this box's chats, distilled memory, ledger, prompts, digest and persona pointer exactly as they are, with the previous adapter left in `models/` as the rollback path. The case it serves: reflect and accumulate the corpus here, train on the box with the GPU headroom, run those weights against this box's memory. **The base-compat guard is the substance, not a nicety** — Migrate and Fetch-snapshot both replace local state wholesale, so adapter/base/config arrive internally consistent, while this one grafts foreign weights onto local state and **nothing downstream re-checks the base**: at 4-bit `inference_backend.load` resolves it from the adapter's own `adapter_config.json` (a mismatch quietly loads a base the local config never named), and at 8/16-bit `_stage_adapter_with_base` overwrites that field with the local `model_id` (a mismatch surfaces only as an opaque peft/unsloth error). So the worker compares the remote manifest's `model_id` against `server_config.json` **on disk** (not the connected server's status — the config is what the guard protects) and **refuses before a byte moves**; `model_id` is never rewritten by the repoint, which would turn a refused mismatch into an accepted one on the next fetch. Same-box guard disables it; the operator reloads the model from the Chat tab to run the fetched weights.
- **`client/ui/training_review_widget.py`** — `TrainingReviewWidget`: "Training review" tab for inspecting — and now **repairing** — the exact rows fed to Unsloth. Its database is the **latest build snapshot's** training render — the newest `server/models/snapshots/<build_id>/sft_render.jsonl` by mtime — projected to its **final** exchange (the only turn training targets under final-turn label masking): the last `user` content (query) and the last `assistant` content split around `<think>…</think>` into CoT + answer. **That projection is built server-side** (`inference/core/training_review.py`) and **fetched over the inference HTTP sidecar** (`GET /training/review`), which is what lets the tab run from a **remote UI box**: finding the render, splitting the turns, collapsing duplicate rows and patching the live sidecars' repaired targets all read files that live beside the model, and the result is a fraction of the render's size (2.6 MB gzipped against a 34 MB render on the 2026-07-24 build) with no adapter weights in it — so reviewing a build no longer needs a Migrate → *Fetch snapshot* pull. `TrainingLoadWorker(QThread)` does the fetch off the GUI thread and scores each entry (persona detection stays client-side, so a repaired row rescores without a round trip); with **no connection** it falls back to this checkout's own `server/` tree by loading that same builder module by path — one definition, so local and remote can't drift. Left panel: a `QListWidget` of every entry in **file order == training order**, each keyed by the first 20 chars of the reply (answer). **Duplicate rows are collapsed:** a cap-age exchange renders *twice* (the masked row + its `unmask_user` user-contamination copy — same `messages`, different `lr_multiplier`; see `build_dataset._contamination_rows`), which is a training-schedule fact rather than a second exchange to review, so rows sharing a `(source_session, exchange_index)` — or, for wander / pre-provenance rows that lack it, identical rendered text (`_dedup_key`) — fold into ONE entry tagged `×N`, with the status line naming the collapse (`N entries (from M rows — duplicate copies collapsed)`). The first (masked) row is the one kept; entry numbering is over the deduplicated list. Right panel: three `QPlainTextEdit` boxes (query · CoT · answer) in a vertical splitter, populated on row selection, over a **regenerate bar** and under an **edit bar** — the query box stays read-only, while **CoT and answer are editable** for any row carrying chat provenance (see the hand-edit route below). A **search bar** (edit box + Search button, Enter-to-search) is itself a **filter**: as the phrase is typed the list narrows to the entries whose query/CoT/reply contains it (case-insensitive, live on `textChanged`), and the Search button then steps through those survivors, wrapping around. The match text is cached per entry (`_haystack`, invalidated when a repair rewrites the displayed CoT/reply) so a keystroke doesn't re-concatenate every row's kilobytes. Beside it a **replacement box + Replace button** (`_on_replace`) is the search phrase's write counterpart: it rewrites every occurrence of the phrase in the SELECTED entry's CoT + answer boxes (case-insensitively, mirroring the filter and the highlight; the replacement is inserted literally, so `\1` in the box is text rather than a backreference), and an **empty replacement box deletes the phrase** — the common case, a stray marker or a leaked template line, which needs no separate control. Like *Strip persona opener* it is a **prefill, never a write**: it marks the entry dirty through the boxes' own `textChanged` and leaves the edit bar's Apply as the one thing that reaches the sidecar. Scoped to one entry rather than sweeping the corpus, because each entry's repair is a sidecar write that freezes ❄ an exchange, so a sweep would be dozens of unreviewed ones; the immutable query box is excluded, since replacing there would show a change that could never be applied. Refuses with a status line (rather than silently no-op'ing) on an empty search phrase, no selection, a read-only row, or zero occurrences. Beside it **Replace all…** (`_on_replace_all` + `_bulk_replace_jobs` + `BulkReplaceWorker`) is the corpus-scale form and the one control on that bar that WRITES: it runs the same substitution over **every entry currently shown** (so the filters choose the scope) and applies each result itself through the same per-exchange `apply_regenerated_exchange` a single Apply uses — freezing ❄ each exchange as it goes, no per-entry review. Built for a poison sequence a bad build smeared across the corpus (`"terms of "`), where per-entry review is a formality nobody performs; that is also why it reuses the single-Apply RPC in a loop rather than gaining a bulk server call — a bulk repair and a hand repair must not come to mean different things on disk. Confirm-first (it names the entry + occurrence counts, how many are already frozen, and that undoing means re-reflecting whole chats), the button becomes **Stop** while it runs (each write is independent, so a stop leaves what it reached repaired and the rest untouched), and Refresh / Rewrite history / the single repair buttons stand down meanwhile — progress patches entries by their `_order` stamp, which a reload restamps (`refresh()` itself returns early mid-sweep, since the tab-open auto-refresh isn't a button press). Read-only rows and rows whose reply would come out empty are skipped and counted, never silently dropped; the list is re-filtered once at the end rather than per write, so it can't reshuffle under the operator mid-sweep. **The search phrase is matched exactly as typed** (`_search_phrase`, the one definition behind the filter, the highlight and both Replace buttons — it no longer strips): the sequence this was built for carries a **trailing space**, and stripping it would delete the words while leaving the double space behind, i.e. a corpus-wide off-by-one traded for a whitespace-tolerant search box. A **CoT/reply ratio filter** hides "normal" rows to surface tiny-CoT/huge-reply runaways, a **frozen filter** (All / Only ❄ frozen / Only unfrozen) narrows the list by human-validated state — the repair workflow's progress view (*Only unfrozen* = what is left to repair) — and a **persona-opener filter** (`Persona opener ≥ <score>`) surfaces the rows whose CoT begins by reciting identity instead of engaging the question. All four compose in `_apply_hiding` (a row must pass every active one), **Clear filters** resets them (search box included), and rows are only *hidden*, never removed, so entry numbering and the source-exchange index mapping stay intact. **Persona-opener detection** (module-scope, pure, no ML dependency — the client carries none) scores each entry's leading CoT lines in two tiers: **ledger**, the line matches a `[persona]` statement read from the raw append-only logs (`weights_persona.jsonl` + `consolidation_anchors.jsonl`, evicted ones included — an injection happened while its statement was live), scored by normalized containment or Jaccard ≥ 0.6, i.e. `persona_render._DEDUP_JACCARD`, the *injector's own* "already expressed" test reused to recognize what it wrote; and **shape**, no ledger match but a long first-person opener with <25% word overlap with the query — the recitation habit those rows taught — capped at 0.59 so a shape hit can never outrank a ledger one. `fact_render`'s deliberate `"I know that …"` line is skipped and does not break the leading run. Measured on the 2026-07-26 build: 24 ledger / 537 shape / 442 clean of 1003 chat rows. The score also drives a **Persona score** sort and a `✦0.92` tag in the list, and is recomputed in-place after a repair lands so a fixed row drops out of the filter without a reload. Auto-refreshes when the tab is opened (`main_window._on_tab_changed`) and via a Refresh button; the status line names the origin (`via http://host:8767`) so an operator can see whether what they are reading came from the box their repairs will write to, and a server with no build shows a "run a training cycle first" message. **Live connect-back (frozen entries):** the render is a frozen forensic copy, so the builder also reads each row's **live** `.state.json` sidecar (`_live_chat_sidecar_dirs` → `server/data/chats` then `server/data/archive/chats` — the ordered chat-corpus home per `training/reflections_path.hot_chats_dir`, NOT the legacy `inference/data/hot/chats`; memoised per chat): when the source exchange's live record is **`locked`** (human-validated / regenerated), the tab shows that record's target (the regenerated CoT/reply, split from `target`) in the preview instead of the stale snapshot, and the left-list entry gets a **snowflake ❄ icon** (a drawn `QIcon`, blank placeholder on the rest to keep text aligned; frozen count shown in the status line). So an operator's regenerated repairs survive a Refresh/reopen and are visually distinct. `_disp_cot`/`_disp_answer` (live-preferred) back the preview, search, and ratio filter alike. **Regenerate flow (regenerate → review → apply):** each render row carries `source_session`+`exchange_index` (added to `train_cycle`'s render row), so the regenerate bar — **Corrupt CoT** / **Corrupt reply** checkboxes + a **temperature** field + an **Adapter** dropdown (which adapter answers: the loaded one by default, or ANY adapter from the server's `models/` lineage — the review payload's `adapters` list, each annotated with its build id / snapshot survival / `current` flag — the "repair with the last known GOOD adapter" path for when the loaded adapter is itself what keeps re-answering badly; the swap is **sticky**: a non-loaded choice costs ONE full model reload, narrated into the dialog by `regenerate_status` milestones, and then stays loaded across the repair session — the box keeps running that adapter afterwards until a Chat-tab load or restart) + a **Regenerate** button (disabled for wander rows and pre-provenance snapshots that lack the fields, when disconnected, or with neither box checked) — has the **currently loaded adapter** re-answer the *selected* row's source exchange (`RegenerateWorker(QThread)` → `regenerate_exchange`, GPU-side via the reflection IDEAL seam). The modal `RegenReviewDialog` (read-only boxes) opens **immediately, at the press**, and fills in as the model writes: the worker streams `regenerate_chunk` deltas into it (a CoT-only regen streams to the CoT box only and shows the kept reply beside it from the start), and a **Stop** button halts the model mid-generation and keeps what it produced — a re-answer costs GPU minutes, so waiting for a finished result meant a frozen tab with no way to abandon an obviously bad generation, and a partial thought is still applicable (and hand-editable). **Apply** is armed only once generation ends, so a half-written thought can't be persisted by a stray click; the streamed text is then replaced by the server's authoritative pair, a stopped one labelled as partial. Dismissing the dialog mid-generation stops the GPU work too. **Apply** (`ApplyRegenWorker` → `apply_regenerated_exchange`) writes a repaired trainable target **straight into the chat sidecar** and **locks the exchange (human-validated)** — a **regenerated reply carries its own new CoT** (the pair was generated together), while regenerating **only the CoT** grafts the new `<think>` onto the trusted original reply, so a written target is never a think/answer mismatch. The lock is **per-exchange**: re-reflection AND a Revisit run (which bypasses the session-level reflect-once freeze) skip a locked exchange, so the original poison can't re-contaminate a future build — while the rest of the chat still re-reflects normally (the intended workflow for a dataset with ~15% corrupt exchanges: regenerate each until coherent, lock it, move on). The apply updates the in-memory row so the tab reflects the repair (the note reminds the operator training reads the LIVE sidecar, not this forensic snapshot). **Hand-edit flow (the second repair route):** for a row the adapter keeps re-answering badly — or one needing a one-word fix — the operator can type straight into the CoT / answer boxes; the edit bar's **Apply** (`ApplyRegenWorker` again, `corrupt_cot`+`corrupt_response` both set, so the pair is written together and never the CoT-only graft) persists exactly what is in the boxes as the reviewed target and **locks (❄ freezes) the exchange** identically to a regeneration, while **Cancel** reverts them to the stored content. Client-only: it adds no server code and no protocol message, reusing `apply_regenerated_exchange`. Unsaved edits are tracked per *entry* (`_edit_order` holds the entry's `_order` stamp, not its row) so a re-sort can never strand them on the wrong exchange, and switching entry / pressing Refresh confirms before discarding them; a successful Apply re-baselines the boxes and, when a frozen filter is active, re-applies it so the repaired row drops out under *Only unfrozen* and the selection advances to the next entry awaiting review. **Strip persona opener** is the semi-automatic half of the repair: it drops the detected leading run out of the CoT box — a **prefill, never an auto-apply**, so the operator reviews and hits Apply (which writes + freezes).
