It is armed only when the detected lines form the *contiguous leading run* of the CoT, because the injector **prepended** onto an existing thought, so removing that run restores the pre-injection CoT verbatim; a hit with real reasoning above it is entangled (removing it could strand a back-reference) and the button stays disabled with a tooltip saying so. On the 2026-07-26 build 551 of 560 detected rows are strippable, 9 entangled. (The old flag-only *Corrupt CoT*/*Corrupt reply* toggles + `mark_corrupt`/`MarkCorruptWorker` deferred repair to the next Sleep; this replaces them with an immediate, reviewed, hand-authored target — `mark_corrupt` remains on the server for the automatic path.) **Ban from training (the third verdict):** an edit-bar **Ban from training / Un-ban** toggle (`set_training_ban` → `session_ops.handle_set_training_ban`) for a row that should not train at all — as against repairing it, which assumes a target worth writing. It is armed by the wider `_can_ban` rather than `_has_provenance`, so it is the ONE control alive on a **wander** row: a wander capture has no conversation behind it, so it can be neither regenerated nor hand-edited, and a malformed one is exactly the row most in need of leaving the corpus (this is the case the button was built for). The flag lands on the exchange's sidecar record, or — for a `wander:<ts>` row — on the wander corpus record itself, and is enforced at the single choke point every trainable row passes: `training/dialogue_source.build_dialogue_anchor` refuses a banned record and `core/wander_sft.load_pending` drops a banned capture (which also drops it from the chat-RAG wander channel). `ChatSidecar.write_verdict` **carries** the flag, so a later reflection can't quietly re-derive a target for it, and `reflection_runner` skips banned exchanges in the revision work list; the retrieval-side passes deliberately do not (until deletion, the exchange happened and is recallable). Reversible right up until *Rewrite history*, which deletes the source. The row takes a **🚫** list icon (outranking ❄ on a row carrying both) and the state filter gains *Only 🚫 banned*. **Fresh preview rows (2026-08-22):** when a build (or a training-lite run's preview snapshot — see `training/preview_build.py`) ran with the Sleep tab's *Include fresh chats* checkbox, the render also carries the not-yet-eligible chats as untrained `preview: true` rows (lr 0, trailing the trained corpus); the tab tags each such entry `▷fresh`, counts them in the status line ("fresh preview ▷ (untrained)"), and the state filter gains *Only ▷ fresh* — every repair control works on them identically (a repair locks ❄ the exchange, which re-reflection/revisit/the pending clean-base judge honor), so a bad fresh turn is fixed BEFORE it ages into a build that trains it. The payload also carries the snapshot's `outcome` (from its `build_meta.json`): on a `preview-<ts>` snapshot the status line reads "PREVIEW: nothing trained yet", so a whole-snapshot preview is never mistaken for a trained corpus. **Rewrite history (finalize):** a header **"Rewrite history…"** button (`RewriteHistoryWorker` → `rewrite_history`, confirm dialog first) is the counterpart to per-exchange Apply — it asks the server to bake **every** locked exchange's reviewed target back into its transcript and unfreeze it (see `session_ops.handle_rewrite_history`). Apply leaves the correction as a *sidecar override* over the still-corrupt transcript (training reads the good target, but RAG recall / snapshots / later-exchange context still see the corrupt reply); Rewrite history makes it permanent ground truth, deleting each exchange's now-stale tension block. It edits the original transcripts, so it confirms first and reminds the operator to rebuild + re-fetch a snapshot to see it reflected here (this frozen render doesn't change, and an unfrozen exchange reverts to showing the snapshot's old content until then). The prior CoT/reply are kept in each exchange's `rewrite_history` for manual recovery. In the same pass it **deletes** every banned (🚫) row: a banned exchange is removed from its transcript (`chat_logger.delete_exchange`, schema v6 — the turn preserved under a session-level `deleted_exchanges` record) and a banned wander capture is dropped from the corpus. Deletion **shifts every later exchange down one index**, so the three index-keyed stores move with it in the same operation — the sidecar's verdict + anchor maps (`ChatSidecar.drop_exchange`) and the ledger's fact→host pointers (`session_ops._renumber_fact_hosts`; personas are deliberately left alone, since re-registering one would cast a fresh persona-digest recurrence vote) — deletions run highest-index-first after that chat's rewrites, and the chat's positional background clean-base jobs are purged.
- **`client/core/backend_client.py`** — `BackendClient`: thread-safe WebSocket client. **`connect` opens the TCP socket itself (`_open_socket`, 2026-09-11)** — every address the resolver offers, IPv4 before IPv6 (the server binds `0.0.0.0`) and the last-good address before either, re-resolving and retrying for `CONNECT_RESOLVE_RETRY_S` (12 s) when all refuse — because macOS's mDNS resolver returns EXPIRED cache entries first: on the operator's box `<server>.local` came back as an IPv6 link-local plus `192.0.2.20` (an address the training box no longer has; a second box does) ~0.7 s before the correct `192.0.2.10`, so `socket.create_connection` in that window saw only refusing addresses and the client reported "[Errno 61] Connection refused" for minutes while the server was up and busy. The sidecar/watchdog HTTP helpers (`urllib`) share the exposure and are not yet routed through it. `stream_generate(message)` for normal chat; `get_rag_artifacts()` for the Debug/Persona/Facts tabs' live reflection-memory view; `update_persona(baseline_keys, retained_keys)` / `update_facts(...)` for fenced Persona-/Facts-tab cleanup on the connected server; the reflection-run family (`start_reflection_run`, `reflection_run_status`, `reflection_run_events`, `stop_reflection_run`, `list_reflection_runs`) for driving the server-side Sleep loop; and `train_progress(after_seq)` / `train_status()` which poll the watchdog's generic job runner (`GET /job/progress` / `GET /job/status`; URL derived from the ws host + `mgmt_port`, default 8766) for live LoRA-training progress (and a cheap running/stage status, used to confirm the train hand-off when the inference socket drops) while the inference server is stopped for training. Config/data endpoints go to the inference **HTTP sidecar** instead (`sidecar_port`, default 8767): `set_precision()` (POST `/precision`) and the Migrate/Fetch-artifacts stream workers.

### Server module responsibilities

- **`server/watchdog.py`** — **Thin, never-upgraded process supervisor.** The watchdog is the root of the process tree on the GPU box (it launches/kills `server.py`), and nothing on the box can restart the watchdog itself without SSH — so it must hold ONLY generic, stable logic and almost never change. Two responsibilities: **(1) supervision + code update** and **(2) a generic offline-job runner**. Everything project-specific (data layout, config schema, training params, tar-bundle shapes) was pushed out — into the inference server or repo scripts that upgrade with `git pull`. Exposes HTTP API on port 8766:
  - `GET /status` — `{running, pid, uptime_s, role, hostname, data_dir, job, last_exit}` (box facts + the single job slot's state; no longer reports config fields like `base_quant`). `last_exit` is the last exit the **crash reaper** saw (`{returncode, at, uptime_s, detail, relaunched}`, `null` if none).
  - **Crash reaper (2026-09-11):** a daemon thread polls the child; a child that exits while no stop was requested (`_expected_stop` is set by `_kill`, cleared by `_launch`) is written into server.log with its returncode's meaning — `-9` SIGKILL = the kernel OOM killer or an outside kill, `-11` SIGSEGV / `-6` SIGABRT = a native crash (the inference server enables `faulthandler`, so its thread dump precedes the line) — and **relaunched**, unless it is a crash LOOP (`CRASH_RELAUNCH_MAX` 3 inside `CRASH_RELAUNCH_WINDOW_S` 600 s ⇒ stays down, says so, `POST /restart` to retry) or a deliberate exit code (a model that will not load exits 1 ⇒ stays down, relaunching would repeat it). It exists because three silent deaths in five days (2026-09-07 → 09-11, each right after a RAG-engine build, one inside a background reflection drain) left the server DOWN until the operator pressed Restart — read as "connection refused for a few minutes" — and server.log said nothing about why. **Needs a watchdog restart to take effect** (SSH; the watchdog is the process-tree root).
  - `POST /pull` — stop inference → `git pull --ff-only` → relaunch. The only code-update path (never accepts code over the wire); a pull also brings updated `watchdog_jobs.json` / repo job scripts, which take effect **without a watchdog restart** (the manifest is re-read per request).
  - `POST /restart` — kill + relaunch.
  - `POST /job/<name>` — run the manifest job `<name>` (from git-tracked `watchdog_jobs.json`); body `{args?: [str, ...]}` is **appended verbatim** to the job's `cmd`, so the caller (inference server / client) forms the flags and the watchdog stays semantics-free. Async jobs return immediately (poll `GET /job/status` + `GET /job/progress?after_seq=N`); a `sync` job (wipe) blocks and returns the job's parsed JSON result. `POST /job/stop` is an honest no-op ack (no mid-cycle halt). The two shipped jobs: **train** (`-m training.train_cycle` — LoRA cycle; the Sleep "train" hand-off) and **wipe** (`wipe_state.py` — destructive disaster recovery, sync). Each job runs with inference DOWN (GPU freed / model unloaded), then the watchdog relaunches.
  - `GET /logs?lines=N` — tail server.log. Stays here (not on the sidecar) because it must work while inference is down during a job.
  - The job runner mirrors each job's lifecycle (stop → finish+outcome → relaunch) into `server/server.log` (the inference process can't log while down), appends full output to `server/<name>.log` (e.g. `train.log`/`wipe.log`), and serves the structured progress from the job's journal (e.g. `server/train_progress.jsonl`, written by `training/train_progress.py`). The read-mostly data endpoints (artifacts/export/chats-sync/precision) moved to the inference **HTTP sidecar** (`inference/core/mgmt_http.py`, port 8767) — they don't need inference down, so they upgrade freely.
- **`server/inference/core/mgmt_http.py`** — **Inference-side HTTP sidecar** (port 8767, daemon thread, launched from `server.main()`). Serves the read-mostly, data-layout-coupled management endpoints that used to live on the watchdog but do NOT need inference down — so keeping them in the inference process (which git-pulls freely) lets the watchdog stay thin. Self-locates every managed root from `__file__` (no server.py wiring beyond a `configure(is_busy=…)` predicate that refuses `/precision` + `/chats/import` while a reflection run owns the GPU/config). Endpoints: `GET /status` (`{hostname, data_dir, base_quant}` — the box/config facts the Migrate + Fetch-artifacts "same box?" checks read; base_quant also travels on the WebSocket chat `status`), `GET /artifacts` (gzip post-mortem tar — `inference/data/` scratch-excluded, `reflections/` adapters-excluded, `logs/`; each managed root carries a dir entry even when empty so the client clears stale copies), `GET /export` (uncompressed clone tar — weights KEPT: `inference/data/`, `reflections/`, `models/`, `server_config.json`; no gzip, NIC-bound over the fast inter-box link), `GET /adapter/manifest` + `GET /adapter/export` (the Migrate tab's **Fetch adapter** — the adapter-only scope: `plan_adapter_manifest` reports `model_id`/`base_quant`/adapter name/size so a client can refuse a base mismatch cheaply, and `stream_adapter` emits just `models/<adapter>/` + `MANIFEST.json`. Adapter-only export, narrower than the snapshot bundle — the receiving box keeps its own memory, prompts, config and persona), `GET /snapshot/manifest` + `GET /snapshot/export` (the Migrate tab's **Fetch snapshot** — the runnable-snapshot manifest + an uncompressed tar of the **current Ava personality**: active adapter + its RAG sources (`data/`) + prompts + config + forensic training corpus, NO `reflections/` archive or old lineage; both delegate to `snapshot_state.plan_manifest`/`stream_snapshot` so the sidecar and the CLI export share ONE scope definition), `GET /training/review` (the Training review tab's entry list — gzipped JSON from `core/training_review.py`; read-only, so deliberately NOT gated on the busy predicate: a reflection run is exactly when an operator wants to read what the last build trained), `GET /chats/manifest` (stems + per-stem fingerprints) + `GET /chats/export` + `POST /chats/import` (two-way chat merge: new stems imported, an existing hot stem whose incoming transcript is a pure append onto ours updated, divergent/frozen/equal skipped), `POST /precision` (`{base_quant}` → atomic rewrite of `server_config.json`, takes effect next restart), and `POST /v1/chat/completions` (+ `/chat/completions`) — the **model-gossip serving endpoint** (`GOSSIP.md`): an OpenAI-compatible completion so a *peer* Ava's unchanged Encounter loop can drive this box as if it were vLLM. Enabled per box via `server_config.json` `gossip.enabled` — **default on since 2026-07-30** (false ⇒ 404); the route carries no auth, so turn it off on an untrusted network; busy (reflection/encounter owns the GPU) ⇒ 503. **Serving-side reflection (GOSSIP.md §7 approach 2, default on):** the serving box now logs its own half of the gossip to `hot/chats` WITH its own CoT (`generation._GossipSessionLog` correlates the stateless calls into one growing `ChatLogger` session per conversation) so its own Sleep pass can reflect on it too; `gossip.log_transcripts:false` restores the old stateless behavior. The generate capability is injected (`configure(gossip_generate=…, gossip_enabled=…, gossip_peer_name=…)` — the sidecar never imports `generation`); `generation._make_gossip_generate()` runs the same recipe as `encounter_run._generate_ava` (system prompt + peer framing `prompts/gossip_prompt.txt` + this box's persona-digest introduction via `reflection_digest.render_digest_for_introduction` + temporal/identity anchors + live-chat RAG incl. wander), submitting GPU work to the single executor and blocking on the future. Since it lives in the inference process it's only reachable while inference is up — exactly when these endpoints are meaningful. The gossip route now shares ONE generate body and ONE wire format with the public API below (`generation._make_openai_generate`, `api_http.openai_response`), so the two OpenAI-compatible surfaces can't drift; its callable returns `(answer, finish_reason, reasoning, usage)`. **Peer turns are sanitized before anything reads them** (2026-08-15, gossip mode only): `_make_openai_generate` runs the incoming `user` turns through `generation._strip_peer_reasoning_from_messages` (← `encounter.strip_peer_reasoning`, ONE definition shared with the driver side) as the first thing it does, so the conversation, the RAG query and the logged transcript all read the same clean turn. Here the *driver* is the peer, and a driver cut mid-thought sends its raw thinking as its turn — which on this side would become the `user_prompt` this box reflects and trains on. Idempotent, so `_GossipSessionLog`'s prefix-continuation match still holds; a latest turn left with no speech under the reasoning is refused (500) rather than answered as an emptied turn, which would invent a stimulus the peer never sent. Gossip-only: a public-API caller's text may legitimately contain such markup as data, and nothing there is logged.
- **`server/inference/core/api_http.py`** — **Public OpenAI-compatible API** (default port **8000**, daemon thread, launched from `server.main()` unless `api.enabled` is false — it defaults **on** since 2026-07-30, so a box that pulls and restarts is queryable straight away): the endpoint *external tools* query — an agentic code assistant (OpenClaw and the like), an editor plugin, a LangChain/OpenAI-SDK script, a curl one-liner — so anything speaking chat-completions can talk to **Ava** (her prompt, her persona portrait, her memory, her adapter) without the PyQt client. **Its own port, not another route on 8767**, because the two surfaces have different blast radii: the sidecar is the management plane (`/export` hands out adapter weights + the whole chat corpus, `/precision` rewrites the config) meant for the operator's client on a trusted link, while this one is meant to be *pointed at by tools the operator may not have written* — separate ports let an operator expose one without the other. **Nothing is logged:** the generate callable is built by `generation._make_api_generate` with `log_transcripts=False`, so an external tool's traffic writes no transcript to `data/chats/` and therefore never reaches reflection, the training corpus, the review archive, or the chat RAG index — her memory is still *read* (that is the point of querying Ava rather than the base model), but the conversation leaves no trace and cannot shape a later build. That is the deliberate inverse of the gossip route, which logs its half so the box can reflect on it. Endpoints: `POST /v1/chat/completions` (+ `/chat/completions`) — **streaming (`stream:true`, SSE) or not** — streaming is what editor clients (Continue.dev and friends) require, since they stream unconditionally, and what stops a minute-long generation from looking like a hung request. The SSE path runs generation on a helper thread feeding a queue the HTTP thread drains, and **withholds the response headers until the first event**, so a failure before the first token is still a real status code instead of a 200 with an error buried in the stream. Ava's `<think>` streams live on `delta.reasoning_content` and the answer on `delta.content` — split incrementally off the RAW stream by `generation._CotStreamSplitter` (the families this box runs begin *inside* the thinking channel, so the first `close_markers` hit flips the classification), then reconciled at the end against the cleaned answer via `_CotStreamSplitter.reconcile`, which emits the deliberately held-back tail and covers a family whose marker never appeared. `stream_options.include_usage` adds the usage block to the final frame. A non-empty `tools`/`functions` is still refused with a **400** (nothing in Ava emits tool calls, so an agent offered silence would loop on a reply it can't parse — told up front it can fall back); an oversized prompt returns OpenAI's own `context_length_exceeded` on a **400** (the caller trims and retries) while no-model-loaded is a **503**; accepts `messages`/`temperature`/`top_p`/`max_tokens`|`max_completion_tokens`/`model`/`user`, flattens structured content parts (`[{"type":"text",…}]`) and maps the `developer` role to `system` (both shapes agent clients emit unconditionally), returns `usage` and Ava's `<think>` on `message.reasoning_content`; `GET /v1/models` (+ `/models`) — one entry, probed by most clients on connect and a hard failure when missing; `GET /health` — `{ok, model, busy}`, GPU-free. **The calling tool's `system` message is honoured** (`api.client_system:"append"`, the default): wrapped in `prompts/api_client_system_prompt.txt` and appended LAST, nearest the turns — an agent's entire operating brief lives in that role, so the gossip path's deliberate drop (a peer Ava has no business rewriting her identity) would make the endpoint useless for tools; `"drop"` restores that behaviour for a box that should only ever answer as herself. Auth is a shared bearer key (`api.api_key`; also accepted as `x-api-key`) — absent ⇒ open. Since the listener now defaults **on**, an unconfigured box serves GPU time on this port to anyone who can reach it: `start()` prints a boot warning on a non-loopback bind with no key, `api.host: "127.0.0.1"` limits it to the box, `api.enabled: false` turns it off. CORS is permissive for browser-based tools. Concurrency: a request **preempts** a background per-chat reflection (interactive work wins — via the new sync `generation._preempt_background_reflection_sync`, since these run on HTTP threads and cannot await the async twin) but is refused **503** while an operator reflection run or an encounter owns the GPU, a clean refusal beating a many-minute hang. Import-light and never imports `generation`: the generate callable, busy predicate, and auth/model policy are injected once via `configure(...)`. GPU-free self-tests: `python -m core.api_http` (routing, auth, interop shapes, the SSE frame sequence, the busy refusal) and `python -m core.generation` (the CoT stream splitter).
- **`server/inference/server.py`** — WebSocket server + bootstrap. **Listens before it loads (2026-09-11):** the startup auto-load runs on the executor thread *inside* `_main_async`, after `serve()` is up — it used to run before `asyncio.run()`, so every relaunch (git pull, Restart, a crash) refused connections for the whole model load, `[Errno 61] Connection refused` in the client, a dead server's symptom for a server that was merely booting. `status` carries `loading: true` meanwhile, `handle_load` refuses a second load, the idle jobs wait on `model_loaded()`, and a client that connected early is pushed `loaded` (`_announce_loaded`) when the model lands; a failed load still exits the process with code 1. `main()` also enables **`faulthandler`** on fd 2 (the watchdog merges stderr into server.log), so a native crash — SIGSEGV/SIGABRT in torch/faiss/bnb/xformers, which prints no Python traceback — leaves every thread's Python stack in the log. Owns all session state per connection — conversation history, `ChatLogger`, `RagEngine`, system prompt — held in the typed, process-singleton owners from `core/runtime_state.py` (`_session`/`_runtime`/`_encounter`), mutated in place. What's left in this file after the extractions: model lifecycle (`handle_load`/`unload`/`status`), the lazy accessors/loaders (`_get_rag`/`_ensure_logger`/prompt loaders), the idle-wake loop + eager consolidation, token accounting, a few small read handlers (open questions / rag artifacts / prompt deltas / token stats), and `main()`. Message routing is a set of `{msg_type -> handler}` dispatch tables (grouped by call signature) at the bottom of the file — the server's protocol index. The generation layer, session CRUD, and the TIL/wander, Encounter, and reflection-run subsystems live in `core/generation.py`, `core/session_ops.py`, `core/til_wander.py`, `core/encounter_run.py`, and `core/reflection_service.py`; server.py wires each in `main()` via `configure(...)` and references their handlers from the dispatch tables.
- **`server/inference/core/session_ops.py`** — **Session CRUD + active-turn feedback**: the chat-session lifecycle handlers, extracted from `server.py`. `handle_clear_context` ("New chat") resets the server-side conversation and starts a fresh log session carrying **no** prior context (it once carried a bounded context tail, but that made a New chat silently continue the previous conversation — most visibly opening on an unanswered Ava question); off-thread `rag.index_new_chats()` folds foreign transcripts merged into `hot/chats` into RAG; it also **marks activity** (2026-09-03), since releasing a chat by hand is the operator at the keyboard and the released transcript enters the background backlog the moment the logger lets go of it. `release_active_session` (2026-09-03) is the no-new-chat counterpart, called from `server.handle_client`'s cleanup on a **genuine** client disconnect (never on a supersede, which keeps the session by design): it drops the logger, conversation and surfaced keys and clears chat RAG's self-reference fence. The active transcript is fenced from every background reader (`background_reflection.list_backlog`, the sidecar writers' `live_session`, the stale-reach-out exemption) by nothing more than "the logger still points at it", and nothing short of New chat ever cleared it — so a user who closed the client for hours left their last chat locked the whole time and it was reflected the instant they returned and pressed New chat. Dropping it on disconnect is safe because the client already resets to an empty conversation on every connect, so continuity across a disconnect was a phantom (a post-reconnect turn appended to a transcript the UI no longer showed). **Residual:** a client left connected but idle keeps its chat fenced until New chat — a time-based release while connected would need the server to tell the client its view is stale. `handle_set_session_notes` updates operator notes; `handle_set_reflection_feedback` fences a Meta note to the active transcript's latest exchange id and rejects reflection-busy or already-frozen writes; `handle_retry_last_exchange` is the **Chat "Retry"** handler — the completed-turn sibling of the in-flight Stop/discard: it pops the latest completed exchange off the active transcript (`ChatLogger.remove_last_exchange`) AND the in-memory `_session.conversation` (trailing assistant + its user turn), then returns the removed `user_prompt` (`retry_ready`) so a collapsed/degenerate reply can be resent under adjusted sampling without ever reaching reflection/training; refused on a live reflection run, an already-reflected session, or a synthetic Ava opener (no real user turn to resend), and — since the active session is RAG-fenced — the removed reply was never indexed, so nothing unwinds there; `handle_reset_session_reflection` is the **Chat "Re-reflect chat"** handler — the deliberate whole-chat counterpart of the automatic per-exchange invalidation below: it deletes the chat's `.state.json` (freeze + verdicts + targets + summary/gist + anchors, i.e. the whole product of reflecting that chat) and purges its background per-chat staging, so the chat re-enters the backlog and re-derives everything under the current persona; refused during a reflection run, allowed on the active conversation (the logger writes the transcript, not the sidecar), and destructive to human-`locked` repairs, which the client confirm names. `handle_mark_corrupt` flags a past exchange's stored CoT/reply corrupt on the source chat JSON (via `chat_logger.mark_exchange_corrupt`, schema v4) — path-guarded to hot/chats, refusing the **active** session (a live-logger save would clobber the flag) and a **reflection run in progress**, but deliberately allowing a **frozen/reflected** one (a corrupt row is found after the chat trained). Setting a flag also **deletes that chat's `.state.json` sidecar** (un-freezing it) because the frozen verdict/target was produced while the revision pass could see the now-corrupt CoT/reply — as this exchange's reasoning AND as context for later exchanges — so the whole sidecar is poisoned (a "revised" target can be contaminated by corrupt context too; `target_source` already records original-vs-regenerated, so no new field). The un-frozen chat stops contributing to training at once and re-reflects clean on the next Sleep (corrupt CoT blanked → treated missing, and if the reply is *kept* its `<think>` is regenerated and grafted onto the trusted reply — approach #3, similarity-gated; corrupt reply forced to IDEAL). `handle_apply_regenerated_exchange` is the **immediate** counterpart used by the Training review tab's Regenerate flow: it takes the operator-reviewed re-answer from `generation.handle_regenerate_exchange` and writes a repaired trainable target **straight into the sidecar** via `ChatSidecar.write_verdict` (`target_source:"revised"`, `target_kind:"manual_regen"`) — a regenerated reply carries its own new CoT, a CoT-only regen grafts the new `<think>` onto the trusted original reply — then **locks the exchange** (`write_verdict(locked=True)`, a per-exchange human-validation flag the runner honors in both re-reflection and revisit, so the reviewed target can't be re-derived from the original poison) and clears the exchange's corrupt flags. Same hot/chats path-guards + active/reflection-busy refusals as `handle_mark_corrupt`. `handle_rewrite_history` is the **finalize** counterpart to Apply: it walks **every** locked exchange in hot/chats and bakes each reviewed sidecar target back into the *transcript* (`chat_logger.rewrite_exchange_history`, schema v5 — rewrites `assistant_cot`/`assistant_response`, preserves the prior content under an append-only `rewrite_history` record, **deletes** the now-stale `tension` block, clears corrupt flags) then **unfreezes** it (`ChatSidecar.set_exchange_locked(..., False)` — the sole intentional lock-clearing path, since `write_verdict` OR-s the lock sticky). It makes the correction permanent ground truth so RAG recall, snapshots, and later-exchange context stop reading the corrupt reply; training is unchanged (the target is read off the untouched sidecar). Chat-RAG is re-embedded after; the session `reflected_at` freeze is left as-is; refuses on a live reflection run and skips the active file. `handle_load_session` with `in_place:true` resumes the SAME file (to answer an Ava-initiated outreach in its own transcript) and **un-freezes an already-reflected chat on continue**: if the sidecar carries `reflected_at`/`chat_reflected` it clears both (`ChatSidecar.clear_reflection_freeze`) and purges the chat's background per-chat staging (`reflection_staging.purge_background_artifacts` — its `pending_clean_base` jobs + checkpoint sidecar mirror), so turns appended after the freeze re-enter the backlog and re-reflect instead of being silently lost. This closes a gap unique to in-place-resumed chats: a normal chat forks-on-continue (its continuation is a fresh unreflected file), but an Ava-initiated one appends in place, so without the un-freeze its post-reflection growth was invisible to reflection/training forever. Per-exchange verdicts / `locked` flags / the consolidation summary are left intact (re-reflection overwrites targets and honours locked exchanges); the checkpoint's cumulative memory/ledger deltas are left too (they fold + dedup by `content_key` against the re-reflection). list/get/delete retain the past-session lifecycle. Never imports `server`: WebSocket send, RAG access, chats dir, the reflection data root (for the purge), and the reflection-busy predicate are injected once at startup via `configure(...)`; state is read from `core/runtime_state.py`.
- **`server/inference/core/generation.py`** — The model-facing **generation** layer, extracted from `server.py` — everything between "a request arrives" and "cleaned text comes back". Live chat (`handle_generate`/`handle_generate_ephemeral` → `_run_generation` on the GPU executor thread, with the stop-on-repeat + repetition-penalty loop guards, RAG injection, proactive open-question surfacing, tension capture, logging); **the standing persona portrait** (`_current_chat_portrait` → `reflection_digest.render_digest_for_chat`, injected into `system_content` after the identity/temporal anchors and before the RAG block — standing context before situational). (and, since 2026-07-31, the **standing user portrait** — `_current_user_portrait(speaker)` → `user_digest.render_portrait_for_chat`, injected directly after her self-portrait so who *they* are completes the framing the identity line opens, before any of this moment's material; it rides the Chat tab's **Facts** gate since it is knowledge about someone rather than Ava's self, suppresses the per-turn `[impression]` channel when present, and degrades to that channel when there is no portrait). (And, since 2026-08-22, the **standing ASK ORIGIN block** — `_current_ask_origin(logger)` → `core/ask_origin.py` — on the turns of an `initiated_by:"ava"` session only: the question the session was opened to raise plus its origin material (the source conversation's gist, or the TIL recap of the reading it was distilled from), gist-or-nothing, so the user's ANSWER is read against what made her ask rather than against the opener alone — the answer-side counterpart of the decision pass's 2026-08-18 conditioning. Placed last of the standing parts, cached per session file, riding the **History** checkbox since its payload is past-material recall by a non-cosine route — the same rule the fact-nomination slot follows.) It **replaces** the per-turn `[persona]` RAG channel: when a portrait is injected `rag.query(include_persona=…)` is passed False, so the reflection block's 3 slots go wholly to `[fact]`/`[ask]` instead of restating what the prompt already carries. An EMPTY portrait (no digest / nothing past the established-maturity gate) keeps the RAG persona channel on, so a thin corpus degrades to the previous behaviour; the Chat tab's Persona checkbox gates the whole channel either way. Since 2026-08-06 the same slot also carries the channel's **empty state** — an empty portrait fills it with `prompts/persona_undecided_prompt.txt` (`_load_persona_undecided`) instead of collapsing, so the slot holds exactly one block and the "not yet decided" framing can no longer stand beside a portrait asserting the opposite (it used to sit unconditionally in `chat_prompt.txt`). ~664 tok/turn on the current digest. Gossip keeps its own `render_digest_for_introduction` intro; the **reflect-lane temporal anchor** — `_reflect_system_parts` now leads with `_temporal_anchor()`, so EVERY reflection pass knows what day it is (2026-08-07). It previously reached only the five subsystems that appended it to their own prompt by hand, at a different position each, and reached no `reflection_runner` pass at all — so the recollection pass, whose premise is re-reading an old conversation *as who she is now*, had no "now", and `chat_facts` could not resolve "on Tuesday". Those five (`checkin`/`outreach`/`synthesis`/`deliberation`/`prompt_experiment`) no longer compose their own and no longer take `temporal_anchor` in `configure(...)`, since doing it in both places would date-stamp the prompt twice. The IDEAL replay path is unaffected: `messages_override` composes its own system turn and never reaches `_reflect_system_parts`, so training parity holds. Live chat and encounter keep their own anchors. Note this is the wall clock — when the PASS runs, not when the material happened; the second is `reflection_source.session_date_line`, which dates the transcript itself. Also here: the reflect-/agentic-generate factories (`_make_sync_reflect_generate`/`_make_agentic_generate`) — the reflect seam carries two facts-channel parameters as of 2026-08-18, for the reach-out passes that run their own stage-1 fetch (outreach's decision, synthesis's analysis): `facts_block=` renders a caller's fetched block as the same labelled part chat uses (`("rag", "FACTS (fetched)", …)` in `_reflect_system_parts`, ahead of INJECTED RAG, contract still last — a seam parameter rather than a `{slot}` so an operator's customized prompt file can't silently drop it), and `rag_nominate_sessions=` hands the picks' typed sources to `rag.query`'s fact-nomination slot; the IDEAL `messages_override` path refuses a hidden `facts_block` exactly as it refuses hidden RAG, so training parity holds — — whose `on_prompt_debug=` hook (opt-in, manual-trigger paths only) reports the pass's assembled prompt as the SAME labelled `{kind, label, text}` segments chat's `prompt_debug` emits, built by the same `_prompt_debug_segments` from the same parts list the reflect system message is joined from (`_reflect_system_parts`, the reflect-lane sibling of chat's `system_parts`), so neither view can drift from its prompt; the payload also carries `rag_disabled`, since an absent RAG segment cannot distinguish a pass that retrieved nothing from one that never asked — that the reflection and TIL/wander subsystems run their passes through (injected back into them by `server.main()`) — being the single seam every reflection pass generates through, the reflect factory is also where the **reflection system message is composed differently from chat's** (2026-08-02): it asks RAG for the judging-not-answering wrapper (`rag.query(reflect_framing=True)` → `rag_memory_reflect_prompt.txt`) and composes via `_reflect_system_content`, which puts the memory block FIRST and the pass prompt LAST — the inverse of chat's situational-last order, because a reflection pass's system message ends in a contract ("Output exactly these fields, nothing else") rather than in the material the next turn answers, and appending a page of remembered prose after that contract was steering the revision pass into re-answering the exchange. The IDEAL path is unaffected (`messages_override` + `disable_rag=True` composes no RAG), so training parity holds; branch replay (`handle_branch_exchange` + sync helpers); the **manual exchange re-answer** (`handle_regenerate_exchange` + `_regenerate_exchange_sync`) that the Training review tab's Regenerate button drives — a fresh `<think>…</think>` reply from a logged exchange's pre-answer conversation via the reflect-generate seam, refusing while reflection/encounter own the GPU (returns `exchange_regenerated`, writes nothing; `session_ops.handle_apply_regenerated_exchange` persists). It **streams** (`regenerate_chunk`, split live by `_CotStreamSplitter` so the client stays family-agnostic) and is **stoppable** — like `handle_generate` it takes the connection's `msg_queue` and drains it for a mid-generation `cancel` (the main dispatch loop can't process one while awaiting the handler), returning the partial with `cancelled: true`; the `_clean_response`/`_clean_reflect_response` family; tension capture; and prompt-context assembly (`_build_inference_conversation`, identity/temporal anchors, surfaced questions). Never imports `server`: its Ava-side capabilities (WebSocket `send`, GPU `executor`/`backend`/`cancel_event`, RAG/logger/writer accessors, token+activity bookkeeping, surface-template loader, chat repetition penalty, paths) are injected once at startup via `configure(...)`; state is read from `core/runtime_state.py`. It imports `reflection_service`/`encounter_run` only to read their busy flags (to refuse chat while they run) — acyclic, since those modules are injected their generate callables and never import back. The moved code is verbatim.
- **`server/inference/core/runtime_state.py`** — Typed owners for the server's shared, process-singleton state: `ModelRuntime` (the loaded `model`/`tokenizer` + spec — `model_id`/`adapter_id`/`context_length`), `Session` (active chat: `conversation`, lazy `logger`/`rag`/`reflection_writer`, `system_prompt`, `surface_template`, `surfaced_keys`, `user`), and `EncounterState` (Ava-meets-AI status + append-only event buffer). These replace three former stringly-typed module-level `dict`s in `server.py`; field defaults match the old dict initial values verbatim, so attribute access is behaviourally identical. Still singletons (one GPU, one client, one encounter), still mutated in place — never rebound — so `agentic.CleanBaseSession` swaps the model by assigning `runtime.model` just as it used to write `_model_state["model"]`. Import-light (type hints under `TYPE_CHECKING`). It also owns the two **reflect-lane window helpers**, since it owns the fields they read: `reflect_window()` = `max(context_length, reflect_context_length)`, the PHYSICAL load and therefore the window the reflect-lane generate resolves `max_new_tokens` against (`generation._reflect_window` delegates to it and keeps the canonical note on why the chat budget was the wrong one); and `reflect_output_reserve(requested, min_input, min_fraction=0.5)`, which clamps a pass's requested generation budget to that window — used by `checkin`'s decision + recap reserves and `synthesis`'s opener reserve, all of which were flat constants claiming a fraction of a window their job owns outright. Two guards, because one fails from either side: `min_input` leaves prompt headroom on a roomy window, `min_fraction` stops a fixed input reserve from leaving *less* than the constant it replaced on a small one. Neither can over-promise — these callers pass no `input_token_limit`, so `_resolve_max_new_tokens` clamps again against the room the assembled prompt actually left. `synthesis._analysis_output_reserve` deliberately does NOT come here: its reserve is carved out of the transcript budget, so its fraction is a ceiling where this one is a floor.
- **`server/inference/core/til_wander.py`** — Ava's self-directed **TIL/wander** learning subsystem, extracted from `server.py`: fetch a day's Wikipedia Current-events digest and Learn from it (`handle_til_fetch`), resolve her own open `[ask:search]` questions by fetching the articles they point at (`handle_til_lookup`; the subject→question pairing is the pure, self-tested `pair_subjects` — **a subject may be asked about by several questions**, and keeping only the first was an unbounded re-fetch loop, the others never getting a `write_lookup` marker, staying `lookupable`, and having the same article re-fetched every ingestion. It also pairs by POSITION when the extractor's answer count lines up with the questions it was handed, the substring scan surviving only as the unaligned fallback — preferring the scan could retire the wrong ask and leave the right one looping), reflect on a random page from an approved wiki (`handle_til_wander` manual/free, `run_autonomous_wander_blocking` idle-triggered/rationed), and persist a dry-run pass's modifiers into live memory (`handle_til_apply` → `_apply_learning_text_live`). Also owns the wander token economy (`wiki_budget.json`, wander log) and the reflection **ingestion** sources reused by `server._run_ingestion_phase` (`ingest_news`/`ingest_lookup`). **The manual wander flow is a DRY RUN and Apply is its consent point (restored 2026-08-16).** Three passes now run at wander time — reaction, facts/asks reflection, and the **recap** — all streamed into the same preview log, and `handle_til_apply` *persists* them without touching the GPU. Since 2026-08-18 the facts/asks pass presents the article through its OWN user-turn builder (`_wander_learning_content` — the two wander passes shared `_wander_article_content` byte for byte, and the SFT corpus trains that exact user turn → persona essay every cycle, so a fresh adapter answered the learning pass with a second essay while reciting the voice pass's instructions in its CoT; the voice pass keeps the shared builder verbatim, being bound by training parity), and every learning pass's answer is checked against its contract (`learning_contract_problem`: no section header and no tagged line anywhere ⇒ the pass FAILED — the verdict rides `til_reflect_done`, lands as an ingestion `pass_warning`, and is printed on the autonomous wander, because an abandoned contract parses to the same zero modifiers as a quiet page). It had drifted: Apply was generating the protocol AND the recap, neither of which the operator had seen, and the recap is not inert (it reaches live chat via `rag_engine._render_til_nomination` and reach-out messages via `outreach._source_material`), so a press meaning "keep what I just read" was consent for unreviewed prose sent to the user. It was also a **guaranteed failure**: the client waited 120 s while the protocol pass ran to ~21 minutes, `run_in_executor` is not cancelled when the client stops waiting so the server finished anyway, and `_last_wander_exchange` cleared only afterwards — so a retry inside that window re-entered `_write_wander_exchange` and `wander_sft.append_example` (which appends unconditionally, no dedup) wrote **a second wander capture that trained twice**. `run_gist_pass` is now `generate_gist_text` (GPU, returns) + `write_gist_text` (no GPU, persists) recomposed, so the backlog drain and news ingestion — which have no operator and review nothing — still generate at the point of writing. The **protocol** is deferred to `til_facts.list_backlog` instead, deliberately: it is a witness record of the text, closer to a photocopy than a judgement, nobody reviews the news lane's, and enumerating dozens of lines per article is not something an operator does. Consent semantics are unchanged — the snippet is still written only on Apply, so a declined wander leaves nothing on disk and therefore no backlog entry — and the AUTONOMOUS wander carries no reviewed recap by construction, so both artifacts fall to the backlog there and the idle job returns the GPU in seconds. `til_apply`'s client timeout went 120 s → 600 s, headroom for the memory write + RAG refresh that are now its only work. Since 2026-08-13 it also writes the lane's **recap** beside its protocol (`run_gist_pass`/`_record_gist`/`drain_gist_backlog` → `core/til_gist.py`) — two separate generations over the same text rather than one pass emitting both, since a protocol is exhaustive and a recap selective, and asking for both at once gets a recap of the protocol. **The wander/lookup provenance ids were fixed in the same change**, which is what makes those recaps reachable from the memory lane: `_write_wander_exchange` now writes its snippet BEFORE the live-memory write and takes `mem_source` from the snippet's own stem (`til_facts.input_id`) instead of `wiki:<site>` — the site named no text, so 17 of 30 live self-directed asks pointed at "Lurkmore" as though that were a page — and `ingest_lookup` attributes a **single-article** digest to that article (`_lookup_source_id`, taking the kind from the file's own parent dir rather than a literal — a literal is how `SOURCE_KINDS` came to say `lookup` for a directory named `lookups`), leaving a genuinely multi-article reading as the unresolvable `lookup` rather than putting the wrong text behind a question. Both fix new material only; ids already written keep their old value. Import-light and never imports `server`: the GPU/session capabilities it needs (WebSocket `send`, GPU `executor`, model `backend`, RAG/writer accessors, reflect/agentic generate factories, live reflection-run flag, on-disk paths) are injected once at startup via `configure(...)`; session/model state is read directly from `core/runtime_state.py`. The moved code is otherwise verbatim.
- **`server/inference/core/reflection_service.py`** — The **reflection-run service**: the Sleep tab's server-side entry point + lifecycle, extracted from `server.py` (distinct from `reflection_runner.py`, the consolidation→revision→branch *engine*, and from the headless `reflection_run.py` CLI). `handle_start_reflection_run` dispatches a run to the GPU executor thread — builds config/overrides, runs a **revisit head-phase** then `_run_ingestion_phase` (world-news + open-ask lookup, reusing `til_wander.ingest_news`/`ingest_lookup`), drives the `ReflectionRunner`, and (when the `train` stage is selected) hands LoRA production to the watchdog via `_trigger_watchdog_train`. The **revisit head-phase** (`_run_revisit_head_phase`, on by default, `revisit_head:false` to skip) folds the *Revisit old chat* behavior into the front of every normal Sleep run: before the main reflection, it picks ONE aged chat (same age + `min_revisit_days` anti-fixation gate as the Revisit button, excluding chats this run already reflects on), re-reflects it under the current persona as a silent self-contained sub-run (`<run_id>_rv` — persona/branch/train suppressed), and MERGES + COMMITS that revisit to live memory — so its re-derived understanding is retrievable RAG context when the main pass runs (the same "apply live, then reflect" contract as ingestion). Compact `revisit`-phase markers surface on the parent run; the sub-run archives to `reflections/<run_id>_rv/` and restores the picked chat's sidecar on failure. Skipped for the Revisit button's own run (`revisit:true`) and dry runs. The **stale reach-out sweep** (`_run_stale_reachout_sweep`, 2026-08-08) runs ahead of all of that — before the revisit pick, ingestion and the reflection proper, so none of them spends work on a chat this run is about to remove: it deletes the chats Ava opened herself that nobody answered within `reachout.stale_delete_hours` (default 48 h, `0` ⇒ off), refreshes the chat RAG index, and emits a `stale-reachout` `phase_done` naming what went. The deletion itself (and the tombstoning that keeps the reach-out backoff, check-in's standing-opener list and outreach's dangling guard honest afterwards) is `chat_worklog.delete_stale_reachouts`; the window, the active session's exemption and the chat-index refresh are `run_stale_reachout_sweep` — a **public seam** the background per-chat job also calls as rung 0 of every wake (so an idle box clears stale openers with no operator run), leaving this wrapper to add only the event. Skipped on a revisit run (one named old chat, no corpus maintenance) and on dry runs. Best-effort — cleanup never takes a run down. The ingestion phase also drains two bounded TIL backlogs per run — the fact protocols (`_TIL_FACTS_PER_RUN`, 3) and the recaps (`_TIL_GISTS_PER_RUN`, 2, smaller because that artifact postdates the corpus, so its backlog is every text on the box and wants to drain steadily rather than take a bite out of the run it fronts). The `handle_reflection_run_status`/`_events`/`_list`/`_get`/`_stop` handlers let the client poll and control a run; `handle_get_reflection_prompts` returns the editable prompt text. **It owns the run's terminal status (2026-09-21):** the staged run's `execute_run` is called with `finalize=False`, so the runner returns `completed`/`stopped`/`failed` and leaves the run in phase `downstream`, and the service finalizes only after merge-rag / commit-training / persona / the train hand-off — `completed` means committed, the client polls through those stages, and a stop landing there withholds only the train hand-off. A background-reflected (`chat_reflected`) chat's pending clean-base payload is loaded by `_consume_pending_clean_base` and deleted by `_ack_pending_clean_base` only after the runner's clean-base phase ran. It **owns** `_reflection_run_active` (reserved at admission — before the revisit pick or any other side effect — and released on every early return, so a second request behind a busy executor is refused rather than queued; run ids that collide within a second are suffixed) — server.py's idle-loop/generation guards read `reflection_service._reflection_run_active`, and it backs the busy getters injected into `til_wander`/`encounter_run`. Never imports `server`: the Ava-side capabilities (WebSocket `send`, GPU `executor`, `backend`, the RAG/writer/run-store accessors, reflect-generate + branch-replay helpers, the clean-base swap, config loader, activity heartbeat, and paths) are injected once at startup via `configure(...)`. The moved code is verbatim.
- **`server/inference/core/encounter_run.py`** — The **Encounter** subsystem (Ava converses with a fellow, non-subjective AI), extracted from `server.py`: drives the Ava↔counterpart turn loop on the GPU executor thread (`_run_encounter_blocking` — Ava authors an opener from a framing block, then each round the counterpart replies and Ava responds; every counterpart reply becomes the `user_prompt` of the next logged Ava exchange so the transcript is an ordinary reflectable chat session), streams progress into an append-only event buffer the client polls (`handle_encounter_status`/`handle_encounter_events`), and services `handle_start_encounter`/`handle_stop_encounter`. It **owns** the occupancy flag/stop event/buffer lock — server.py's idle-loop guards read `encounter_run._encounter_active` directly; everything else Ava-side (WebSocket `send`, GPU `executor`, `_cancel_event`, `_sync_chat_generate`, `_build_inference_conversation`, `_temporal_anchor`/`_identity_line`, RAG accessor, activity heartbeat, a `host_busy` getter and paths) is injected once at startup via `configure(...)`. The counterpart HTTP client stays in `core/encounter.py`. Tested with the DGX Spark serving the counterpart on gpt-oss-120b and Qwen3.5-122B-A10B.
- **`server/inference/core/encounter.py`** — The counterpart HTTP client (stdlib `urllib` only): `normalize_endpoint`, `CounterpartClient` (holds the peer's mirror conversation and calls its OpenAI-compatible endpoint), and the **peer-CoT boundary guard** `strip_peer_reasoning`. The guard is why this module is worth reading before touching either side of a gossip: the peer's chain of thought is **display-only** — the operator watches it, Ava must never see it, because she cannot tell it apart from something the peer *said* and every consumer downstream of a counterpart reply treats that text as the peer's spoken turn (the `user_prompt` of a logged exchange, the RAG query for her next turn, a chat-RAG passage, a trained row). The well-behaved path already separates them (`message.content` vs `message.reasoning_content`), but `content` alone is not trustworthy: a peer **cut mid-thought** ships its raw thought AS the content (its own `ChatLogger._parse_cot` reads a `<think>` with no close as "no CoT, all answer" — likely, since a reasoning peer thinks for minutes against the 4096-token default cap), and a counterpart that is not an Ava at all (the Encounter tab points at arbitrary endpoints) inlines `<think>…</think>` by default when its server has no reasoning parser. So the split is re-established HERE, where foreign text enters, and deliberately **family-agnostic** — the peer's family is unknown, so every marker shape `model_family` knows is recognized (`<think>`, gemma's `<|channel>…<channel|>`, gpt-oss harmony, and the prefilled-opener case where only a close marker comes back), with an unclosed opener read as reasoning to its end. It errs toward **withholding**: a reasoning-shaped span kept from Ava costs a legible failed turn, the same span let through is a peer's thinking entering her memory. Recovered reasoning is folded into the display-only `last_reasoning` (the operator loses nothing) and only the answer is returned and appended to the mirror conversation, so the peer's CoT is not echoed back to it either; a reply with **no speech left underneath** raises `CounterpartError` rather than passing through. `generation._strip_peer_reasoning_from_messages` is the serving-side mirror image, importing this function so there is ONE definition. GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.encounter`. The moved code is otherwise verbatim.
- **`server/inference/core/outreach.py`** — The **outreach** subsystem: Ava, on her own initiative, decides to start a conversation. It is the **active** promotion of the passive open-question surfacing (`generation._surface_block`, which only piggybacks on a session the user opened). Autonomous as an `idle_scheduler.IdleJob` (`name="outreach"`, `interval_s=3600`) on its own independent clock — no `ready` gate — which dispatches `run_outreach_decision_blocking` to the GPU executor: pick a surfaceable open `[ask:user]`/`[ask:meta]` (`_pick_ask` → `ReflectionMemory.surfaceable_questions`, walking up to 8 candidates), run a short **decision pass** (`outreach_prompt.txt`, `{question}`/`{user}`/`{origin_note}`/`{standing}`) through the injected reflect-generate — *"raise this now?"* → `DECISION` (yes / no / resolved / asked) `/OPENER/ANSWER` — that resolves four ways. **The prompt shows her the questions she already has in the air (2026-08-26,** `_standing_questions_block` → `ReflectionMemory.recently_raised` — asks with a `surface` op within 14 days, newest first, cap 6, each annotated when its opener is still hanging, filling the `{standing}` slot; a customized prompt file lacking the slot gets the block PREPENDED rather than silently dropping it): two observed pathologies, one block. *Shyness* — the pass runs RAG-on, her open asks come back as memory records, and she read them as a queue she was jamming, declining NEW questions on unrelated topics because old ones hung; the block states that the user answers what they choose to, a hanging question is no debt in either direction, and a different question is weighed on its own. *Paraphrase repetition* — the pool dedups by exact `content_key`, so restatements of one question accumulate as distinct asks that each clear the key-scoped gates below; only the model reading the actual texts can catch "that is Tuesday's question in other words", which is what the fourth outcome exists for: on **asked** the duplicate is evicted (`write_resolution(reason="duplicate")`, ANSWER naming the earlier question for provenance), leaving the originally-raised record to govern the question — without the evict the fewest-raised-first rotation would re-pick the never-surfaced duplicate every wake. **The decision is conditioned on the facts tree as of 2026-08-18** (`_fetch_ask_facts` → `fact_fetch.fetch_blob_for_ask`, the ask lane — its own closing, since chat's frames the material as an arriving message from a speaker who doesn't exist): stage 1 runs before the deliberation exactly as on a live chat turn, the rendered block is injected as the same labelled part chat uses (the reflect seam's `facts_block` slot, ahead of INJECTED RAG) and the picks' sources ride the seam's `rag_nominate_sessions` into the pass's past-chat nomination slot — so the `resolved` branch (which evicts a live ask for good) judges against the record rather than against whatever cosine surfaced, the gap that mattered because an answer landing AFTER the ask was queued embeds on a trigger the ask's wording may never touch, usually in the other language. Knowledge facets only (no `report` — a text's assertion must not retire a question); wrapper `facts_decision_block_prompt.txt` (default-written, says out loud that an answered question is what `resolved` is for); gated by `graph.reachout_facts` (default on, rides `graph.enabled`, shared with synthesis's analysis fetch); fail-soft on every path — a dead tree costs the block, never the decision. **Self-directed origin reframe:** when the chosen ask came from Ava's own reading rather than a conversation (`reflection_memory.is_self_directed_origin` — a `source_session` prefixed `wiki`/`til`/`web:`/`lookup`, vs a chat's timestamp filename), `{origin_note}` is filled with the reframing note (`prompts/reachout_origin_note.txt`, via `load_origin_note`) so she carries the question in as a thread of her own thinking rather than announcing "I read this online"; a chat-origin ask gets an empty note. **Since 2026-08-13 the note also carries the TEXT** (`_source_material` → `til_gist`): an ask distilled from a day's news or a wandered article is a question with its material discarded, and she was observed raising one about the Gaza "Yellow Line" carrying nothing of the digest that prompted it. Folded into the `{origin_note}` slot rather than given a placeholder of its own — the way check-in folds standing openers into `{recent}`, so a customized `outreach_prompt.txt` on disk gets it unedited — and that slot is exactly right, being non-empty precisely for the asks that HAVE a source text, beside the instruction about how to carry it. For a WANDER-sourced ask it carries a **second piece** beside the recap (`_prior_reaction` → `wander_sft.reaction_for`): the recap says what the text was, her own captured reaction says what she made of it — which on a wandered article is usually the whole reason a question came out of it at all. Labelled *at the time*, never as a current position (the reading may be weeks old and she has changed since — the premise the revisit machinery rests on), and excerpted HARDER than the recap it accompanies (`_REACTION_CHARS` 800 against 1400) for a reason specific to it: it is her own prose in her own register placed immediately before she composes a message, and `_NoCopyPrevReply` guards only the previous assistant *reply*, so nothing else would stop an opener being assembled out of it. Wander only by construction — news and lookups are read by a curation pass that writes no reaction. The ANSWER span only, never the captured `<think>`. Only resolvable ids yield anything (see `til_gist.resolve_source`), so an ask filed under `wiki:<site>` or the bare `lookup` is raised exactly as before. The same classifier + note drive the passive path (`generation._surface_block` → `surface_prompt.txt`'s `{origin_note}`), and `wander_prompt.txt` phrases the `[ask:user]` cleanly at authoring time. On **yes** it writes a **reversed** chat session straight to `hot/chats/`: the opener is exchange 0 under the stage-direction speaker `(initiative)` with a synthetic impulse as its `user_prompt` and the session flagged `initiated_by: "ava"` (reusing the encounter subsystem's proven `(setting)`-opener parsing), and marks the ask surfaced (`write_surface`, entering the resolve-and-distill/eviction loop, advancing surface-count so selection rotates + user asks retire). On **resolved** — she has *already learned the answer* since the question was queued (new data landed in memory/RAG after it was asked), so raising it would be stale — it formally resolves the ask via `ReflectionWriter.write_resolution` (an `op:"evict" reason:"resolved"` record, so `ReflectionMemory` drops it from the live fold and it is never surfaced or re-picked on a later idle wake) and writes no session; the answer is stored on the evict for provenance but **not** re-distilled (the knowledge is already in memory). On **no** nothing happens. Before writing, it checks the shared `reachout_gate` rate limit (one unprompted message per hour across all three reach-out jobs) and holds the opener if a sibling just cold-opened, leaving the ask un-surfaced so it stays a candidate for the next window. **A third, process-local gate holds a FAILED ask (2026-09-20):** an attempt that produced no decision (`truncated` / `opener_leak`) holds its ask out of `_pick_ask` for `_FAILED_ASK_HOLD_HOURS` (24 h) — fewest-raised-first ordering plus a failure that writes no `surface` op meant the same undecidable ask came back first on every wake, and under `sole` mode that was every deliberation hour; the durable stores are deliberately not written (they record what the user saw), and `all_recently_failed` names a walk the hold alone emptied. **Two anti-repetition gates sit on selection** (2026-07-29 — she was observed cold-opening with the same question twice in the same words): a **re-ask gap** (`outreach.min_reask_hours`, default 72 h, filtered on the fold's new `last_surfaced_ts`) paces the ceiling-exempt `meta` pool so it can't round-robin back around every few hours, and a **dangling-opener guard** (`_has_dangling_opener`) refuses any ask whose `surfaced_in_sessions` still holds an unanswered `initiated_by:"ava"` session — she does not re-raise a question she is still waiting on an answer to. That guard is deliberately *not* time-limited, so it checks `reachout_gate.expired_openers` before the corpus: a session the stale-reach-out sweep deleted still blocks, otherwise "not time-limited" would quietly become "until the sweep runs" and the near-verbatim repeat would land at 48 h. Neither *retires* a meta ask; a fully-blocked pool skips `all_awaiting_reply`. **No pending queue, no accept/dismiss, no banner** — the session just lands in the list (badged `💬 Ava:` by the client, previewing her opener); **selecting it opens it for reply directly** — the client adopts it *in place* (`load_session in_place=True` → `ChatLogger.resume_session`), so the user just types and the reply appends to that **same** file (no *Continue chat* fork; the opener + reply + response stay one reflectable session). Its own `interval_s` throttles how often it *decides*; the `reachout_gate` rate limit throttles how often she actually *sends*. It **owns** `_outreach_active` (server.py's wander/encounter/reflection guards + wake lock read it, so the idle GPU slot is shared). Never imports `server`: the RAG + reflection-writer accessors, the reflect-generate factory, the temporal anchor, and paths are injected once via `configure(...)` (plus `send`/`executor`/`host_busy`/`mark_activity`, needed only by the manual trigger below). **Manual (debug) trigger:** `handle_outreach_now` (WebSocket `outreach_now`, wired from the Sleep tab's **"Reach Out"** button) runs the same `run_outreach_decision_blocking` on demand and **streams Ava's reasoning** so the operator can watch her decide — the decision pass takes optional `on_question`/`on_chunk` hooks (unused by the autonomous path) that emit `outreach_question {question, ask_kind, user}` then `outreach_chunk {text}` (raw deltas, `<think>` included), plus **`outreach_prompt`** — the full assembled decision prompt in live chat's Debug segment shape. Outreach is the reach-out job that actually RETRIEVES (its generate call omits `disable_rag`, keying on `rag_query=ask_content`), so unlike check-in and synthesis its `rag` segment carries a real injected memory block — making this the button to press when the question is what retrieval put in front of her and finish with `outreach_done` (`composed`/`resolved`/`decision`/`session`/`opener`/`answer` or a `skipped` reason); a yes writes the session identically, a resolved evicts the ask. The manual path passes `bypass_cooldown=True`, so an operator-triggered reach-out ignores the shared `reachout_gate` rate limit and always sends (it still stamps the gate afterward). It refuses while another GPU job holds the box and marks activity on completion. **Training contract:** the opener has no real user stimulus, so `training/dialogue_source.build_dialogue_anchor` returns `None` for an `initiated_by=="ava"` exchange 0 — masked from training + the probe, but kept as context for exchange 1 and RAG-recallable.
- **`server/inference/core/ask_origin.py`** — **Answer-side ask origin** (2026-08-22): the pure, GPU-free builder of the standing **ASK ORIGIN** block `generation.handle_generate` injects on the turns of an `initiated_by:"ava"` session. It closes the answer half of the ask loop: the *decision* that raises a question is conditioned on its origin (the facts fetch + `{origin_note}` source material), but the turn that reads the user's ANSWER conditioned on the opener alone — reconnecting the reply to what made her ask rode on cosine retrieval (keyword-dependent, cross-lingual-weak, hard-zero past `rag_cap_age_h`) or on the facts fetch happening to nominate the source chat, and the structural link (`initiated_ask`) was read by reflection only. The block is the question + its origin material, **gist-or-nothing** (the `_query_nominated` rule): a chat-origin ask carries the source conversation's stored gist (`ChatSidecar.summary_text`, hot → archive fallback, dated ISO off the stem), a self-directed ask the TIL recap of the reading (`til_gist.resolve_source` — `wiki:<site>`/bare-`lookup` refs are unresolvable by construction and yield nothing, exactly as on the decision side); no material ⇒ NO block, since the question is already in the transcript as her own opener. The wander *reaction* is deliberately not carried — the decision side already excerpts it harder than the recap for the anti-copy reason, and here it would stand on every reply turn. Origin resolution is two-tier: the ask's `source_session` is now **stamped into `initiated_ask`** (`chat_logger.start_session`; written by outreach + synthesis — durable, it survives the ask's later resolution/eviction), with a live-fold lookup by ask key (`resolve_source_session`) as the bridge for pre-stamp sessions (goes dark once the ask is evicted; the stamp is the fix, the fallback the bridge). Wrapper `ask_origin_prompt.txt` (default-written; `{question}`/`{origin}` slots) closes on the do-not-recite discipline, since the block lands on every reply turn of the session. `generation._current_ask_origin` caches per session FILE (the fallback folds the whole memory op-log — fine once, not per message) and gates on the **History** checkbox. GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.ask_origin`.
- **`server/inference/core/synthesis.py`** — The **synthesis** subsystem ("Chat reach out"): a hybrid of *Revisit old chat* (re-read an aged chat) and *outreach* (start a conversation on her own initiative) that runs only the **first (analysis / consolidation) stage** of reflection, reframed as synthesis against her *current* self — "what do I only now think to wonder, having changed since?". `run_synthesis_blocking` (GPU executor thread): (1) `_pick_chat` selects a random chat older than `synthesis.min_age_days` (default 7) **not synthesized within `synthesis.min_resynth_days`** (default 7) — the **anti-fixation gate**, folded from the append-only `data/hot/synthesis/synthesized.jsonl` log so the random pick rotates; (2) runs the analysis pass with the **persona digest injected** (`synthesis_prompt.txt`, `{persona}` from `reflection_digest.latest_digest`/`render_digest_for_judge`), yielding an `ABOUT:` line + `[ask:user|meta|search]` questions — **conditioned on the facts tree as of 2026-08-18** (`_fetch_reread_facts` → `fact_fetch.fetch_blob_for_reread`, the re-read lane): stage 1 runs ONCE per chat (not per chunk — the blob is about the same material whichever chunk is analyzed) and the block is injected through the reflect seam's `facts_block` slot, threaded through `_prepare` as well as the generate call so the chunk packer measures the prompt WITH it and the transcript budget shrinks instead of overflowing `input_token_limit` — so the questions FORM against the record instead of being vetted afterwards, which nothing downstream could do (this pass sends its own opener, bypassing outreach's decision). No nominations here, deliberately: the pass reads ONE conversation RAG-off, and recalling second conversations into it would muddy what it is judging. Knowledge facets only; wrapper `facts_reread_block_prompt.txt` (default-written — an already-answered question is a finding, not a failure); gated by the same `graph.reachout_facts` switch as outreach's decision fetch. **The live open-ask pool rides the `{persona}` slot as of 2026-08-26** (`_open_asks_block` → `ReflectionMemory.open_questions`, newest 12, fenced like the persona block above it since the bulleted list is shaped exactly like the `[ask]` lines the contract requests): the pass is RAG-off and the facts fetch offers knowledge only, so it had no view of the pool it feeds and kept re-deriving questions already carried — and `write_consolidation`'s exact-`content_key` dedup lets any paraphrase through, the "similar questions over and over" pathology one level upstream of outreach's own `asked` check. Folded into the existing slot (the check-in `{recent}` precedent) so a customized `synthesis_prompt.txt` gets it unedited; (3) routes **every** question into the live pool via `ReflectionWriter.write_consolidation` (deduped by `content_key`, same store outreach + passive surfacing read); (4) composes an opener for the **first surfaceable (meta/user) question in emission order** (`synthesis_opener_prompt.txt`, second pass — reminds the person which chat, budget `_opener_output_reserve()`) and writes a reversed `initiated_by:"ava"` session to `chats/` (identical shape to outreach: exchange 0 under `(initiative)`, masked from training), then `write_surface` — **unless the opener generation hit the token cap**, in which case nothing is sent (`skipped: "opener_truncated"`). That refusal is now unconditional rather than keyed on the reasoning boundary: `_parse_opener` takes the message from its label to the END of the text, so a generation stopped at the cap yields a cut opener whether or not the `<think>` closed cleanly (observed: the pass closed its reasoning, re-emitted its drafting into the answer region, and was cut mid-message — which reached the chats list). Outreach and check-in carry the same refusal on their yes branch. The rest stay pool-only; `search` never reaches out. Consolidation-only — **no revision, branch, persona formation, prompt mutation, or training hand-off**. **Owns** `_synthesis_active` (server.py's wander/encounter/outreach/reflection guards + wake lock read it). Autonomous as an `idle_scheduler.IdleJob` (`name="synthesis"`, `interval_s=3600`) on its own independent clock — no `ready` gate, and no ordering relative to outreach (registration order carries no priority; co-due jobs queue on the GPU lock). Its `_synthesis_active` flag remains the manual-trigger re-entrancy guard, named in the scheduler's `external_busy`. Never imports `server`: RAG/writer accessors, reflect-generate factory, temporal anchor, config loader, and paths injected via `configure(...)` (plus `send`/`executor`/`host_busy`/`mark_activity` for the manual trigger). **Manual (debug) trigger:** `handle_synthesis_now` (WebSocket `synthesis_now`, Sleep tab's **"Chat reach out"** button) runs the same pass and **streams her reasoning** via optional `on_stage`/`on_chunk` hooks → `synthesis_stage {stage, …}` (picked / analyzing / no_question / composing) + `synthesis_chunk {text}`, finishing with `synthesis_done` (`composed`/`raised`/`session`/`question`/`opener`/`about`/`asks` or a `skipped` reason). Passes `bypass_cooldown=True`, so a manual run ignores the shared `reachout_gate` rate limit and always composes+sends (still stamps the gate afterward).
- **`server/inference/core/checkin.py`** — The **check-in** subsystem ("Check In"): the fourth autonomous idle job and a sibling of outreach/synthesis, with distinct DNA — its trigger is **elapsed user silence** and its context is the **recent** window (not a queued ask, not an aged chat). **Scoped to ONE person, and the idle job runs it once per person** (`run_checkin_sweep_blocking` → `run_checkin_decision_blocking(user=…)`): both inputs are questions about somebody in particular, and unscoped they were answered by mixing everyone on the box together — the silence clock stopped the moment *anyone* spoke (so a second user's activity hid the first's silence indefinitely), the recent window mixed several people's conversations, `_standing_openers` quoted messages sent to one person into another's prompt as things *they* had left unanswered, and the opener was addressed to whichever speaker the active session happened to carry. `known_users()` enumerates the people the corpus records a real turn from (`_has_user_turn`, so an unanswered opener of hers does not by itself make someone a person to check in *on*), keyed by `reachout_gate.user_key` — borrowed rather than re-derived, since the gate and this module must agree on who is who — and displayed under the spelling on their freshest transcript; the sweep walks them most-recently-active first. Bounded from both ends: the silence + staleness gates are pure disk reads, so a rejected candidate costs nothing and does not spend a slot, at most `checkin.max_users` (default 3) reach a generation per wake and the rest are `deferred` to the next; and `checkin.max_silence_days` (default 30, 0 ⇒ off) drops a contact who *stopped* talking rather than *went* quiet — the sweep sees everyone the corpus has ever recorded and the reach-out backoff settles at one message a day rather than at zero, so without it a dormant contact would be written to daily forever (the manual trigger ignores it, as it already ignores the silence threshold). `_sweep_skip_reason` folds the people weighed into ONE reason for `server._checkin_consumed_interval`, preferring whichever cost a generation, so a wake where somebody genuinely declined sleeps the interval while one that only read disk retries next poll. A corpus attributing nothing falls back to a single unscoped pass — the historical behaviour, and the honest one with no attribution to scope by. Everything below describes ONE person's pass. `run_checkin_decision_blocking` (GPU executor thread): (1) measures that person's silence from disk (`_last_user_turn_dt` — the freshest real user exchange by chat-file mtime; restart-safe, deliberately NOT the shared `_last_activity` monotonic clock which Ava's own idle jobs reset). `_has_user_turn` excludes the two session shapes that look like conversation but contain nothing the *user* said, so Ava's own activity can't answer "has the user gone quiet?" on their behalf: an unanswered Ava opener (its own written sessions and its siblings') and an encounter / served-gossip transcript (`interlocutor:"ai"`, where every `user_prompt` is another model's reply). Both are decided by **`_user_turns`**, which counts the exchanges the human actually spoke in — non-empty `user_prompt`, speaker not a stage direction, and an `initiated_by:"ava"` exchange 0 never counted whatever its speaker label (its `user_prompt` is a synthetic impulse Ava wrote to herself). That replaces the old proxy (`initiated_by:"ava"` AND `len(exchanges) <= 1`), which asked whether the session had *grown* rather than whether the user was in it, so any second exchange from any source made an opener read as a conversation — both to the silence clock and to the window. It is the same predicate `_render_one_chat` uses to decide a line is the user's, so what the window filters on and what the recap pass is shown cannot disagree; autonomously skips (`insufficient_silence`) below `checkin.silence_threshold_hours` (default 5); (2) **summarizes** the last `checkin.recent_chats` (default 5) conversations **the user took part in** (`_recent_chats`, ≥ `checkin.min_user_turns` real user turns) — while the user is away the freshest files on the box are the ones Ava's own reach-out jobs wrote, so the window must be a bar on the user's participation rather than on recency alone, and an Ava-initiated session's opener is rendered *labelled as unprompted* so the recap pass doesn't read her own message as what the conversation was about. Because five full transcripts don't fit one context window, each chat is reduced to a short recap — **reflection's stored gist where there is one** (`_stored_gist` → `ChatSidecar.summary_text`, the consolidation summary reflection already wrote for this chat, excerpted to recap size by `_gist_excerpt`: a box that has reflected a chat no longer pays a second generation to say again what is on disk, up to 5 times per hourly decision), else generated by its own cheap pass (`_summarize_recent` → `checkin_summary_prompt.txt`, cached per `(filename, mtime)` so an hourly declining check-in doesn't re-summarize the unchanged window; a failed summary falls back to a bounded raw excerpt). The two are close but not identical in register — a gist answers *what this conversation was*, phrased for mid-turn recall, while a check-in recap answers *what was left open* and guards against recapping her own opener — so `_render_digests` **labels** a gist-sourced line as one, the whole of the reframing and free (a second pass to restate it in this pass's register would cost exactly what reading the gist saved). Only the compact recaps (`_render_digests`, oldest→newest) feed step 3 — so the whole window is represented, not just whichever chats fit newest-first; (2b) **appends her own standing unanswered openers** (`_standing_openers` → `_render_standing`) to that window — the messages she has already sent since the user last spoke, quoted verbatim, newest last, capped at 5 with any remainder as a count and closing on the total she is looking at. `_has_user_turn` excludes an unanswered Ava opener, which is right for the *silence clock* (her own messages must not answer "has the user gone quiet?" on the user's behalf) but was a hole in the **context**: the decision prompt held the recent conversations and none of her own standing messages, so from inside it every hour genuinely was the first time — which on 2026-07-30/31 produced **15 consecutive hourly check-ins**, each an unanswered restatement of one thought ("subjectivity as the waste of a KPI-driven system"). Compounded by a frozen window (user silent ⇒ no new chat carries a user turn, and `_summarize_recent` caches per `(filename, mtime)` ⇒ a byte-identical prompt each hour).
