Openers the stale-reach-out sweep has since deleted are folded back in from `reachout_gate.expired_openers` (marked `expired`, quoted from the tombstone's stored text): they are by construction the OLDEST ones — the evidence this has been going on a while — so reading only what remains on disk would hand the pass a window that resets itself every 48 h, which is the shape of the very bug this list was added to fix. It is folded into the existing `{recent}` slot rather than a new placeholder, so an operator's already-customized `checkin_prompt.txt` gets it too; the `standing` count rides the `deciding` stage into the Sleep tab. NOT an ask-dedup failure — check-in consults no ask pool. (3) runs a two-outcome decision pass over the recaps (`checkin_prompt.txt`, `{hours}`/`{user}`/`{recent}`; yes/no — no `resolved`, framed to synthesize *across* the conversations) and on **yes** writes a reversed `initiated_by:"ava"` session to `chats/` (identical shape to outreach/synthesis: exchange 0 under `(initiative)`, masked from training via `build_dialogue_anchor`), else declines (silence is valid). **Shared reach-out gate:** immediately before writing the session it calls `reachout_gate.may_reach_out(user)` and stamps `mark_reachout(user)` on a compose, so outreach/synthesis/check-in together cold-open the user at most once per window (`core/reachout_gate.py`) — and that window **doubles per unanswered opener** (capped 24 h), which is the hard limit on the runaway above; a hold returns a `reachout_cooldown`/`reachout_backoff` skip. Check-in passes the **person** (outreach/synthesis stay unscoped), so the limit is on how often she cold-opens *someone*: a message to one person is not a reason to withhold one from another, and one person's silence must not drive the backoff for people who are answering normally. The scoped view still counts an unscoped sibling's send when it went to the same person. **Autonomous** as an `idle_scheduler.IdleJob` (`name="checkin"`, `interval_s=3600`, registered in `main()`) — it takes **no** `ready` gate (the reach-out limit used to be one, which starved this job outright), the silence gate lives in the blocking pass (a cheap `insufficient_silence` skip, classified un-`consumed` so it retries next poll), and the scheduler's GPU lock handles mutual exclusion (no per-module OR-chains). **Owns** `_checkin_active`, its manual-trigger re-entrancy flag, named in the scheduler's `external_busy`. Never imports `server`: RAG accessor, reflect-generate factory, temporal anchor, config loader, and paths injected via `configure(...)` (plus `send`/`executor`/`host_busy`/`mark_activity` for the manual trigger). **Manual (debug/simulate) trigger:** `handle_checkin_now` (WebSocket `checkin_now`, Sleep tab's **"Check In"** button) **forges the silence period** (`forced_hours`, default = threshold, clamped up to real elapsed so it stays realistic) so it fires regardless of real elapsed time. It runs ONE person's decision — the message's `user` field, else the active session's speaker (the client sends the Chat tab's user box) — not the whole sweep, whose several deliberations would interleave into one unreadable stream; watching one is the point of the button. Streams progress via `on_stage`/`on_chunk` → `checkin_stage {stage: considering | summarizing (i/n, session) | recapped (i/n, session, when, user, recap, source, cached, truncated) | deciding}` + `checkin_chunk {text}` (decision-pass reasoning only; per-chat summaries stream a stage marker, not tokens — but their **finished recap text** rides the `recapped` stage, since those recaps are the entire input the decision pass reasons over and a watcher who sees only "recapping 3/5…" then a yes/no cannot tell a sound decision from one made on a garbled window). The autonomous path routes the same hook into the activity journal (`server._checkin_on_stage`), rendering the recap as an indented body under the headline — but **only for a freshly generated recap**: the window freezes while the user is away (the cache is keyed `(filename, mtime)`), so journalling a cached one — or a stored gist, whose body already reached the journal through the reflection run's own mirror — would repeat identical text hourly. The Sleep tab shows cached + gist recaps too, marked as such — an operator who pressed the button wants to see what she is reading, not where it came from. It also streams **`checkin_prompt`** per generation — the full assembled prompt of each uncached recap pass and of the decision pass, in live chat's Debug segment shape (see the protocol table), which is what makes this subsystem's retrieval inspectable: **both of its passes run `disable_rag=True`**, so the view reports `rag_disabled` rather than leaving an absent block to be read as an empty retrieval. Finishing with `checkin_done` (`composed`/`decision`/`session`/`opener`/`silence_hours` or a `skipped` reason). Passes `bypass_cooldown=True`, so a manual run ignores the shared `reachout_gate` rate limit and always sends on a yes (still stamps the gate afterward).
- **`server/inference/core/background_reflection.py`** — The **background per-chat reflection** subsystem: the fifth autonomous idle job, and the one place a user chat *preempts* reflection instead of being refused. The expensive part of a Sleep run is the *per-chat* work (consolidation + revision + branch generation, one generation per exchange), and it parallelizes across chats — so this does it incrementally after a long **user-idle** stretch (its own `idle_seconds=1800`, longer than the reach-out jobs; `interval_s=120` re-arms while a backlog remains), leaving the nightly cycle far less to do. **A wake is a three-rung ladder, cheapest first, and it stops at the first rung with work** (`run_background_reflection_blocking`, GPU executor thread). **Rung 0 — stale reach-out sweep** (GPU-free, unconditional, and first so no rung below spends a generation on a transcript about to be removed): delete the chats Ava opened herself that nobody answered inside `reachout.stale_delete_hours` (48 h) via the injected `stale_sweep_fn`, wired to `reflection_service.run_stale_reachout_sweep` — the SAME policy object (window, live-session exemption, chat-index refresh) the reflection run's head phase uses, extracted so "stale reach-out" means one thing on the box and an idle box clears them without waiting for an operator Sleep run. **Rung 1 — sidecar backfill**: every chat missing its gist (`<stem>.summary.json`) or fact protocol (`<stem>.facts.json`), **oldest first** (`list_sidecar_backlog`), because two cheap generations per chat across the whole corpus buys broad recall + a complete extraction record long before one generation *per exchange* of one chat buys deep targets. That backlog is deliberately NOT gated on the reflect-once freeze (the chats needing it most are old ones reflected before these artifacts existed) and asks `ChatSidecar.summary_text` rather than the file, so a legacy in-sidecar `consolidation_summary` counts as done; it skips the active session, an empty chat, and an unanswered opener (rung 0's business). The generation is `core.modules.run_module_blocking` — the same `chat_summary`/`chat_facts` specs the Modules tab simulates with, so what lands on disk is reproducible and retunable from the workbench — and `_write_backfilled` is the **sink the registry deliberately does not carry**: the pass returns its value and this caller decides where it goes, which is the first real use of that detachment. A preempt mid-generation returns partial text, which is DISCARDED rather than written (same rule as a preempted chat). Rung 1 *gates* rung 2, so two guards stop it wedging the ladder: a chat whose passes generate but produce nothing writable is retired for the process (`_backfill_unproductive` — in memory, not on disk, since the cause is usually the prompt or the model and a restart is how those change), while a **box-level** refusal (`no_model`/`no_prompt`/unknown module) instead stops the drain and reports a skip, so the scheduler retries promptly and no chat is retired for a condition that was never about it. Kill switch `background_reflection.sidecar_backfill: false`. **Rung 2 — the per-chat reflection drain**, only when rung 1 is empty (the overlap is accepted knowingly: a chat reflection re-derives both sidecars anyway). It enumerates unreflected chats (`list_backlog` — neither `reflected_at` nor `chat_reflected` frozen, not an unanswered outreach, not the active session) and drains them **one at a time**, each in a **fresh throwaway staging dir**, delegating the single-chat reflection to the injected `reflect_one_chat_fn` (wired to `reflection_service.run_chat_only_reflection`, so this module carries no generation plumbing). It runs `chat_only` mode (`ReflectionRunConfig.chat_only` → `ReflectionRunner.execute_run` skips the run-level persona digest and the caller passes `clean_base_ctx=None`, so the clean-base judge + fact placement do NOT run here) and does NOT reach out or train. As each chat is frozen it also records ONE first-person `conversation` entry in Ava's episodic worklog (`_record_conversation_worklog` → `core.chat_worklog.record_conversation`: "I talked with {user}. {gist}"; this module supplies only the two inputs read from its own throwaway staging — the transcript and the per-chat consolidation gist — while the entry itself, and the close of the reach-out thread that opened the chat, are shared with the FOREGROUND runner's `reflected_at` freeze, so a chat produces one such entry whichever path reflected it) — this is the answer to "when does a chat produce a worklog entry", since a live transcript has no clean close. **Two-stage freeze:** each completed chat is frozen `chat_reflected` (stage one; `ChatSidecar.mark_chat_reflected`) and its clean-base **job payloads** (`judge_jobs`/`fact_candidates`, plain JSON) are persisted (`reflection_staging.write_pending_clean_base` → `data/hot/reflection_pending_clean_base/<stem>.json`, a **sibling** of the checkpoint so the fold's `discard_checkpoint` never deletes unconsumed jobs); its one-chat staging deltas + frozen sidecar are APPENDED into the durable `data/hot/reflection_checkpoint/` (`commit_background_chat`, accumulating across wakes). The next NORMAL reflection run folds the checkpoint into live at start, then (a new per-session branch in `execute_run`, driven by `consume_pending_clean_base_fn`) loads those payloads into its end-of-run clean-base phase **without re-generating** the per-chat passes and stamps `reflected_at`. A preempted (partial) chat leaves only the throwaway staging (discarded) — nothing reaches the checkpoint, so the cache is never corrupted; the chat is re-reflected from the top later. **Preemption:** `request_preempt()` (called by `generation._run_generation` + the ephemeral/regenerate guards, and by an operator Sleep-run start in `reflection_service`, when `_background_reflection_active`) sets the run stop flag and fires the shared `_cancel_event`, so the in-flight reflection generation aborts within roughly one step (the reflect-generate path clears+checks `_cancel_event`); the caller waits briefly for the executor to free, then proceeds. An operator-launched Sleep run / encounter is still *refused* (deliberate work must not die to a stray chat turn). **Owns** `_background_reflection_active` (named in the scheduler's `external_busy`) + the current run id. Never imports `server`/`reflection_service`: the single-chat engine, the stale sweep, the chat-index refresh (rung 1 writes gists, which are chat-RAG passages), the config loader, chats/data/runs dirs, host-busy predicate, and cancel event are injected via `configure(...)`. GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.background_reflection`.
- **`server/inference/core/graph_rebuild.py`** — **Facts-tree upkeep**: the idle job that folds the `.facts.json` protocols into `data/graph/tree.json`, the file both fetch channels read (live chat's `generation._fetch_facts_block_sync` and the TIL reading pass's `til_wander._fetch_reading_facts`). It exists because **nothing on the box was doing this** — building was a manual `python -m graph.build`, so a **clean install had no tree at all** and skipped every fetch with `no_tree` forever while `graph.enabled` reported `true` (a channel off in substance and on in configuration, writing protocols correctly and never reading one), while a running box froze its tree at the last hand-run build. Neither state announces itself: an empty blob from a missing tree is indistinguishable at the call site from an empty blob because nothing was relevant. `staleness()` is the pure check, three signals cheapest-first — **no tree** (missing/corrupt/unrecognised, which `read_tree` already collapses to `None` because all three mean *rebuild*), **count drift** against the `stats.read.files` the tree recorded (what catches a *deleted* protocol, which moves no mtime forward, and a restored copy whose mtimes predate the build), and **any protocol newer than the tree FILE** — deliberately the file's mtime rather than the document's `built_at`, which is a naive local ISO string whose clock and offset would have to be reconstructed to compare against a filesystem mtime; a Migrate/snapshot restore can reset mtimes and buy one spurious rebuild, which costs milliseconds and self-corrects. `rebuild()` delegates to `graph.build.build` so the job and the CLI cannot come to mean different things, and takes `now` as a parameter for the reason `store.build_doc` takes `built_at`. Rebuilding wholesale is safe by construction: the tree is *derived and disposable — never a store*, and `fold` is deterministic. Registered as an `idle_scheduler.IdleJob` (`interval_s=3600`, `idle_seconds=300`) beside `worklog_sweep`, the box's other **GPU-free** upkeep job, and takes its short idle window for the same reason — a full idle hour would starve it on precisely the busy box where the tree goes stale fastest. Skips when current (so it does not log hourly) and never raises: a failed fold returns an error line rather than taking the scheduler down, and says so out loud, since every downstream skip would otherwise read as "nothing relevant found". The journal line leads with **`showable`** — the `property`+`event` count, i.e. what a fetch pass may actually be OFFERED after `claim_candidates` filters to the knowledge facets and drops `person:_self` — because the raw claim total says almost nothing about whether the channel has anything to work with (measured 2026-08-15: 197 claims, 5 candidates). GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.graph_rebuild`.
- **`server/inference/core/assoc_bridge.py`** — The bridge to **`assoc/`**, the associative facts library (ASSOCIATIVE_MEMORY.md), wired in BESIDE the facts-tree channel (2026-09-10). Six surfaces: the **feed** (`run_feed_blocking`, idle job `assoc_feed`, under the GPU lock because it loads BGE-M3; **its cadence is connection-aware since 2026-09-18** — `server._assoc_feed_ready`: with NO client connected it fires on its 5-min idle window, with one connected only after an HOUR of real idle time (a chat turn queues on the executor behind a running feed, and the idle clock sees sent turns, not typing — so a feed started during a long message landed the reply minutes late; "skip while connected" alone would starve it on a client left open all day), and once fired it re-arms every 15 min rather than hourly (`_ASSOC_FEED_INTERVAL_S`), a no-change wake being a cheap `current` bail; **its sync + rebuild run in a SUBPROCESS since 2026-09-11** — `core/assoc_feed_worker.py`, spawned as `python -m core.assoc_feed_worker` with the paths + raw `assoc` config on stdin, progress relayed line by line through the parent's stdout so the activity tee sees it, the report as a marked last line; a rebuild is minutes of pure-Python work that never releases the GIL, unlike a CUDA-bound generation, so in-process it starved the asyncio loop for the whole wake and a client connecting meanwhile timed out on the WebSocket opening handshake. The library never loads the LLM, so the worker needs nothing from the parent; the one model-bound piece, the typed-relation pass, runs in the parent AFTER the worker's rebuild over the build it produced — `assoc.rebuild.relation_pass`, ONE function with the rebuild's own call, cache-only there — and the NEXT wake's rebuild folds the cache, forced when the cache holds relations the build lacks. After every worker run the parent calls `Library.reload()`: the `Store` holds `keys.json` in memory from construction, and without the reload a pull over the worker's build found nothing allowed. `assoc.feed_subprocess: false` restores in-process; `assoc.feed_timeout_s` (7200) kills a hung worker) — `assoc.ava_import.sync` over the live corpus: transcripts under `data/chats/` and texts under `data/til/snippets/<kind>/`, each fingerprinted (mtime + size of the file and its two sidecars, `data/assoc/state/feed.json`) so an unchanged corpus costs a stat per file; a new or grown document is ingested, its `.facts.json` protocol imported as the library's protocol (`witness.import_protocol`, every fact anchored to a chunk by lemma or L2-cell overlap — the cells are what place an English fact over a Russian exchange) and its `.summary.json` as the gist, then `Library.rebuild()` (full on the first feed, staleness-driven after; ~350 s for 394 documents, 0.6 s to skip). Nothing generates: the library reads what the reflection passes wrote, and `data/assoc/` is derived and disposable like the tree (wiped with the regenerable state). The **fetch** (`fetch_block`): stage 1 of a chat turn through `pull → select → inject`, returned in the SAME shape as `generation._fetch_facts_block_sync`'s result — `text`/`claims_text`/`picked`/`n_rendered`/`candidates.n`/`sources`/`sessions` — so the Chat tab's facts block, the Debug view and the past-chat nominations need no new protocol; `channel:"assoc"` and `assoc_ids` ride the result, and after the reply lands `touch_after_turn` records an access on what was injected (the library's activation state). Taken under `assoc.enabled` (default **true** since 2026-09-10); a library with no build yet falls through to the tree channel, and `false` restores it exactly. The **workbench** (`prepare_for_module`/`finish_for_module`): the `assoc_fetch` module in `core.modules`, so the two channels can be compared on one chat with real output before the switch is flipped — the discipline `fact_fetch` was built under. The select pass runs through the reflect seam thinking-off and greedy like every other judgement pass (`make_generate_fn` adapts the seam to the library's `generate_fn(system, user, *, thinking, max_new_tokens, temperature)`), its prompt is `prompts/assoc_fetch_prompt.txt` (default-written from the library's own `select_prompt.txt`) and is handed to the library on the live path too, so an edit in the Modules tab is what a live turn runs. Policy: `person:_self` claims withheld from the catalogue (the §10 rule, enforced in the library's `Policy`), `max_picks` from config. The **aha** (`run_aha_blocking`, idle job `assoc_aha`, hourly on the default idle window; 2026-09-10): ASSOCIATIVE_MEMORY.md §4 on the reach-out surface. `sync_needs` registers every open `[ask]` from `ReflectionMemory` as a library need (keyed by the ask's `content_key` in `data/assoc/state/ava_needs.json`; an ask that resolved or was evicted closes its need), the documents the feed brought in since the last wake are the stimulus (`state/aha_stimulus.json`, pushed by the feed; the newest chats with a protocol when nothing was fed) — one conversation per `aha()` call, its named entities weighted 3× its passages, `person:_self` and the user's own node carrying no weight (hubs on every chat), `Library.aha()` is the arithmetic and `Library.judge()` the thinking-on pass through the reflect seam (one judgement per wake by default — minutes at 4.5 tok/s), and a `connect`/`satisfies` verdict is composed into an opener (`prompts/assoc_aha_prompt.txt`, default-written) and written EXACTLY as an outreach reach-out is: outreach's own `_write_outreach_session` (injected) with `ask_kind:"aha"`, the shared `reachout_gate` cool-down + backoff, `write_surface` on the ask, a first-person worklog episode that opens a thread, a touch on the resource. A truncated or reasoning-leaking opener is refused like outreach's. A cheap bail (no needs, nothing fed, no candidate) retries next poll; a judgement spends the interval. `assoc.aha: false` turns it off. The library side grew registered-need support for this (`aha.need_seeds`/`find_candidates`/`fill_candidate` take `registered`; `Library._registered_info` anchors a registered need by what its text retrieves at tier 2). The **witness** (`run_witness_blocking`, idle job `assoc_witness`, hourly on the default idle window; 2026-09-10): the library's OWN chat witness (`assoc/prompts/witness_chat.txt`, rewritten for this — third person with names resolved, `the assistant` for `Me`, the assistant's turns recorded only for what they establish, `(entities:)` restricted to named things, inline `(rel: predicate(A, B))` from the closed predicate list) over her transcripts, newest first, `assoc.witness_per_wake` per wake (~3.5 min per chat at 4.5 tok/s, thinking off), REPLACING the `chat_facts` protocol the feed imported (`Library.pending(include_imported=True)`); once a document carries the library's own protocol the feed never imports Ava's over it again (`kept_own`). A group the base model's safety reflex REFUSES (thinking off, a transcript about sex or violence — parses to zero facts like an empty group) is retried once with thinking on and, still refused, reported + backfilled from the document's previous protocol for exactly those chunks (`assoc/refusal.py`, shared with the relation pass, which leaves a refused batch uncached; 2026-09-12). A refused document is re-queued ONCE PER NEW ADAPTER (`pending(current_model=…)`; the protocol's `model_id` is the loaded adapter's name via `witness_model_id`, no longer the constant `"ava"`): the reflex is in the weights, so a prompt edit is not a retry. `chat_facts` keeps running on Ava's side — the facts tree reads it. Measured on three real chats against the imported prompt: abstract entities (`warmth of hands`, `smell of tobacco`) gone, first-person copies of the assistant's prose gone, `asked_about` relations appearing, 25–30% fewer output tokens. Skips without spending its interval when no model is loaded. The **relation pass** rides the feed's rebuild (`assoc.relations`, `assoc.relations_per_wake` claims per wake, newest documents first, cached per claim in the library's `state/relations_cache.json`; only when a model is loaded) — it is what raises `asked_about`/`looking_for`/`wants` needs from claims that carry no inline relation, i.e. the imported ones. The **pivot** (`run_pivot_blocking`, idle job `assoc_pivot`, hourly on the default idle window; 2026-09-10): ASSOCIATIVE_MEMORY.md §6 in its `wander` mode — from the newest conversation with a protocol, `Library.pivot` finds a word in play with another sense on record (or, under `assoc.pivot_bridges`, a word of the same root or one that sounds like it: `инструмент → instruments`, `reach → beach`), the far side is rendered through the library's budgeted renderer, and one thinking-on pass (`prompts/assoc_pivot_prompt.txt`, default-written) decides `raise` (an opener written as an outreach with `ask_kind:"pivot"`, behind the shared reach-out gate) / `keep` (a first-person `pivot` worklog episode — the first site where the worklog's summary is written by a generation) / `nothing`, the honest default. Never an access: the far side is touched only when a message goes out. Measured on the real library: sense anchors are as often topics as senses (the M4 caveat — `reach` splits Iran/war from conversation), the genuine ones are there (`выход`: exit vs a release date; `режим`: regime vs a game mode), and the sound bridge needed two rules the corpus forced — never a word's own inflection, never a same-stem pair (that is the root bridge's) — plus `since`/`самый` on the stoplists. `assoc_pivot` is also a workbench module (writes nothing; shows every jump offered). Never imports `server`; paths + the config loader via `configure(...)`. GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.assoc_bridge` (hash embedder, temp dirs).
- **`server/inference/core/deliberation.py`** — The **deliberation executive** — the read side of the worklog: Ava reads her recent episodic worklog (`worklog.recent(N)`) + open threads (`worklog.open_threads()`) and chooses what to do next among the DRIVES her autonomous subsystems already expose — `wander` (til_wander) / `synthesize` (synthesis) / `reach_out` (outreach) / `check_in` (checkin) / `revisit` (a revisit reflection run) / `aha` / `pivot` (assoc_bridge) / `nothing` (rest). `run_deliberation_blocking` (GPU executor thread) renders the recent history + loose threads as context, injects the current persona portrait + temporal anchor, runs one reflect-generate decision pass (`deliberation_prompt.txt`, thinking on, RAG off) and parses `ACTION:`/`WHY:` over the post-CoT answer (robust to the gemma-channel shapes — reuses the `_answer_after_think` hardening). **Since 2026-09-14 it DISPATCHES:** with `execute=True` the chosen action's handler runs right there, in the same GPU slot, and its summary rides the result as `outcome`. The handlers are a `dispatch` map injected by `server.main()` — **the drives' own bodies, defined once** (`_drives` in `main()`) and handed to both their IdleJob and this map, so "wander" means one thing whether a timer or her decision triggered it; an action with no handler is reported (`dispatch_skipped: no_handler`), never guessed, and a raising one carries its error. `revisit` is the exception: a reflection run is a coroutine that dispatches to this same executor, so `_run_revisit` schedules `handle_start_reflection_run(None, …)` onto the loop (ws=None — `_send` tolerates a headless run) and returns `{scheduled}`; the run then holds `_reflection_run_active` for its duration. Wander re-checks its token budget (the IdleJob's `ready` gate has no executive counterpart). **Registered as the `deliberation` idle job under `server_config.json` → `deliberation.mode`** (`settings()`): `sole` (**default since 2026-09-17**) registers no timer for `CHOSEN_JOBS` (outreach/synthesis/checkin/wander/assoc_aha/assoc_pivot) so she is the only chooser — the upkeep jobs (feed, witness, graph rebuild, worklog sweep, background reflection) are maintenance, not choices, and keep their clocks; `shadow` (the mode it shipped in) runs the executive BESIDE the per-drive timers, so what she chooses can be compared in the Activity tab against what the timers do; `off` leaves only the Worklog tab's button. `interval_s` (3600), `recent` (20) and `execute` (true; false = decide + journal, run nothing) beside it; read per wake. `consumed`: a decision pass spends the interval whatever she chose (rest included), a no-model/no-history bail retries. `describe` journals the choice + her WHY + the outcome. **Her context also carries the last six executing dispatches and what each came to** (2026-09-20, `_recent_dispatches` / `_render_recent_dispatches`, process-local): a drive that ran and produced nothing records no worklog episode, so the next deliberation had no evidence it failed and chose it again — observed as every hour spent on an outreach that could not be decided. Shown to her, never folded into the worklog. **And since 2026-09-21 the context is dated and says where things stand:** every worklog line carries how long ago it happened (the worklog moves only when an episode completes, so after a rest the same newest line was there again an hour later, undated, and "I just reached out — let the silence work" was as true at hour nine as at hour one — observed as wander stopping for good under `sole` mode with budget unspent, every option reasoned as redundant with, or waiting on, the last reach-out); a process-local **rest streak** (`_rest_streak`, `_render_rest_streak`) says how many times in a row she has rested and since when, a rest being the one decision nothing else records; and injected `note_fns` (`configure(note_fns=…)`, rendered as *Where things stand*) carry a drive's precondition to her instead of checking it silently at dispatch — `server.main()` wires the wander budget (`_wander_budget_note`). The prompt separates the two options that send nothing to anyone (wander, revisit) from those that may end in a message, and says silence owed to a person is silence toward THEM, not idleness. The decision itself is NOT a worklog entry — a dispatched drive records its own episode, and an hourly "I decided to rest" would fill the window the next deliberation reads. Manual via `handle_deliberate_now` (WebSocket `deliberate_now`, the Worklog tab's **Deliberate** button — dry run unless the message says `execute: true`; streams `deliberation_stage`/`deliberation_chunk` → `deliberation_done`, which names the box's `mode`). Owns `_deliberation_active` (named in the scheduler's `external_busy`). Never imports `server` nor any drive: RAG accessor, reflect-generate factory, prompts dir, config loader, the dispatch map and the manual-trigger plumbing are injected via `configure(...)`. GPU-free self-test (parsing, mode knob, dispatch seam with stubs): `python -m core.deliberation`. Future agentic actions (e.g. "search chat history — did we talk about X?") slot into `_ACTIONS` + `_WOULD_DO` + the dispatch map with the loop unchanged.
- **`server/inference/core/modules.py`** — The **module workbench**: run ONE pass against ONE chat and return what it produced, **writing nothing**. It exists because every generation on this box is the same shape — *something is injected into the prompt, something is read, something is produced* — while each pass hardcodes its own answer to all three inside the run it belongs to, so nobody can say what a given pass sees without reading its call site and nobody can try a change to one without triggering the whole run. A `ModuleSpec` (frozen dataclass: `prompt_file`, `max_new_tokens`, `build`, `finish`, `injectables`, `stop_on_repeat`, `degen_stop`) is that shape as data, and `MODULES` is the registry — one dict, so growing it is a data edit. **`build` returns a LIST of content blocks** and **`finish` turns the raw generations into the displayed result**, because how the material is cut up and how the output is rendered are per-pass properties: `chat_facts` reads the whole session as one block and emits records, while `chat_summary` runs once per *consolidation chunk* and emits prose. `finish` returns the display `lines`, so the client prints them verbatim and adding a module needs no client change — the property that makes the registry worth having (it replaced a facts-shaped `parse`/`summarize` pair the moment the second module arrived). `run_module_blocking` loads the chosen transcript (path-guarded to the chats dir), builds the reading content via `reflection_source.build_session_reading_content` at the **reflect** window, generates through the shared reflect seam, parses, and **returns** the records. That return is the load-bearing part: the production pass writes its sidecar inside itself (`reflection_runner._run_chat_facts_pass_for_session`), which is exactly why it can be neither experimented with nor chained — here the sink is DETACHED, so a facts simulation never touches `<stem>.facts.json`. The result also separates a generation **cut inside its `<think>`** (`reasoning_text.truncated_before_answer` — no answer region at all, so an empty parse means the pass FAILED) from a chat that genuinely established nothing, a distinction the operator must not have to infer from a bare zero. **Scope so far:** two modules — `chat_facts` (per-chat extraction protocol; its closing question + budget copied verbatim from the production pass so a simulation is faithful, asserted by the self-test) and `chat_summary` (the consolidation gist; reproduces the production pass's per-consolidation-chunk loop and gist sanitation rather than approximating it with the whole-session block, and REPORTS a part the sanitizer rejected instead of dropping it silently as production does). `chat_summary` carries ONE stated divergence: its chunks are built with an empty open-questions block, since production packs live open `[ask]` items into that content and this module declares that it injects nothing — declaring the open-questions block a real injectable is the honest fix. Its **closing** (`reflection_chunking.SUMMARY_CLOSING`, 2026-08-08 — the line that stops the pass answering the chat's last message instead of recapping it) is shared text applied at two points: baked into the chunks here so the packing budget counts it, appended at the production call site because those chunks are shared with consolidation. Input bound to one chat, and **nothing injected** (`disable_rag=True`, no persona, no clock). `injectables` is empty, and empty means **refused, not ignored**: a non-empty `inject` returns `unsupported_injectable`, because an operator reading output as evidence about an injection that never happened is worse than an error. **`default_prompt` on the spec** (2026-09-10) supplies the prompt when the file is missing and writes it, so a fresh box shows and runs the real prompt instead of refusing with `no_prompt` — the first `assoc_fetch` simulation on the training box did exactly that, the file being default-written by the live path only. **`stop_on_repeat` lives on the spec** because whether the verbatim loop guard is correct is a property of the OUTPUT SHAPE, not of the call site: a pass emitting prose wants it, a pass emitting a fixed-template list is killed by it. `chat_facts` sets it **False** — its `[fact] (about: NAME) (class: CLASS)` prefix tokenizes past the guard's 12-token window, so the window sits wholly inside the prefix and is identical on every line sharing an (about, class) pair, firing on the 4th consecutive such fact (a single-topic chat cannot get past 3). Since 2026-08-18 the facts modules set **`degen_stop=False`** too: the diversity guard was believed to separate cleanly from this and was caught live halting the TIL twin — a run of template lines sharing an (about, class) prefix craters the rolling distinct-token ratio exactly as a real collapse does, whether emitted in the answer or drafted inside the think — so both guards are off for the protocol shape and the token cap bounds a genuine runaway. The result reports `stopped_on_loop` separately from `truncated`, since a guard halt reads as a truncation and would otherwise be diagnosed as an exhausted budget. **No longer manual-only as of 2026-08-08:** the Modules tab is still the only *interactive* trigger, but `background_reflection`'s sidecar backfill calls `run_module_blocking` on the idle path and writes what it returns — the same specs, two callers, one deciding to persist and one not, which is the detached sink finally doing work. Owns `_module_run_active` (named in the scheduler's `external_busy`, so a workbench run and the idle jobs exclude each other both ways). Never imports `server`: RAG accessor, reflect-generate factory, prompts/chats dirs and the manual-trigger plumbing are injected via `configure(...)`. GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.modules`. `til_facts` is the one module with **no** duplication of its production twin: both it and `til_wander.run_facts_pass` go through `til_facts.build_reading_blocks`/`parse_facts`, so the simulation and the real pass share their block builder and parser outright rather than mirroring each other (the self-test asserts the prompt file, budget and `stop_on_repeat` still agree). **Known duplication:** `chat_facts` is a SECOND definition of the chat-facts pass beside the runner's — temporary, and the direction is the runner becoming a caller of the registry, not the registry accumulating a copy of every pass. Not yet built: injectables, saved `(module, input, expected)` cases, chaining, and any sink *inside* the registry (the backfill's is its own, which is the intended shape). See `documentation/AVA_OPEN_PROBLEMS.md → Prompt Composition`.
- **`server/inference/core/worklog.py`** — The **first-person episodic worklog**: Ava's durable, semantic record of what she *did*, one entry per meaningful episode in her own voice ("I reached out to Artemy about the worklog idea") — deliberately distinct from `activity_log` (that is lossy machine-phrased telemetry in a ring; this is keep-forever autobiographical memory). Each entry INDEXES the scattered traces (`refs`: chat stem / wander title / ask key / run id) rather than copying them, and carries a light op-log fold — an episode may `opens` a loop a later one `closes` by id, folded by `open_threads()` (the "what's still hanging" state a future planner needs, mirroring `reflection_memory.open_questions`). A **leaf sink** like `reachout_gate`/`activity_log`: imports nothing from the project, imported directly (`from core import worklog; worklog.record(kind, summary, refs=…, opens=…, closes=…)`), no `configure`-injection beyond a one-time `configure(path)`, no cycle. It **never generates** — the first-person `summary` is supplied by the caller (templated today; a real generation at chosen sites later), keeping the module pure. Durable id high-water recovered from the file tail on `configure`; loads the WHOLE file (memory, not a ring). **Record sites wired now** at episode-close: `outreach` (compose + the self-answered `resolved` case), `synthesis` compose, `checkin` compose, `til_wander` autonomous-wander finish, `reflection_service` normal/revisit run completion (one aggregate "I reflected on N" entry per successful run — dry runs excluded), and — because a live user transcript has no clean close — **both** chat-freeze paths via `core/chat_worklog.py` (see its entry below): `background_reflection`'s per-chat `chat_reflected` freeze AND `reflection_runner`'s foreground `reflected_at` freeze each record one first-person `conversation` entry per user conversation as it is processed, closing the reach-out thread that opened it. **Its consumer is the deliberation executive** (`core/deliberation.py`, 2026-09-14): it reads `recent()`/`open_threads()` to choose what she does next; the Worklog tab previews the same log. Served via `get_worklog` → `worklog_batch`. Storage `data/hot/worklog/worklog.jsonl`. GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.worklog`.
- **`server/inference/core/chat_worklog.py`** — The thin layer above the `worklog` leaf holding the two record sites that key on a **chat** rather than on a subsystem's own episode, so both live in ONE place instead of inside whichever subsystem needed them first. `record_conversation(filename, session, gist)` writes the first-person `conversation` entry for a reflected user conversation and **closes** the reach-out thread that opened it (matched by `refs.session`), reusing the per-chat consolidation gist as the "about X" (sanitized via `summarize_gist` — moved here from `background_reflection._summarize_gist`, still layered over `chat_sidecar.sanitize_gist`, so a degenerate/structured summary such as a raw `## RAG`/`[fact]` block or `<eos>` is rejected in favour of the plain template and reflection format never leaks into the line). It **includes an Ava-initiated chat the user replied to** — outreach/synthesis/check-in logged only the opener, so the ensuing exchange is a real conversation; only a still-unanswered opener and `interlocutor:"ai"` transcripts (encounters/gossip) are skipped. **Why it exists:** this was private to `background_reflection`, which meant the only path that ever closed a thread was the background per-chat pass. On a box reflecting through operator Sleep runs the close therefore never fired (`reflection_runner` held no worklog reference at all, and `reflection_service` records only the aggregate run entry with no `closes`), so `open_threads()` grew without bound and the "what have I left unfinished" state a deliberation pass reads was pure noise. Both freeze paths now call it. `expire_stale_reachouts(chats_dir, max_age_hours=DEFAULT_STALE_HOURS)` covers the other half: an Ava-initiated chat the user never answered is deliberately skipped **un-frozen** by both reflection paths (a later reply must still make it reflectable), so it can never reach a close site even in principle. Three cases are written off — an unanswered opener past the window (48 h), a thread whose session is gone from disk (**at any age**, ahead of the window — 2026-09-20), and a thread whose session **was** answered but is already frozen (the pre-fix backlog: reflection has been and gone, so `record_conversation` will never fire for it) — while an answered-but-unfrozen session is left alone, since that close belongs to reflection. It **records a real closing episode** rather than filtering at read time, so the fold stays an honest op-log and "she was ignored" survives as information; the entry takes the *opener's* kind (outreach/synthesis/checkin), being the tail of that episode. `close_removed_session(filename, who, unanswered=…)` (2026-09-20) is the third record site and the one the USER drives: `session_ops.handle_delete_session` calls it at the point of effect, because deleting an Ava-initiated chat from the Chat tab is how someone declines *that* conversation — and until then the thread stayed open for the sweep's whole 48 h window, so the deliberation executive kept waiting on a chat that no longer existed instead of starting a new one. The entry names who took it down and that it went unanswered (information for the next decision, not silence — no reach-out tombstone, no backoff); the sweep's missing-session case now calls the same function, as the catch-up for a removal the handler did not see. `delete_stale_reachouts(chats_dir, max_age_hours, exclude)` (2026-08-08) takes that same judgement to the **corpus**: once an opener is written off, the transcript goes too (with every file its stem owns, via `chat_sidecar.chat_products`). It is the one session shape that never becomes anything — reflection skips it un-frozen forever, so it is permanent backlog, a dead entry in the chat list, and a chat-RAG passage where her own unanswered message can be retrieved as though it were dialogue. Run at the start of a reflection (`reflection_service._run_stale_reachout_sweep`), where the corpus's periodic tidying lives, **and at the head of every background per-chat wake** (rung 0, through the shared `reflection_service.run_stale_reachout_sweep`) — so a box nobody launches Sleep runs on still clears them. **Three on-disk consumers read exactly these files**, and a plain deletion breaks all three in the same direction — the openers old enough to delete are the *longest*-ignored, so each guard would weaken as the silence lengthened, which is the inversion of its rule: `reachout_gate.unanswered_streak()` (the backoff window would shrink), `checkin._standing_openers()` (the "what have I already said into this silence" list would blank every 48 h — the 15-cold-opens bug), and `outreach._has_dangling_opener()` (explicitly *not* time-limited, and would become "until the sweep runs"). So the sweep **tombstones** each opener — stem, addressee, send time, and her message text — via `reachout_gate.record_expired` BEFORE unlinking, and all three consumers fold the tombstones in; a tombstone that cannot be written **aborts that deletion**, since losing the evidence of a reach-out is worse than keeping a stale transcript. What is deleted is the transcript (the thing that was never a conversation); what survives is that she said it and was not answered. `exclude` carries the live logger's file, exempt whatever its age (an opener adopted in place is a conversation being answered right now). Each deletion records its own first-person worklog episode and closes the reach-out thread. Guarded on a non-empty chats dir, so a mis-wired path or a mid-wipe box can never mass-close the fold or mass-delete. `expire_stale_reachouts` is driven by the GPU-free `worklog_sweep` idle job (`server.main()`, `interval_s=3600`, `idle_seconds=300` — waiting a full idle hour would starve it on exactly the busy box where threads pile up; a sweep that finds nothing stale returns `skipped` so the Activity journal dedups it). GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.chat_worklog`.
- **`server/inference/core/activity_log.py`** — The **unified activity journal**: the single, box-wide "what is Ava doing right now" log every autonomous/GPU subsystem writes to, so the UI is never silent while the box works (the fix for "an idle job is clearly running on the GPU but no tab shows anything," and for the silence after a reflection run ends and the box moves on to autonomous work). A **leaf sink** like `reachout_gate` — imports nothing from the project, imported directly (no `configure`-injection beyond a one-time `configure(path)` at startup), so any subsystem can `from core import activity_log; activity_log.append(...)` with no wiring and no import cycle. ONE global monotonic **restart-durable `seq`** across all sources (recovered from the file tail on `configure`), append-only to `data/hot/activity/activity.jsonl` (in-memory ring + size-gated rotation), so a client tracks a single cursor and gets everything interleaved. `current()` returns the open (started-but-not-finished) activity for a live status chip. `set_current()`/`clear_current()` manage that chip WITHOUT writing a journal line (a job that fires hourly and finds nothing to do would otherwise flood the log with started→skip pairs); the journal gets only meaningful lines. **Writers:** (1) `idle_scheduler._dispatch` opens the chip, runs the job, emits ONE terminal line (`finished`/`skipped`/`failed`) phrased by the job's optional `IdleJob.describe` (registered next to the job so the scheduler stays semantics-free), deduping consecutive identical skips — so every idle job is visible for free; (2) each subsystem's existing `on_stage`/`on_question` phase hooks are routed here on the autonomous path (never the raw token `on_chunk` stream — flood), so outreach/checkin/synthesis narrate their reasoning; (3) `reflection_config.ReflectionRunStore.append_event` mirrors reflection events at **Sleep-tab detail for EVERY run** (`_mirror_to_activity`, ONE grain since 2026-07-28 — it was previously coarse phase markers for an operator run "so as not to duplicate the Sleep tab", which left anything an operator wanted to *watch* invisible in the one always-live view): one whitelist (`_ACTIVITY_MIRROR_EVENTS` — phases/sessions plus branch outcomes, ask resolutions, and BOTH `pass_error` and `pass_warning`), chat name + exchange counters on every line, and the pass's actual reflection output indented under the `phase_done` line — revision's raw VERDICT-WHY-IDEAL text, the user-notes pass's raw generation (thinking included — a pass whose whole verdict is formed in the `<think>` would otherwise mirror as a bare "nothing new about X", since its `phase_progress` deltas are never mirrored), the anchor pass's ABOUT + tags, and consolidation's parsed distilled items (`_render_report_items`: `[fact]`/`[ask:*]`/`[resolved]` lines, since its text streams as never-mirrored `phase_progress` deltas) — clipped at `_MIRROR_TEXT_CAP` (4000 chars). A **`pass_warning`/`pass_error`** carrying `text` renders the same indented body (2026-07-31): the anchor, user-notes, portrait-synthesis and recollection passes each attach their raw generation when the parse comes back empty, since "unparseable or empty (skipped)" alone can't distinguish a never-closed `<think>` from a label-less output. The raw token stream is never mirrored. Those two bounds are what make one grain affordable; a long operator run can still evict older entries from the 2000-event ring (the on-disk journal keeps them), and the client's **Hide reflection detail** box collapses the bodies back to headlines. Served to the client via `activity_events` → `activity_events_batch` (see the protocol table). GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.activity_log`. **Four levels since 2026-08-11** (`level` on each record, plus a `text` field kept separate from the one-line `message` so a body never has to be clipped to keep a headline readable): `event` (the lifecycle/phase markers this journal originally held), **`body`** (ONE finished generation — the pass's verbatim CoT *and* output, with its outcome flags), **`stream`** (a heartbeat every `heartbeat_s`≈20 s carrying elapsed, tokens so far and a rolling tail of what is being written — the fix for a pass holding the GPU for 21 minutes in silence, since `set_current` deliberately writes no line), and **`raw`** (one stdout/stderr line from any process). **Coverage comes from two insertion points, not from per-subsystem wiring:** `begin_pass`/`pass_tick`/`end_pass` are called by `generation._make_sync_reflect_generate` and `_make_agentic_generate` — the two functions EVERY background generation on the box passes through — so reflection, TIL/wander, outreach, check-in, synthesis, deliberation, modules and the clean-base evaluations all report what they produced with no call-site change; and `install_stdout_tee()` converts the codebase's universal `[tag] …` print convention into journal lines (parsing the tag as the `source`), which is also the only way to capture a foreign library's output. **Per-token deltas are deliberately never journalled** — ~100k tokens per reflection run is ~25k records at the generator's batching, which would evict everything else from the ring every run; the heartbeat plus the final body carries the same information for ~1% of the records. Labels come from `pass_context` (explicit) or `set_ambient_label` (ambient, per thread) — the reflection runner sets it from each `phase_started` so every body reads `revision`/`chat_facts`/`anchor`, and `idle_scheduler._labelled_run` RESETS it at each job boundary so a finer label can never leak onto the next job's passes. That reset **must run on the generating thread** and therefore lives inside the executor call, not beside `set_current` in the `_dispatch` coroutine: the label is thread-local and sticky, the box has ONE executor thread, and a job body that names its own pass (`til_wander`'s `til_facts:<kind>`, `modules`' `module:<name>`) sets it from *there* — so until 2026-08-13 the reset landed on the asyncio thread, which never generates, and every later job reported under the previous one's label (live: outreach and check-in passes journalled as `til_facts:wander`). A live-chat pass on the same thread names itself via `pass_context` (`generation._fetch_facts_block_sync` → `fact_fetch`) rather than inheriting whatever an idle job left. The **offline train cycle installs the same tee** from its own process (`train_cycle` already puts `inference/` on `sys.path`), so unsloth's output is in the same journal — durable across the relaunch, and live via `train_progress.jsonl`, which the tee mirrors into because the watchdog is the only server reachable while inference is down. Cross-process appends are `flock`-guarded; `seq` uniqueness rests on the watchdog never running two writers at once. Rotation writes **numbered segments** (`activity.<seq>.jsonl`, `retain_segments` kept) instead of the former rewrite-down-to-the-ring, which silently discarded history at 8 MB; a cursor past the served window is reported as `gap` rather than rendered as continuity; `configure()` reads only the file's tail. **The public API contributes nothing, by construction** — its generate path (`_make_openai_generate`) has no hook, which is what keeps "requests are NEVER logged" structural. Tunable via `server_config.json` `logging`. Excluded from runnable snapshots (telemetry about the source box, not state Ava depends on). Design brief: `LOGGING.md`.
- **`server/inference/core/reachout_gate.py`** — The shared neutral **gate** on Ava's unprompted reach-outs, answering ONE question via `may_reach_out() -> (allowed, reason)`: *may she cold-open the user right now?* `outreach`/`synthesis`/`checkin` each write a reversed `initiated_by:"ava"` session and, being independent idle jobs, may come due together; this is the single gate they all honor. It folds **two** limits. **(1) A cool-down** — at most one unprompted message per window regardless of origin (`COOLDOWN_SECONDS` = 3600). **(2) Backoff on unanswered openers** — the window *doubles* for every message she has sent since the user last spoke and had no reply to (`unanswered_streak`), capped at `MAX_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` (24 h, reached at a streak of 5: 1→2→4→8→16→24). (2) exists because a flat cool-down equal to the jobs' own re-arm interval gates nothing: on 2026-07-30/31 check-in cold-opened the user **15 times in 19 hours**, each an unanswered restatement of the same thought, every send within the rules. It is the generalization of `outreach._has_dangling_opener` (which stops re-raising ONE ask she awaits an answer on) to the two jobs that compose free-form and so have no ask to key a guard to. Backoff, not a hard stop — being ignored should make her quieter, not mute. It gates the **message, not the job**: each blocking pass runs to completion (analysis, pool-filling, its own interval consumed) and checks only immediately before writing a session, stamping `mark_reachout()` once it does — a loser returns a `reachout_cooldown` (a sibling just sent) or `reachout_backoff` (she is talking into silence) skip, both of which the `consumed` classifiers in `server.py` count as spent (else a day-long hold re-runs the decision generation every 5-min poll). It was once an idle-job `ready` gate, which coupled the jobs and starved check-in outright. **Durable, not process-local**: the streak and the last-send time are read from the chat corpus on disk, because a `time.monotonic()` stamp — fine for a 1 h burst guard — would let a restart zero a 24 h backoff and resume the burst; the in-process stamp stays as a floor so a just-written session counts before its file is read back. Reading the corpus has one consequence: **deleting a transcript would erase the evidence of a reach-out**, so since `chat_worklog.delete_stale_reachouts` began removing openers that go unanswered past 48 h, the deleter tombstones each one here first (`record_expired` → `data/hot/reachout/expired.jsonl`, wired via `configure(chats_dir, expired_log=…)`) and `unanswered_streak`/`seconds_since_reachout` fold those tombstones in beside the live files, deduped by stem and under the same "sent after the user's last turn" cutoff. `expired_openers(user)` exports them (with her message text) for `checkin._standing_openers` and `outreach._has_dangling_opener`, the two other guards that would otherwise weaken exactly as the silence lengthened. No log configured ⇒ no tombstones and no deletions (`delete_stale_reachouts` refuses), i.e. the pre-2026-08-08 behaviour. An opener is timed by its **filename stem, not mtime** (`ChatLogger` rewrites per turn, so an answered opener's mtime is the *reply*), and one sent *before* the user's last turn doesn't count against her — they did engage, just elsewhere. **Global or per person:** every entry point takes an optional `user`. Unscoped (what `outreach`/`synthesis` pass) it asks the original box-wide question; scoped it asks the same question of that person's thread alone — their openers, their last turn, their streak, their in-process stamp. `checkin` scopes, because it decides per person: a message to one person is no reason to withhold one from another, and one person ignoring her is not evidence that a second is. Strictly a *narrowing* — unscoped callers still see every send whoever it went to, and `mark_reachout` always moves the global stamp as well as the addressee's. A **leaf** like `activity_log`/`worklog`: imports nothing from the project (so the small "is this a real user turn?" predicate is deliberately duplicated from `checkin`, as is `user_key`, this module's copy of `reflection_writer.normalize_person` — **exported**, so `checkin` scopes by the identical rule rather than a second implementation that could disagree about who is who), wired once via `configure(chats_dir)` in `server.main()`. GPU-free self-test: `python -m core.reachout_gate`.
- **`server/inference/core/reflection_runner.py`** — `ReflectionRunner`: server-owned, synchronous consolidation→revision→branch loop, executed entirely server-side (dispatched via `run_in_executor`). Loads sessions, builds context-aware consolidation chunks (`reflection_chunking.py`) and replay-faithful revision context (`reflection_source.py`), generates each pass, parses + persists via `ReflectionWriter` and the chat sidecars, and emits progress events. **Consolidation packing is final-prompt-aware:** it reserves generation headroom, measures the exact tokenizer/chat-template prompt (Sleep prompt + bounded reflection-memory RAG + capped open questions + historical session prompt + transcript), reuses that prepared prompt for generation, makes boundary overlap conditional, and losslessly fragments an individually oversized exchange; historical chat-RAG is excluded as redundant, and deterministic budget overflow is never retried unchanged. Synthesis shares the exact-fit chunker; revision remains per-exchange. Branching is a core part of revision and always runs when the branch-generation capability is wired in — both the WebSocket server and the headless `reflection_run.py` CLI provide it (via the shared `core/branch_replay.py`). Run config/state/overrides live in `reflection_config.py`; prompts load via `reflection_prompts.py`. After all sessions finish it runs the **persona-digest** pass (`reflection_digest.py`), then a **batched phase-two clean-base pass** (one `agentic.CleanBaseSession` swap per run via the `clean_base_ctx` callback) that runs two evaluations inside that single reload: the **branch judge** (logged/flip, emits `branch_judged` events) and **fact placement** (`_run_clean_base_fact_placement` — assigns each unhosted `[fact]` a host exchange **within its own chat** (data locality, REBUILD §3) whose reasoning rests on it, snapshotting `source_exchange` onto the anchor so `train_cycle` can inject it into that CoT as an "I know that …" line; emits `fact_placed` events); persona clustering; and — since 2026-08-04 — **fact dedup** (`_run_clean_base_fact_dedup` → `core/fact_dedup.py`: collapse paraphrases in the live `[fact]` store, the fact-side counterpart of persona clustering, since a fact is deduped at write time by EXACT content key only and restatements of one truth therefore accumulate indefinitely — a live corpus reached 10+ copies of one evening, which is how three same-topic lines came to fill a chat block). It runs **last** in the batch, which is what lets it see the facts *this* run distilled (the fold reads staging over live), and writes through the runner's own `ReflectionWriter` — i.e. into **staging**, promoted by `merge-rag` and discarded with a discarded run, unlike the manual handler which writes straight to live. It is the one batch task depending on nothing the run produced, so it can carry the batch alone. It is handed `execute_run`'s `embed_fn` (wired from `reflection_service._make_embed_fn` — the live RAG embedder, CPU, so the clean-base swap does not affect it) because the pass **blocks the store by subject**, without which it is near-blind (see `core/fact_dedup.py`), and it reports one `fact_dedup_progress` event per block — its own event rather than `phase_progress`, since anything sent on that one suppresses the `phase_done` body, which here is the keep/drop report. Opt out with `overrides.fact_dedup=False`. The same runner backs the headless `server/reflection_run.py` CLI and the staged sandbox (`reflection_staging.py`); the CLI omits `clean_base_ctx`, so all the clean-base passes are simply skipped there. Before that batch — GPU-free, so outside the clean-base window, and deliberately AHEAD of dedup, which unions the triggers of what it merges and so would fuse a broken cue into a survivor and spread it — it runs **`_run_trigger_purge`** (`core/trigger_hygiene.py`): strip pasted-sentence recall cues out of the live trigger-indexed store, writing through the runner's own `ReflectionWriter` (staging, promoted by `merge-rag`) like the dedup beside it. Opt out with `overrides.trigger_purge=False`. **`_run_user_notes_pass_for_session`** (2026-07-31) is the per-session "what did I learn about this PERSON?" pass — the user-side counterpart of the revision pass's persona formation, writing `[impression]` records that fold into that person's standing portrait (`core/user_digest.py`). It is its own pass rather than a consolidation section because consolidation is *chunked* (a person-level reading written per chunk would form from a fragment and be emitted several times per chat) and because the framing differs: it wants the notice-don't-diagnose discipline and the "writing nothing is correct" permission. Runs after revision (a failure must not touch the trainable target), once per session, on revisits too (re-reading an old chat as who she is now is a genuinely new reading of the person; the suppression a revisit applies is about not letting an obsolete chat reshape *her own* identity). Skipped for an `interlocutor:"ai"` transcript or an unnameable speaker. Fenced (`before_session=filename`) **and** run with `rag_include_impressions=False` — shown her prior readings she restates them, and recurrence across distinct sessions (the portrait's only ranking signal) would then measure the prompt rather than independent noticing: the circular self-vote `reflection_digest._TENURE_DECAY` discounts after the fact, prevented here instead. Opt out with `overrides.user_notes=False`. **`_run_self_notes_pass_for_session`** (2026-08-06) is its OUTSIDE-view sibling, run on the same session right after it: what the transcript shows about HER to a reader with no access to her `<think>` (the builder strips it), written as `[self_impression]` and folded by `core/self_portrait.py`. Independent evidence from the revision pass's introspective `[persona]`, and kept independent on purpose — the gap between the two readings is the point. Unlike user notes it runs on `interlocutor:"ai"` transcripts too (an encounter has nobody to portray but is still a record of how she comes across), and its run-level `_plan_self_portrait` / `_cluster_self_portrait` / `_synthesize_self_portrait` seams sit beside the user-portrait ones at the same three points. Opt out with `overrides.self_notes=False`. **`_run_chat_facts_pass_for_session`** (2026-08-06) is the third per-session reading pass and the only one that writes to no live store at all: it records the conversation's **protocol** — everything it established, enumerated literally — to that chat's own `<stem>.facts.json` (see `core/chat_facts.py`). Runs after revision like its siblings (a failure must not touch the trainable target) and on `interlocutor:"ai"` transcripts too (an encounter still establishes facts). It reads the session through `reflection_source.build_session_reading_content` with **its own reversed-session note** (`chat_facts.AVA_INITIATED_NOTE`, not the revision-written default that reads as "skip your own turns") — and that builder now **pins the opening group** on an Ava-initiated session (`format_context_block(keep_first=…)`), since the oldest-first drop is right when context leads up to a later exchange and wrong where the opener is the turn the conversation exists for. The pin applies to its sibling reading passes (user notes / self notes / recollection) too; the revision context is deliberately untouched. It is the ONE pass that generates with the **verbatim loop guard off** (`stop_on_repeat=False`, 2026-08-07): its output is a fixed-template list whose line prefix exceeds the guard's 12-token window, so the guard fired on the 4th consecutive fact sharing an (about, class) pair and returned a list cut mid-prefix — which the pass then reported as "output unusable". Layer 2 (`_DegenStop`) is off with it as of 2026-08-18 (`degen_stop=False` through the reflect seam): believed unaffected, it was caught live halting the TIL twin on the same template shape. Mirrored by `core.modules._CHAT_FACTS`, whose self-test asserts the two still agree. Opt out with `overrides.chat_facts=False`. The run-level `_plan_user_portraits` / `_cluster_user_portraits` / `_synthesize_user_portraits` seams mirror the digest's three, planning before the clean-base window, clustering inside it, synthesizing after on the adapter — and plan from **live** memory only, never this run's staging (the portrait file is written straight to live, so staged evidence a discard erases must not stand under it). **`_run_recollection_pass_for_session`** (2026-07-31) is the one **revisit-only** per-session pass: after revision and anchors it writes ONE `[recollection]` — what Ava now makes of the re-read conversation (see `reflection_writer.write_recollection`). Its retrieval is deliberately unlike every other pass here: **unfenced** (`before_session=""`), because the premise is reading an old chat with what she knows *now* and the replay-faithful cutoff would make the output indistinguishable from the original consolidation; with the past-chat block off (the transcript is already the content) and `rag_include_recollections=False`, so her previous reading of this chat cannot anchor the new one into restating it. Opt out with `overrides.recollection=False`.
- **`server/inference/core/chat_facts.py`** — Per-chat **fact-extraction protocol**: the record of everything one conversation established, enumerated literally and exhaustively — including the biographical trivia every other pass is right to discard ("wears band t-shirts to the office"). The fourth thing reflection derives from a chat, beside the per-exchange verdicts (`.state.json`), the prose recap (`.summary.json`) and the distilled memory that lands in `rag_memory.jsonl`. **It is not a second fact store, and that boundary is the design:** `rag_memory.jsonl` stays authoritative — it is what retrieval reads, what eviction acts on, what `fact_dedup` merges across chats — while this is an *immutable extraction record*: what THIS chat yielded as of the run that read it, never deduped, never evicted, never rewritten by a later merge (a fact merged from four chats belongs to no single chat's sidecar; an evicted one leaves no trace of having been said). Same source-and-fold relationship `weights_persona.jsonl` already has to the ledger. The protocol is not inserted wholesale into the reflection-memory block. The implemented facts-tree build and associative feed consume it, and their retrieval channels select source-backed claims for live prompts. Each parsed line carries `(about: NAME)` (normalized through `normalize_subject` — `reflection_writer.normalize_person` plus ONE carve-out, so a subject resolves to the same identity as a fact's `about` and a portrait's slug). **The carve-out is `SELF_SUBJECT` (`_self`), for a fact about Ava herself** (2026-08-07): `normalize_person` collapses `me`/`i`/`user`/`the user` to `""` — *a fact about nobody in particular* — and nothing in the pipeline gives her a name (`chat_prompt.txt` names no one, the reflect lane composes no identity line, and `session_transcript_turns` renders her turns as a bare `Me:` for replay fidelity), so every fact she stated about herself was filed as being about no one. Invisible on an ordinary chat, where the facts are mostly about the named user; nearly total on a conversation she started. A reserved marker rather than a configured name, because identity here accumulates rather than being declared — and the same reserved slug `user_digest.RESERVED_SLUGS` already holds for the outside-view portrait, for the same reason. The leading underscore keeps it clear of the people namespace. Only unambiguous first-person labels map (`self`/`myself`/`me`/`i`); `the user` does NOT (it normally means the human, and guessing would misfile a real person's fact), and neither does her name — recognizing one would put a hardcoded name in a codebase that has none. `subject_raw` preserves what she actually wrote, so pre-fix records are auditable. Kept LOCAL to this module: `normalize_person` is shared with the live fact store, eviction and dedup plus **`entities`** and **`when`** (2026-08-07, for the offline graph build): `entities` is the OTHER nodes the fact touches — the people, places and things it names or refers to — kept as MENTION strings rather than resolved keys, because extracting the mention needs the *conversation* ("her sister" is a node the sentence only refers to, and the pass reading the transcript knows whose sister) while RESOLVING mentions to canonical nodes needs the whole corpus at once and so belongs to the build. Formatting normalization only (`normalize_entities`: split, strip decoration, dedup case-insensitively, cap 8) with the surface form preserved — deliberately NOT `normalize_person`, which reduces to a first token and would turn "New York" into "new". `when` is an `event`'s date, resolved against the conversation's own date (see `reflection_source.session_date_line`) or kept as the phrase said; an unresolvable time is written verbatim rather than guessed. **Topic tags were considered and declined** — a topic is an interpretation, and this pass is defined as witness-not-interpreter, while a free tag vocabulary generated per chat with no view of the others fragments by language, granularity and morphology (the exact limit `exchange_anchor.normalize_tag` already documents). Topic clustering belongs to the offline build, which has the global view and the existing machinery (`fact_dedup`'s subject blocking + `persona_cluster`'s map-reduce) and can be re-run when it is wrong. Each line also carries a **`fact_class`** the live store does not draw: `standing` (true beyond the moment — a habit, possession, relationship) / `stated` (a position someone holds, true only as a record that they said it) / `event`. `source_class` already records who *said* a thing, which is a different question from what kind of thing was said, and the difference is the one a graph build needs — a standing fact is still true next month, a stated one can be contradicted by the same person next week and must never be read back as something simply known. An unmarked line is written `unspecified` rather than guessed. **Participant labels are anchored to the recorded spelling (2026-08-22)** — the pass was observed labelling one person differently per generation ("artemyvo"/"Artemy"/"Артемий", and Ava herself as "Ava"/"AI"/"user"), since the prompt asks for `(about: NAME)` without saying which name while the caller structurally knows it (session `user` + per-exchange `speaker`). Two shared definitions, used by both callers (the runner pass and `core.modules`' workbench twin, asserted by the modules self-test): `participants_note(session)` states the canonical spelling in-context (composed into the closing — never the prompt file, and not `ava_initiated_note`, which only reaches reversed sessions) and restates the self rule's two uncovered leaks (her own name, "AI"); `make_subject_fn(session)` rides `parse_facts`' `subject_fn` seam and folds any label naming a recorded participant onto that participant's canonical key, inflection-tolerantly via `exchange_anchor.words_match` (≥6-char shared prefix, so "Artemy"→"artemyvo" folds while "Artemis" and short-name near-misses structurally cannot). Cross-script variants stay the graph alias table's residue, made rare by the note; already-written protocols are untouched (`is_stale_record` correctly does not flag them). Parsing is lenient (markers in either order, a line missing one still yields a record, non-`[fact]` text ignored so a chatty generation degrades to fewer facts rather than to garbage), reads **only the answer region** (2026-08-18: the pass drafts tag-identical `[fact]` lines inside its `<think>`, so a raw-completion scan stored deliberation as protocol — near-duplicates, cap exhaustion, literal `</think>` fusions that reached live chat through the graph fold; the callers additionally refuse a generation cut inside an unclosed channel via `reasoning_text.truncated_before_answer`, the one shape no text inspection can see, and `is_stale_record` flags stored records carrying reasoning markers so pre-fix protocols re-derive through the backlogs), and drops the final line on a truncated generation — half a fact cannot be repaired by a later pass, the record being immutable. It also owns **`AVA_INITIATED_NOTE`**, this pass's reversed-session framing, which replaces `reflection_source._AVA_INITIATED_NOTE` (that one is written for the REVISION pass — it closes "when judging your reply" and calls the self-descriptions in a session she started hers "not facts about them"; every reading pass inherited it, and to a pass recording what was *said* that reads as "skip your own turns", which is what she did — dropping her own opener from the protocol). It lives here, in the pure module owning this pass's data contract, because both callers need it — the production pass and `core.modules`'s registry entry — and a second copy is a second thing to keep in step. Pure/GPU-free: the store lives on `ChatSidecar` (`read_facts`/`write_facts`), the generation in `reflection_runner._run_chat_facts_pass_for_session`. Self-test: `python -m core.chat_facts`.
- **`server/inference/core/til_facts.py`** — The **TIL lane's fact-extraction protocol**: the wander/news counterpart of `chat_facts`, and it exists for the same reason. `learning_prompt.txt` / `wander_prompt.txt` are *curation* passes — told in as many words that most of what they read is noise and that finding almost all of it irrelevant is correct — so an article is read once, a handful of `[fact]` items reach live memory, and everything else the text established is gone (measured 2026-08-09: 4 news digests kept 26 facts between them, 10 wandered articles kept 15, with duplicates inside a single digest). This is the enumerated literal layer underneath that, on the same contract as the chat protocol: **per-source extraction, separate from live-memory dedup/eviction/merge**. The facts tree and associative feed consume it; selected claims reach runtime retrieval. Re-extraction can replace the protocol, so it is not an immutable historical archive. It matters more than the chat one for a knowledge-graph build, since wander and news are the only paths by which anything about the wider WORLD enters the box. **Shares the parser** — `chat_facts.parse_facts`, which now takes a `subject_fn` — so a marker-placement or format fix lands on both lanes at once, and shares the class vocabulary, entity rules, truncation contract and staleness probe. **Two forced divergences.** (1) *Subject namespace*: `chat_facts.normalize_subject` resolves to a PERSON key via `normalize_person`, which lowercases and reduces to a first token — right for the handful of people the box knows, fatal here, where the subject is a country, an organisation, a trope or an event. It would file "New York" under `new`, "United States" under `united`, and everything unrecognised under `""` (*nobody in particular*) — invisible, because on a world-facts lane an unowned fact is the NORMAL case. So a subject here is an entity **mention**, surface form preserved, normalized only for formatting; resolving mentions to canonical nodes needs the whole corpus and belongs to the build. Identical argument to `chat_facts.normalize_entities`, applied to the subject slot. (2) *Provenance instead of attribution*: the source is a TEXT, not a speaker, so the document carries the text's identity (`source_kind`/`source_ref`/`source_url`/`source_title`/`source_date`) and fact-level `about`/`source`/`source_class` are absent. Load-bearing here, because the approved wiki list is chosen for **tone, not truth** (Lurkmore/Neolurk/WikiTropes are humour wikis), so a Current-events digest and a joke wiki asserting one sentence are not the same evidence. Deliberately **not** resolved into a per-fact confidence or authority score: judging a source is an interpretation and this pass is defined as a witness, and a score invented per line is the one field a later build could not recompute — provenance is recorded exactly and weighting by it is the build's job (same split as mention resolution). `build_reading_blocks` paragraph-packs the text into one or more blocks, because a transcript is bounded by the context its chat ran in and an article is bounded by nothing (every snippet in the current corpus fits one block at the 24k budget; the pre-wipe corpus held a 26,001-char article). **`SOURCE_KINDS` are DIRECTORY names** — `iter_snippets` resolves `<snippets>/<kind>/`, so a value that does not name a real dir silently enumerates nothing, which is what `lookup` did for a directory called `lookups`: 18 fetched articles had no protocol and no recap and never would have, and a graph-lane ref for one could not resolve back through `til_gist.resolve_source` (fixed 2026-08-13). `list_backlog` is what covers material older than the pass: unlike a chat — reflected once, soon after, by a pass that runs anyway — an article is read exactly once at fetch and never revisited, so without it the protocol would exist only for material fetched after the day it shipped. It folds in `chat_facts.needs_reparse`, so this lane self-heals on a parser fix exactly as the chat one does. It owns **`DERIVED_SUFFIXES`** — this lane's `chat_sidecar.SIDECAR_SUFFIXES`, load-bearing for the identical reason: a snippet owns its stem, `iter_snippets` is the ONE place deciding what counts as a source text, and every derived file shares that stem and ends in `.json`, so a suffix missing there gets enumerated as an article — the protocol pass then records facts about Ava's own recap and the graph build ingests them as things the world stated. Caught exactly that way when `.summary.json` arrived. Pure/GPU-free (storage helpers + parse only; the generation is `til_wander.run_facts_pass`). Self-test: `python -m core.til_facts`. **Not yet in the Modules registry** — that is chat-shaped end to end (`_load_session` path-guarded to the chats dir, `exchanges` required, `build(session, …)`), so a TIL module needs an input *kind* on `ModuleSpec` plus a source list in the client, which is why this prompt cannot yet be tuned from the workbench.
- **`server/inference/core/til_gist.py`** — **Per-source prose recap for the TIL lane** — what one article or digest *was*, as she would recall it.
