The session-level reflect-once freeze (`reflected_at`) is left as-is. Refuses while a reflection run is active; skips the active conversation file. In the same pass every **banned** (`set_training_ban`) exchange is DELETED from its transcript (`chat_logger.delete_exchange`, schema v6 — the turn kept under a session-level `deleted_exchanges` record; later exchanges shift down one index, so the sidecar's verdict + anchor maps and the ledger's fact hosts are renumbered with it) and every banned wander capture is dropped from the corpus. Replies `history_rewritten {rewritten, deleted, wander_deleted, chats, skipped, errors}`. |
| `load` / `unload` / `status` / `cancel` | Model lifecycle. `status` replies carry `loaded` and, since 2026-09-11, `loading` (the startup auto-load is in flight — the server listens before it loads); `load` is refused while `loading`. The server also pushes an unsolicited `loaded` to the connected client when the startup load lands, the same message `load` answers with, so the Chat tab unlocks without a reconnect (`ChatWidget.sync_model_state`, driven by the main window's 2 s timer). |

| Server → Client | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `chunk` | Streaming token text |
| `prompt_debug` | `{segments: [{kind, label, text}]}` — the full prompt this turn conditions on, sent once before the first `chunk` when `generate` carried `debug: true` (Chat tab's **Debug** checkbox). `kind` is `system` / `rag` / `user` / `assistant`, which the client renders as blue / green / red / muted grey. Built by `generation._prompt_debug_segments` from the SAME labelled `(kind, label, text)` parts list the system message is joined from — not a re-parse of the finished string — so a newly injected block appears here for free and the view can't drift from the prompt. Replaces the former flat `log` text dump of the same prompt. |
| `facts_block` | `{text, skipped, error, picked, n_rendered, n_candidates, sessions}` — what the **facts-tree fetch** (stage 1 of the two-stage turn) chose for THIS message, sent once before the first `chunk` so the Chat tab renders it above the reply's CoT — the operator reads the thought against the material it was given, and a premise arriving after the conclusion is something else to reconcile. **Not gated on the Debug checkbox**, unlike `prompt_debug`: this is the one injected channel whose content was *selected for* the message rather than embedded near it, so it is what the reply rests on rather than a diagnostic. `text` is the blob WITHOUT `facts_block_prompt.txt`'s wrapper (the same paragraph every turn). Sent whenever the fetch RAN — an empty result included, since a reply built on nothing is a different reply from one built on three facts, and `n_candidates` is the denominator that makes "picked nothing" readable; a box with `graph.enabled` false sends nothing at all. `skipped` separates the ordinary empty outcomes (`picked_nothing` / `no_candidates`, rendered green with the block) from the broken ones (`no_tree` / `no_prompt` / `generate_failed` …, rendered red), the same split the server prints under `[facts]`. `sessions` are the conversations those facts came out of, best-first — what the picks NOMINATED to the past-chat channel (`rag_engine._query_nominated`), rendered by the Chat tab on the header line. Reported rather than left to be inferred from the RAG block, because the engine's caps and fences mean this list is what was *offered*, not necessarily what was recalled: an operator needs to see that a fact had a conversation behind it even on a turn where the slot went unspent. Names only — the recap itself is already in the RAG block. The candidate list itself never rides this message: it is ~10k tokens. |
| `done` | `{response, input_tokens, memory, tension?}` — server-cleaned final response |
| `reflection_run_started` / `reflection_run_status` / `reflection_run_events_batch` / `reflection_run_stop_requested` / `reflection_runs_list` / `reflection_run` | Reflection-run lifecycle + buffered progress events (consolidation/revision/branch phase markers, per-pass reports, branch outcomes). |
| `activity_events_batch` | `{events, latest_seq, activity}` — the unified activity-journal batch answering `activity_events`: events with `seq > after_seq`, the current high-water `latest_seq`, and `activity` (the live open activity for the status chip, or `null` when the box is idle). | Also `gap: true` when `after_seq` predates what the server can still serve — a truncated history must be stated, never rendered as continuity. Each event may carry `level` (absent ⇒ `event`) and `text` (the body: a pass's CoT + output, or a heartbeat's tail).
| `worklog_batch` | `{entries, latest_id, open_threads}` — answers `get_worklog`: episodic worklog entries with `id > after_seq`, the current high-water `latest_id`, and the folded `open_threads` (episodes that opened a loop no later one has closed). See `core.worklog`. |
| `reflection_prompts` | `{sleep, revision, branch}` — current prompt text. |
| `branches` | `{filename, exchange_index, eligible, [reason], original, n_generated, candidates: [{text, position, token, alt_token, margin, similarity_to_original}], dropped: [same + drop_reason]}` |
| `open_questions` | `{questions: [{content, source_session}]}` — live unresolved `[ask]` items |
| `persona_updated` / `facts_updated` | `{ok, removed?, remaining?, snapshot_touched, conflict?, message?}` — outcome of an `update_persona` / `update_facts`. `conflict: true` means the live set of that kind changed since the tab fetched it and nothing was written (the tab refetches); `snapshot_touched` is always `false`. |
| `outreach_question` / `outreach_prompt` / `outreach_chunk` / `outreach_done` | Manual outreach (Sleep "Reach Out"): the ask Ava is weighing `{question, ask_kind, user}`, the assembled decision prompt `{pass, segments, input_tokens, rag_tokens, rag_disabled, max_new_tokens, context_length}` (see `checkin_prompt` — same shape, same renderer; this is the reach-out job that actually RETRIEVES, so its `rag` segment is a real injected memory block), streamed reasoning deltas `{text}`, then the outcome `{composed?, resolved?, duplicate?, decision?, session?, opener?, answer? \| skipped, message?}` (`resolved` = she already knew the answer, so the ask was evicted, no session written; `duplicate` = she recognized it as a re-asking of a question already put to the user, so the duplicate ask was evicted, the original left governing). |
| `modules_list` | `{modules: [{name, label, prompt_file, prompt, injectables, output, max_new_tokens}]}` — the module registry, each entry carrying its prompt text so the Modules tab can open on what is actually on disk. |
| `module_inputs` | `{source, inputs: [{id, label, sub, …}]}` — answers `list_module_inputs`. A `til` entry also carries `kind`/`chars`/`has_facts` (whether a protocol is already recorded for that text, which is what makes it worth simulating against). |
| `module_stage` / `module_chunk` / `module_done` | Module simulation (Modules "Simulate"): phase markers `{stage: reading \| generating \| parsed, …}`, streamed generation deltas `{text}` (`<think>` included), then the outcome `{ok, module, session, exchanges, parts, raw, records, lines, counts, count, truncated, stopped_on_loop, cut_before_answer, input_tokens, max_new_tokens, context_length, written: false \| skipped, message?}`. `written` is always false — nothing was committed. `lines` is the display text rendered by the module itself, and `parts` says how many generations were joined (a chunked module such as `chat_summary` runs once per consolidation chunk; the token counts then describe the LAST part, not a total). `cut_before_answer` distinguishes a pass cut inside its `<think>` (an empty `records` means the pass FAILED) from a chat that established nothing, and `stopped_on_loop` distinguishes BOTH from a stop-guard halt — a guard ends the generation far short of the cap yet still reads as `truncated` (neither stop ends on EOS), so without it a halt at 1.2k of 12k tokens is reported as running out of budget. |
| `deliberation_stage` / `deliberation_chunk` / `deliberation_done` | Deliberation (Worklog "Deliberate"): phase markers `{stage: reading \| deciding \| decided \| dispatching \| dispatched, …}`, streamed reasoning deltas `{text}`, then the outcome `{mode, decided?, action, why, would_do, executed, outcome?, dispatch_skipped?, episodes, open_threads, truncated? \| skipped, message?}`. `mode` is the box's `deliberation.mode`; `executed` says whether a handler ran (never on the dry-run button), `outcome` is the drive's own summary dict, `dispatch_skipped` names why a chosen action did not run (`no_handler` / `handler_error`). |
| `checkin_prompt` / `outreach_prompt` | `{pass, segments: [{kind, label, text}], input_tokens, rag_tokens, rag_disabled, max_new_tokens, context_length}` — the FULL model-facing prompt of ONE pass, in the **same labelled-segment shape live chat's `prompt_debug` uses**, so the Sleep tab renders it with the Chat tab's colour scheme (blue framing / green RAG / red the turn being answered / grey history) and an operator comparing chat retrieval against a background pass's doesn't also have to translate between two layouts. Emitted once per generation by `generation._make_sync_reflect_generate`'s `on_prompt_debug` hook — check-in sends one per *uncached* recap plus one for the decision pass (`pass` names which), outreach one for its decision pass. Built from the same `(kind, label, text)` parts the reflect-lane system message is joined from (`_reflect_system_parts`), so a newly injected block appears here for free. `rag_disabled` is what an absent `rag` segment cannot say: whether the pass *asked* for retrieval at all — the first thing to establish when retrieval is under suspicion, and true for BOTH check-in passes today. Manual (Sleep-tab button) paths only; the autonomous jobs pass no hook, so nothing of this size reaches the activity journal. |
| `checkin_stage` / `checkin_chunk` / `checkin_done` | Manual check-in (Sleep "Check In"): the window Ava is weighing `{stage:"considering", hours, chats, user, simulated}` — `user` is whose check-in this is, and the autonomous path emits a wake's several people in sequence, so the activity journal keys its lines on it, one chat being compressed `{stage:"summarizing", i, n, session}` and its finished recap `{stage:"recapped", i, n, session, when, user, recap, source, cached, truncated}` (the recaps ARE the decision pass's whole input, so both surfaces render the text; `source` is `reflection` — reflection's stored gist, no generation — or `generated`, and `cached` keeps its narrow meaning of the in-memory recap cache), streamed reasoning deltas `{text}`, then the outcome `{composed?, decision?, session?, opener?, silence_hours?, user? \| skipped, message?}` (a yes wrote the reversed check-in session into the chats list). |
| `dedup_stage` / `facts_deduped` | Semantic fact dedup: per-block progress `{stage:"clustered", facts, subjects, multi, singletons, candidates}` once after the subject blocking, then `{stage:"block", i, n, items, groups, merged, rejected}` per grouping call — the pass is dozens of sequential clean-base calls, and one that reports nothing is indistinguishable from a hang. Terminal `facts_deduped {before, after?, groups, evicted, reinserted?, dry_run \| skipped, message?}`, where `groups` is the keep/drop report. |
| `digest_dryrun_stage` / `digest_dryrun_chunk` / `digest_dryrun_done` | Persona-digest dry run (Sleep "Persona digest (dry)"): phase + per-block progress `{stage, i?, n?, themes?, rejected?, round?, before?, after?, calls?, rejected_blocks?, …}`, streamed synthesis deltas `{text}`, then the outcome `{items, stats, themes, digest, rendered, current \| skipped, message?}` — `themes` carries every merged theme's members so a wrong merge is visible, and `current` summarizes the ACTIVE digest for comparison. Nothing was written. |
| `regen_persona_stage` / `regen_persona_chunk` / `regen_persona_done` | Persona regeneration (Persona tab "Regen persona"): phase + per-block progress `{stage: gathered \| clean_base_enter \| map \| reduce_started \| reduce \| clustered \| synthesizing \| digest_written \| producing_persona, …}`, streamed synthesis deltas `{text}` (digest then self-portrait, `<think>` included), then the outcome `{run_id, items, themes, stats, counts, digest, rendered, activated, persona_path, persona_error? \| skipped, message?}` — `rendered` is the portrait exactly as a chat turn will see it (`render_digest_for_chat`; empty until the maturity gate passes), `activated` false means the digest was written but the previous persona stayed active. |
| `retry_ready` | `{user_prompt, speaker, exchange_index}` — the latest completed exchange was rolled off the active session (answer to `retry_last_exchange`); the client excises the rendered turn and restores `user_prompt` in the input. |
| `context_cleared` / `session_notes_saved` / `sessions_list` / `session_data` | Responses to above requests. `sessions_list` entries carry `initiated_by` so the client badges an Ava-initiated outreach session (`💬 Ava:`); selecting it adopts it in place (`load_session in_place=True`) so the user replies in that same file. `session_data` is the transcript JSON with, per exchange that has a decodable stored `tension` series, a render-only `tension_spans` (`{cot, answer}` of `[text, margin, relief, pain]` runs, the `done` payload's format) attached for the Chat review tab's colouring — never written to the file. |
| `regenerate_chunk` | `{cot, reply}` — a delta of an in-flight `regenerate_exchange`, already classified into the two boxes the Training review dialog shows (the raw stream is family-specific, so the split runs server-side through the same `_CotStreamSplitter` the public API streams with). A live preview: the terminal `exchange_regenerated` is authoritative and replaces it (that terminal payload also carries `adapter` — the authoring adapter's name read off the RUNTIME, since under the sticky swap the request alone can't name it; `""` = bare base). |
| `regenerate_status` | `{text}` — an adapter-swap milestone of an in-flight `regenerate_exchange` whose `adapter` required an actual swap (the swap is sticky, so only the FIRST regeneration under a new choice reloads). Sent because a reload is minutes of otherwise-total silence in a review dialog that opened at the button press. Never sent when the requested adapter is already the loaded one. |
| `session_reflection_reset` | `{filename, removed, had_sidecar}` — answer to `reset_session_reflection`: which files were removed, and whether the chat had a sidecar at all (`had_sidecar: false` = it was already awaiting reflection). |
| `exchange_corrupt_marked` | `{filename, exchange_index, corrupt_cot, corrupt_response, sidecar_invalidated}` — the resulting per-exchange corruption state after a `mark_corrupt`; `sidecar_invalidated` true when the chat's poisoned sidecar was deleted (chat un-frozen, will re-reflect). |
| `history_rewritten` | `{rewritten, deleted, wander_deleted, chats, skipped, errors}` — outcome of a `rewrite_history`: how many locked exchanges were baked into their transcripts + unfrozen, how many banned exchanges were deleted from them, how many banned wander captures were dropped, the per-chat breakdown (`{session, exchanges, deleted}`), and any skipped (active session / empty target) / errored exchanges. |
| `training_ban_set` | `{target, exchange_index, banned, kind}` — the row's training-ban state after a `set_training_ban`. `kind` is `"chat"` or `"wander"`. |

### Config and storage

| Path | Contents |
|---|---|
| `config.json` (repo root) | `{"server_host": "...", "user_name": "..."}` — `user_name` is the name Ava sees the local operator as |
| `server/server_config.json` | `{model_id, context_length, reflect_context_length?, consolidation, adapter_id?, chat_repetition_penalty?, chat_min_p?, chat_degen_guard?, chat_degen?, base_quant?, lora_r?, train_max_seq_length?, train_lr_schedule?, train_lr?, train_plateau_epochs?, checkin?, api?}` — the base `model_id` stays **frozen**; `train_cycle.py` (re)points `adapter_id` at the persistent LoRA adapter it saves each cycle (`server/models/adapter-<run_id>/`, named after the triggering reflection run; `adapter-<ts>` fallback for a standalone build). `reflect_context_length` (default `32768`; back-filled `== context_length` on older configs, so no split until raised) is the **reflection window AND the physical `max_seq_length` the model loads at**: the model is loaded ONCE at `max(context_length, reflect_context_length)`, so chat stays capped at `context_length` (guaranteeing its transcripts fit a later reflection) while a reflection run packs a bigger window (sleep prompt + RAG + open questions + transcript). `context_length` is the **chat / default** budget (`generation.py`, status, token-metering read it unchanged); `_runtime.reflect_context_length` == the physical load, and `agentic.CleanBaseSession` preserves both across the clean-base swap. A reflection run's window is the ceiling by default but can be dialed DOWN per run from the Sleep tab's **Reflect ctx** control (`start_reflection_run` `reflect_context_length`, clamped server-side to `[context_length, ceiling]`); raising the ceiling needs a config edit + reload. `handle_status` reports `reflect_context_length` so the client bounds that control. **Degeneration floor** (keys keep their historical `chat_*` names; scope differs per knob — `chat_repetition_penalty` is chat/ephemeral/encounter only, while `chat_min_p` + `chat_degen_guard`/`chat_degen` ALSO cover the reflect/agentic generate factories since 2026-07-31, that lane having run with no penalty, no n-gram ban and the longest budgets on the box): `chat_repetition_penalty` (default `1.1`; `1.0`/`null` to disable) is the mild live-chat repetition penalty — applied to **generated tokens only** via a custom `LogitsProcessor` (the prompt, incl. injected RAG blocks, is exempt; transformers' native kwarg penalizes the whole `input_ids` and so suppressed exactly the vocabulary RAG primes) — with ONE carve-out: the immediately-previous assistant reply's tokens ARE penalized (unioned into the gather as if already generated), part of the **anti-copy guard** that also n-gram-bans extending a verbatim ≥8-token copy of that reply (`_NoCopyPrevReply`; the t≈1.0 verbatim-regurgitation attractor — see the `inference_backend.py` entry); the halt-only verbatim `stop_on_repeat` loop guard is always on; `chat_min_p` (default `0.02`; `null`/`<=0` to disable) is a **Layer-1** relative-probability sampling floor that cuts the implausible tail seeding a collapse while leaving a high-entropy persona's nucleus intact; `chat_degen_guard` (default `true`) enables the **Layer-2** *drifting-degeneration* halt (distinct-token-ratio / single-token-dominance over a rolling window) that catches the associative-walk / letter-soup runaway the verbatim guard is blind to — the shape the repetition penalty itself converts a verbatim loop into; `chat_degen` optionally overrides its thresholds (`{window, min_gen, distinct_ratio, top_freq}`). All content-blind, so they hold for any emergent persona. Layers 1+2 apply to reflection too: Layer 2 is halt-only (no sampled token altered), Layer 1 shapes reflection sampling and so the IDEALs that become training targets. Branch replay generates through `generate_from_ids_batch` and is covered by none of this. `base_quant` (`"16bit"`/`"8bit"`/`"4bit"`; absent ⇒ historical 4-bit default) is the base-model **load precision**, set by the Migrate tab when cloning to a roomier box; `handle_load` **and the startup auto-load** read it (unless a `load` message overrides) and thread `load_in_4bit`/`load_in_8bit` into the backend. **With an adapter present, raising precision (16/8-bit) requires loading the full-precision `model_id` base + attaching the LoRA on top** — because a LoRA's `adapter_config.json` hardcodes its (bnb-4bit) `base_model_name_or_path`, so the convenient `from_pretrained(adapter_dir)` path is pinned to 4-bit and ignores the request. `inference_backend.load` detects the override and stays on unsloth's *native* load path (base load → adapter attach → inference patches — hand-rolling the attach with raw peft skips unsloth's patch step and crashes with `unhashable type: 'set'`), but points it at the full-precision base by staging a throwaway adapter dir (`_stage_adapter_with_base`: symlinks to the real weights + an `adapter_config.json` whose `base_model_name_or_path` is rewritten to `model_id`; deleted after load, original untouched). A LoRA is precision-agnostic so a 4-bit-trained adapter applies cleanly to the bf16 base. This means the **full-precision base repo must be downloadable** (the bnb-4bit repo alone can't be upcast). `train_max_seq_length` (default `8192` since 2026-07-31, clamped to `context_length`) is the **offline training** sequence cap read by `train_cycle` — it drives the answer-preserving message-level truncation (drop oldest history turns, keep system + final exchange + target), and a row whose irreducible floor still exceeds it is quarantined rather than handed to TRL's answer-chopping head-slice. It **no longer bounds the fused CE-loss chunk**: that is pinned to `2 × UNSLOTH_CE_LOSS_TARGET_GB` regardless of sequence length (see the env block atop `train_cycle.py` — unsloth_zoo's `get_chunk_size` rounds to ONE uncapped chunk below a qlen threshold, so the target, not the cap, is the CE-side lever and lowering the cap alone can *raise* the peak). The two are separate budgets: tune `UNSLOTH_CE_LOSS_TARGET_GB` for a CE-side OOM and this cap for an activation-side one. It was 4096 while the logit buffer was the binding constraint; 8192 covers observed row lengths outright (at 4096 a real run still trimmed 3/42 rows and quarantined 3 more). Training-only; inference always uses the full `context_length`. `lora_r` (default `32`, from `training.decay.TRAIN_LORA_R_DEFAULT`; clamped `>=1`) is the **adapter rank** (`get_peft_model` r) of the from-scratch LoRA fit — the capacity/VRAM knob (8/16/32/64/128 exposed in `settings.py`); an explicit caller (CLI `--lora-r`, Sleep `train_params.lora_r`) overrides it. Scaling is **rank-stabilized** (`use_rslora=True`, γ = `lora_alpha`/√r with `lora_alpha` a FIXED `training.decay.TRAIN_LORA_ALPHA` = 4, *not* tied to r), calibrated so γ == 1.0 at r=16 — identical to every adapter built before the switch, so `train_lr` carries over. This makes rank an approximately **LR-neutral** knob: under the former `lora_alpha == r` (γ pinned at 1.0) the weight delta `dW ≈ γ·dB@A` summed over r terms while Adam moved each entry of B by ~lr regardless of rank, so ‖dW‖ grew ~√r and raising the rank silently raised the effective LR (r=32 came out *less* stable than r=16). No migration — peft records `use_rslora` per `adapter_config.json` and recomputes γ at load, so older adapters keep their original scaling. `train_lr_schedule` (`"age_ramp"` default, or `"triangular"`) selects the global LR shape on top of age/wander/contamination multipliers. `age_ramp` keeps the global shape flat and honors the caller’s epoch count (default 1). `triangular` uses one warmup epoch, `train_plateau_epochs` hold epochs (default 3), and one decay epoch, forcing `epochs = train_plateau_epochs + 2`. Across those passes, each row’s scheduled LR fractions sum to `train_plateau_epochs + 1`, independent of corpus position. This does not make optimizer updates independent of order. Chronological SequentialSampler order and batch=1/GA=1 remain. `train_lr` (default `8e-6`, from `training.decay.TRAIN_LR_DEFAULT`) is the base/peak SFT LR the per-row multipliers + schedule shape scale on top of; an explicit caller (CLI `--lr`, Sleep `train_params.lr`) overrides it. The inference server **back-fills every documented knob** that is absent into the config on boot (`_backfill_server_config` → `config_schema.backfill_defaults`, 2026-09-17; it used to be three hand-listed training keys) so each new setting is present for hand-editing after one run — behaviour-preserving, box state (`model_id`/`adapter_id`) never written, a present null left alone. `checkin` (optional) tunes the **check-in** idle job, which decides **once per person** (see `core/checkin.py`), so every knob below is per person: `silence_threshold_hours` (default `5`) is how long *that person* must be quiet — measured from their own transcripts on disk, not the idle clock — before an autonomous check-in may reach out to them, and `recent_chats` (default `5`) is how many of their recent conversations she reviews to decide. `max_users` (default `3`) caps how many people may reach a *generation* in one wake; the silence and staleness gates are pure disk reads, so a candidate they reject costs nothing and does not spend a slot, and anyone held back is `deferred` to the next wake — raise it on a box with many active users, lower it to spend less GPU per wake. `max_silence_days` (default `30`, `0` ⇒ no ceiling) is where someone stops being a candidate at all: past it they *stopped* talking rather than *went* quiet. It exists because the sweep enumerates everyone the corpus has ever recorded a turn from, and the reach-out backoff settles at one message a day rather than at zero — so without a ceiling a contact who left a year ago would be written to daily forever. The manual Sleep-tab button ignores it, as it already ignores the silence threshold. `min_user_turns` (default `1`) is how much of the USER a session must contain to be one of them — real user turns counted structurally (`_user_turns`), not inferred from the file having grown. While the user is away the freshest files on the box are the ones *Ava's own* reach-out jobs wrote, so the window would otherwise fill with threads whose content is mostly her own openers and the recap pass would hand the decision pass a summary of what she already said. `1` admits every genuine exchange (a short answer is still the user speaking); `2` demands actual back-and-forth and drops an opener-plus-one-line-reply thread, at the risk of an empty window (`no_recent`) on a quiet box. `max_new_tokens` (default `12288`) is the **decision pass's** generation reserve and `summary_max_new_tokens` (default `8192`) each **per-chat recap's**, both clamped by `runtime_state.reflect_output_reserve` against the **reflect** window (the physical load) — the decision pass leaving 4096 for its input, the recap pass 8192 because it reads a whole transcript where the other reads five short recaps. They were flat constants (8192 / 2048), which is the wrong shape for a job that owns the GPU and the whole window for its duration: under the thought ceiling 2048 gave a recap ~1434 tokens of thought to read up to 8000 characters of transcript, so the half that overflowed was the READING, not the writing — and since a truncated recap is deliberately left UNCACHED, an overflowing chat was re-generated and re-logged on every hourly check-in forever. The clamp carries a `min_fraction` floor as well as the input reserve, because subtracting a fixed input budget is the wrong guard on a SMALL window (an 8192-token box would have left the recap pass 512, less than the constant it replaced). Absent ⇒ the defaults; the Sleep-tab **"Check In"** button forges the silence period so it fires regardless. `background_reflection.sidecar_backfill` (default `true`) is the kill switch for rung 1 of the background wake — producing the missing gist/fact sidecars for the whole corpus, oldest first, *before* any per-chat reflection. It gates rung 2 by design, so on a large corpus with no sidecars the box will spend hours on cheap artifacts before reflecting anything; that is the intent, and this makes it reversible without a code change. `reachout.stale_delete_hours` (default `48`, `0` ⇒ off) is how long an **unanswered** chat Ava opened herself stays in the corpus before it is deleted — at the head of a reflection run (`reflection_service._run_stale_reachout_sweep`) **and** at the head of every background per-chat wake (rung 0), both through the shared `reflection_service.run_stale_reachout_sweep` → `chat_worklog.delete_stale_reachouts`. It is the one session shape reflection can never do anything with — skipped un-frozen forever — so past the point where a reply is realistic it is permanent backlog, a dead chat-list entry, and a RAG passage where her own unanswered message can come back as if it were dialogue. Deleting it does NOT make her forget being ignored: each opener is tombstoned first (`data/hot/reachout/expired.jsonl`), so the reach-out backoff, check-in's standing-opener list and outreach's dangling-opener guard all read it exactly as they read the file. Raise it to keep unanswered openers around longer; `0` restores the pre-2026-08-08 behaviour of keeping them forever. `outreach.max_new_tokens` (default `12288`) is the **outreach decision pass's** generation reserve — thought + `DECISION` + the opener, clamped by `runtime_state.reflect_output_reserve` leaving 4096 for its input (the shape `checkin.max_new_tokens` and `synthesis.max_new_tokens` have); it was a flat 8192, observed exhausted inside the think on a long meta ask with the window three quarters empty. A pass that hits the cap sends nothing and holds that ask out of selection for 24 h (process-local), so the next attempt weighs a different one instead of failing on the same one every wake. `outreach.min_reask_hours` (default `72`) is the **re-ask gap**: how long before Ava may raise the *same* open ask again on her own initiative. It exists because `meta` asks are exempt from the surface-count ceiling ("never retires"), which on an hourly outreach clock turned a small live meta pool into a round-robin that re-raised each question every few hours in the same words; it is the ask-side counterpart of `synthesis.min_resynth_days` / `revisit.min_revisit_days`. Scoped to outreach — the passive session-opening path (`generation._surface_block`) keeps no gap, since the user paces it by opening the session. `synthesis.max_new_tokens` (default `12288`) is the **synthesis analysis pass's generation reserve** — thought AND structured output for one chunk — clamped to at most two thirds of `context_length` and to whatever leaves a 4096-token input budget. It is a knob rather than a constant because the ceiling that stops a truncated CoT depends on the model's thinking length: this pass re-reads an aged chat against the *current* self and reasons about the difference, a longer thought than the consolidation pass's forward read, and it exhausted the inherited 8192 inside an unclosed `<think>`. Raising it takes room from the transcript, which is the correct trade — a smaller input budget splits the chat into more chunks and every one is still read, while a CoT cut before its channel closes yields no answer region at all and wastes the whole pass. `synthesis.opener_max_new_tokens` (default `12288`) is the **opener-composition pass's** budget, clamped against the **reflect** window (the physical load — `synthesis._reflect_window`, mirroring `generation._reflect_window`, since that is what the reflect-lane generate resolves `max_new_tokens` against) minus a 4096-token input budget. Separate from the analysis reserve because it competes with nothing: this pass's input is one small template, not a transcript, so unlike the analysis reserve it takes no room from a chunk budget and splits no chat. It was a flat 4096, under which the thought ceiling (`generation._reflect_think_ceiling`, 30% of the budget held back for the answer) left the message itself ~1.2k tokens — and this pass drafts several openings inside its CoT and then writes the chosen one out in full, so it was observed re-emitting its drafting into the answer region and being cut mid-message. A truncated opener is now refused outright (`skipped: "opener_truncated"` — see below), so a small budget here costs a reach-out rather than corrupting one. `graph.enabled` (**default `true` since 2026-08-12**; it shipped `false` the day before, and FACTS_TREE.md §10 records the flip rather than being left to contradict the code) turns on the **facts-tree retrieval channel**, i.e. FACTS_TREE.md §10 consumer 5. On, a live chat turn becomes **two-stage**: before the reply is written a short thinking-off pass reads the numbered candidate list plus the conversation and picks which recorded facts the message turns on, and `graph.blob.render_claims` renders those claims into a block injected ahead of the RAG block (`generation._fetch_facts_block_sync`, wrapped by `prompts/facts_block_prompt.txt`). It costs **a whole extra generation on time-to-first-token** — cheap to decode (≤8 numbers) but its prefill is the candidate list, ~10k tokens on the current corpus — which is why it was opt-in for a day and why the candidate cap in `graph.blob.claim_candidates` bounds that prefill; set `false` to restore the single-stage turn. An **unreadable** config yields `false` whatever the default, since an unknown-state box should not silently spend a generation a turn. The two §10 safety rules hold by construction rather than by check: the candidate list is built already filtered to the knowledge facets with `person:_self` removed, so a `position` claim or anything from the self subtree is never *shown* and therefore cannot be picked; and the block is rendered from returned ordinals in code, so nothing generated becomes prose. Rides the Chat tab's **Facts** checkbox (knowledge about the world, not Ava's self), so unchecking it still means "no fact knowledge this turn". Every failure path — no tree, no prompt, no model, a failed generation, nothing picked — yields an empty block with a named reason, never a failed turn. `graph.max_claims` (default `6`) caps the block and `graph.fetch_max_new_tokens` (default `512`) the pass's budget. `graph.gist_facts` (default `true`, rides `graph.enabled`) extends the same stage-1 fetch to the **TIL recap pass** (`til_wander.run_gist_pass` → `fact_fetch.fetch_blob_for_text`), so a news digest or wandered article is read against what is already on record instead of as a text about strangers — the fix for her processing world events as a distanced observer while the corpus held that the user spent the 2026 Iran war in a bomb shelter. Only the *selective* passes are conditioned: the protocol pass (`til_facts`) stays `disable_rag=True` because its contract is exhaustive witness, and that uninjected record is exactly what makes conditioning the recap recoverable. **Fiction is a fourth fact class as of 2026-08-16** (`chat_facts.FACT_CLASSES` gains `depicted`, `fold` maps `(til, depicted) → FACET_DEPICTION`): the `til_facts` wander framing used to assert *"an encyclopedia article … most of what it states is standing description of its subject"*, which is false for three of the five enabled wander sources (WikiTropes entirely, Lurkmore/Neolurk largely) and for the manual-visit path that takes any URL — and on such a page it is an instruction to file an invented world's contents as `standing`, i.e. `property`, i.e. a **knowledge facet offered to live chat**. The witness contract has no answer for a text that asserts nothing about the world, so `depicted` is a new class rather than a reused one; it is **carried by the reading lane, not withheld** (a tropes article meeting an earlier conversation is what that lane is for) with `blob._SOURCE_VERB` rendering *"depicts"* against a report's *"reported"*, since the verb IS the epistemics. Safe at every layer: `depiction` is outside `KNOWLEDGE_FACETS`, and `(chat, depicted)` is deliberately unmapped so a vocabulary drift lands in `unclassified` — also outside knowledge — making the failure mode withheld material rather than promoted material. `graph.gist_max_reports` (default `2`, `0` ⇒ off) is the **attributed-report channel**, the reading lane's own facet rule. It widens that lane's candidate list to `graph.blob.READING_FACETS` (knowledge **+ `report`** — what a TEXT asserted) and budgets the render for it; the two move on ONE knob because offering claims nothing will carry is a wasted prefill and budgeting for claims never offered is a silent no-op. The asymmetry with chat is the same one that governs the rest of the package: `report` is withheld from a chat turn because a pass told *"things you know"* would state a news assertion as fact **to a person**, while a recap asserts nothing to anyone — so the epistemics move from *withhold* to **attribute**, and `graph.blob.report_line` names the text (*"a digest of world events from 2026-07-28 reported (2026-07-28): …"*), rendered in its own section so it is never folded under a node as knowledge. The phrase comes from an INJECTED `describe_source` (`til_wander._describe_source`, the pattern `render_blob` uses for `words_match`, since resolving a ref reads the snippets tree and `graph/` never imports the inference role) and names the KIND as well as the date — on this box that is the difference between evidence and a joke, the wiki list being chosen for tone rather than truth (*"an article about Ельцин on Lurkmore"*). An unresolvable ref falls back to the raw ref and a source-less claim to *"something on record reported"*: an unattributed report is the one thing this channel must never render as known. `position` is offered to **neither** lane — a report has a text behind it that can be named, while a position is one person's view, and rendering it here would put the interlocutor's politics into a recap of the world. Reports carry their own budget rather than sharing `gist_max_claims`, since a TIL protocol yields an order of magnitude more claims than a chat one and a shared budget would let news crowd out the scarce conversational half. **`person:_self` is never widened** — that rule is about the subtree whatever the facet. `graph.gist_max_claims` (default `3`) is tighter than chat's `max_claims` for a measured reason — `claim_candidates` on the live tree offers **5 claims, all about the one person she talks to** (16 of the 21 knowledge claims are `person:_self` and dropped by §10), so the failure mode is a recap of world events drifting into a recap of him, and a recap is a few hundred characters where a chat turn is a reply. Cheap here despite costing a generation: the candidate list IS the cost and it is five lines (~1.7k-token prompt on a real digest, against the chat path's ~10k). Fails soft to the unconditioned recap on every path; `run_gist_pass` reports `facts_picked`/`facts_chars`/`facts_sources`/`facts_skipped` beside the recap. `graph.nominate_max` (default `1`, `0` ⇒ off) caps how many CONVERSATIONS a turn's picked facts may recall — the fact-nomination slot in the past-chat block (`rag_engine._query_nominated`). Under `graph` because it cannot happen without the fetch that produces it, and a knob rather than a constant because it is the one part of the channel that spends prompt budget: each nomination is an additive passage on top of `top_k`. One, because the pass is asked for its picks most-important first — so the first nomination is the conversation behind the fact that mattered most, and a second is already an argument about budget rather than about relevance. `graph.reachout_facts` (default `true`, rides `graph.enabled`) extends the stage-1 fetch to the **reach-out lane** — the two passes that RAISE questions: outreach's decision (the ask lane, `fact_fetch.fetch_blob_for_ask` — its `resolved` branch is the box's one formal "have I already learned the answer?" judgement and evicts a live ask for good, so it must judge against the record, not against whatever cosine surfaced) and synthesis's analysis (the re-read lane, `fetch_blob_for_reread` — its questions form against the record instead of being vetted afterwards, which nothing downstream could do since it sends its own opener). One switch for both because the reason to condition them is the same; they share chat's `max_claims`/`fetch_max_new_tokens`/`til_max_age_days`. Both lanes are knowledge-facets-only (no `report` claims — a text's assertion must not retire or suppress a question); outreach additionally hands its picks' sources to the pass's own past-chat nomination slot (the reflect seam's `rag_nominate_sessions`), synthesis deliberately does not (its pass reads one conversation, RAG-off). `graph.til_max_age_days` is the freshness scope on world facts and is **unset by default since 2026-08-12** — a TIL claim is offered whatever its age, exactly like a chat-backed one. It shipped at `7`, on the reasoning that a news digest is spent within days where a standing fact about a person holds next month; what that missed is that the TIL lane carries far more than news (an article about a place, a trope, an organisation ages like a chat fact, not like a headline), so a cut keyed on the LANE discarded the durable material to bound the perishable. What it was really protecting — the size and mix of the candidate list, TIL supplying 34.3 showable claims per protocol against chat's 1.5 — is `DEFAULT_MAX_CANDIDATES` (400) plus the chat-backed-first ordering, which truncates oldest-TIL-first and never touches conversational material. Set an integer to restore a scope; the mechanism is intact, only its default changed. Needs a built tree (`python -m graph.build`); a missing one is a named skip, since missing/corrupt/unrecognised all mean *rebuild* and none mean "no facts". `assoc` (optional, absent ⇒ defaults) configures the **associative library bridge** (`core/assoc_bridge.py`, `assoc/`): `enabled` (default **`true`** since 2026-09-10; it shipped `false` for a day) hands stage 1 of a chat turn to the library instead of the facts tree — a library with no build yet (a fresh box before its first `assoc_feed` wake) falls through to the tree channel, so a pull never costs a turn its facts; `false` restores the tree channel exactly; `feed` (default `true`) keeps the library built by the `assoc_feed` idle job; `feed_subprocess` (default `true`) runs that job's sync + rebuild in a worker process (`core/assoc_feed_worker.py`) so the rebuild's GIL-holding Python cannot starve the server's event loop — `false` restores in-process — and `feed_timeout_s` (default `7200`) is how long the worker may run before it is killed and the wake reported failed; `tier` (default `2`, the §12 verdict; `3` adds the activation spread), `budget_tokens` (default `3000`, the injected block's budget across the three grains), `max_picks` (default `6`), `device` (`cuda`; falls back to CPU), `embedder` (`bge-m3`; `hash` is the offline stand-in); `aha` (default `true`) runs the `assoc_aha` idle job — open asks as needs, the judge on what the feed brought in, an opener behind the reach-out gate — and `aha_max_judgements` (default `1`) caps the thinking-on judgements per wake; `witness` (default `true`) runs the `assoc_witness` idle job that replaces the imported `chat_facts` protocols with the library's own witness, `witness_per_wake` (default `3`) chats per wake; `relations` (default `true`) folds the typed-relation pass into the feed's rebuild when a model is loaded, `relations_per_wake` (default `120`) claims per wake; `pivot` (default `true`) runs the `assoc_pivot` idle job and `pivot_bridges` (default `["sense"]`; add `"sound"`, `"root"`) chooses the bridge kinds — the sound bridge is an edit-distance pass over the whole vocabulary, seconds per call, fine for an idle job. `anchors.enabled` (default `true`) is the kill-switch for the **anchor retrieval channel** (`rag_engine._query_anchors`) — set it false to cut the reserved anchor slot without a code change while the channel is being evaluated; anchor *production* during reflection is unaffected (that is `overrides.exchange_anchors`). `recollections.enabled` (default `true`) is the same shape for the **`[recollection]` channel** — retrieval only; production is `overrides.recollection`. `impressions.enabled` (default `true`) is that shape once more, for the **`[impression]` channel** (Ava's readings of the people she talks to) — again retrieval only; producing them is `overrides.user_notes`. `self_impressions.enabled` is the same shape for the **`[self_impression]` channel** (her reading of her own transcripts from the outside) but **defaults `false`**, alone among these switches: nothing in the box asks for the channel, the records exist to be folded into `users/_self.json` rather than recalled, and a reading of her own transcripts surfacing inside a live turn is the exact circularity the artifact is built to stay out of. `self_portrait.enabled` (default `true`) governs the **fold** into that file, the counterpart of `user_portrait.enabled` — with no injection layer to gate, since nothing injects it; production of the records is `overrides.self_notes`. `reflection_block.subject_cap` (default `1`) / `.subject_sim` (default `0.45`) tune the **subject cap** on the injected reflection block — how many of its three slots may go to records about ONE subject, and the cosine at which two count as the same subject (`rag_engine._subject_crowded`). It exists because the near-duplicate rule beside it can only reject a record that RESTATES a chosen one, while a mature store accumulates several genuinely distinct records about one topic, which then rank together and take every slot. `0` disables the cap; `2` leaves one slot guaranteed to a different subject; `1` makes every slot a distinct subject, buying breadth at the cost of depth on a focused query (the past-chat channel still carries the conversation itself). Retrieval-only — nothing about how facts are stored changes. `user_portrait.enabled` (default `true`) governs the **standing per-person portrait** folded from those impressions, and is read *independently* by the two halves (`reflection_runner` folds, `generation` injects), so a box can go on folding portraits it does not yet inject, or stop folding while keeping the last one live. Its curve is tuned under `consolidation.wall_clock.recollection` (`hold_h` 96, `cap_age_h` 192, `floor_weight` 0.3). `api` (optional, absent ⇒ **all defaults, listener ON**) configures the **public OpenAI-compatible API** for external tools (`core/api_http.py`): `enabled` (**default `true`** since 2026-07-30 — a pulled box is immediately queryable; `false` means the listener never starts), `port` (default `8000`, else the `--api-port` flag — note the watchdog does NOT pass that flag through on launch, deliberately, since it stays thin and never-upgraded, so on a watchdog-run box set `api.port` here), `host` (empty ⇒ `--api-host`, else `--host`), `api_key` (shared bearer secret; **empty ⇒ no auth**, so anyone who can reach the port spends GPU time as Ava — a non-loopback bind without one warns at boot), `model_name` (default `"ava"`, the id `GET /v1/models` reports and the client's picker shows), `max_tokens` (default `"75%"` — used when a request omits it; takes an integer or chat's percentage-of-remaining-context string), `client_system` (`"append"` default / `"drop"` — whether the calling tool's own system message is honoured), `inject_rag` (default `true` — retrieve her memory per request, as live chat does; off ⇒ prompt + adapter only, usually what a code assistant wants) and `inject_persona` (default `true` — the standing persona portrait, as live chat). Requests are never logged under any setting: that is structural (`_make_api_generate` forces `log_transcripts=False`), not a knob — and the activity journal inherits that guarantee by construction, its generation hooks living in the reflect/agentic factories the API path never touches. `prompt_rewrite` (optional, absent ⇒ defaults) configures the **prompt-rewrite locator** (PROMPT_REWRITE.md; stage 1 only so far): `keep_locator` (default `true`) runs the prompt-mutation pass on a KEPT exchange whose baseline-normalized CoT tension clears `tension_percentile` (default `0.8`) — one extra reflection pass per such exchange, about 5% of kept exchanges on the 2026-09-18 corpus; `false` restores the revise-only locator; `maturity` (default `1.9`) and `min_chats` (default `2`) are the stage-2 gate — a pattern of same-change deltas may fund a rewrite when its tension-weighted recurrence over distinct chats reaches `maturity` with at least `min_chats` chats behind it. `enabled` (default **`true`** since 2026-09-19 — the owner's call: run it live and adjust, never disable) turns on stage 3 — the `rewrite_prompt` deliberation action that spends them (`core/prompt_rewrite.py`): `samples` (5), `temperatures` (empty ⇒ 0.9 each; a list such as `0.9,1.0,1.1,1.2,1.4` restores the owner's sweep), `final_temperature` (0.9), `min_gap_hours` (24 — an attempt, changed/stayed/declined, rests the option this long) and `order_check` (false — rerun the blind choice reversed; a disagreement keeps the current prompt). `deliberation` (optional, absent ⇒ defaults) configures the **deliberation executive** (`core/deliberation.py`): `mode` — `"sole"` (**default since 2026-09-17**: the executive is the only chooser — outreach / synthesis / checkin / wander / assoc_aha / assoc_pivot are not registered as idle jobs; the upkeep jobs stay on their clocks), `"shadow"` (the mode it shipped in on 2026-09-14: the executive runs as an hourly idle job AND the per-drive timers keep firing, so her choices can be compared with theirs in the Activity tab), `"off"` (no autonomous deliberation; the Worklog tab's dry-run button still works); `interval_s` (default `3600`, floor 60), `recent` (default `20`, the worklog entries she reads), `execute` (default `true`; `false` = decide + journal the decision, dispatch nothing — a pure shadow). An unknown mode falls back to `sole`; an unreadable config yields `off`. `logging` (optional, absent ⇒ all defaults) tunes the **unified activity journal** (`core/activity_log.py`): `heartbeat_s` (20, `0` ⇒ no live `stream` records — restores the pre-2026-08-11 silence during a long pass), `body_max_chars` (16000; a longer generation is elided in the MIDDLE, never clipped at the end, since the answer after `</think>` is exactly what an end-clip drops), `stream_tail_chars` (240), `segment_bytes` (16 MB) / `retain_segments` (8) for rotation, `stream_enabled` / `raw_enabled` (the stdout tee), `raw_max_lines_per_s` (200, past which lines coalesce into one suppressed-count note), `raw_max_line_chars` (2000) and `tee_denylist` (extra regexes the tee drops). Every level has an off switch, because the failure mode of a logging change is that it becomes the reason a run dies |
| `server/inference/data/` | Reflection memory, ledgers, portraits, staging, run/activity journals, and scratch. Chats, TIL, graph, and associative state live under `server/data/`; see `training/reflections_path.py`. |
| `server/data/chats/` | *(sidecar also carries a top-level `anchors` map since 2026-07-28: `{exchange_index: {about, tags, generation, run_id, ts}}` — the per-exchange retrieval anchor, written by `ChatSidecar.write_anchor`, read by RagEngine’s reserved anchor retrieval channel; deliberately outside `exchanges` so it does not enter training.)* Active timestamped session JSON + `.state.json` sidecars (each carries `user` + per-exchange `speaker`, and a per-exchange `tension` block when capture is on). A sidecar carries a doc-level `reflected_at` once fully reflected (reflect-once freeze), and — **two-stage freeze** — an intermediate `chat_reflected` stamp set by the background per-chat pass (`core/background_reflection.py`): stage one (consolidation + revision + branch generation done, run-level clean-base still pending). A `chat_reflected`-not-`reflected_at` chat is NOT skipped by a normal run — that run finishes it (clean-base over its persisted jobs) and stamps `reflected_at`. An exchange may also carry optional `corrupt_cot`/`corrupt_response` flags (schema v4, set from the Training review tab via `mark_corrupt`) marking a bug-corrupted CoT/reply so reflection treats it as missing / re-derives an IDEAL and training drops it. **Schema v5** (Training review "Rewrite history"): an exchange's `assistant_cot`/`assistant_response` may be rewritten in place from a human-reviewed sidecar target, in which case the prior content is preserved under an append-only per-exchange `rewrite_history` list and the now-stale `tension` block is removed. An exchange may also carry `banned` (set from the Training review tab's Ban button): the operator's verdict that it must never train again — `training/dialogue_source` refuses it, `ChatSidecar.write_verdict` carries it across re-reflection, and it is NOT sticky (unlike `locked`). **Schema v6** (the same "Rewrite history" action): a banned exchange may be DELETED from the transcript, shifting every later exchange down one index (the sidecar's `exchanges` + `anchors` maps and the ledger's fact hosts are renumbered in the same operation) and preserving the removed turn — minus its tension block — under a session-level append-only `deleted_exchanges` list. A session may also carry two optional top-level markers, both absent on an ordinary user chat: `initiated_by:"ava"` (Ava opened it — outreach/synthesis/check-in; exchange 0 is her opener under a stage-direction speaker, masked from training) and `interlocutor:"ai"` (the other side is a model — encounters + served gossip, where every `user_prompt` is a peer's reply). `interlocutor` exists so anything measuring *the user's* presence (check-in's silence clock + recent window) can tell Ava-talking-to-a-peer from the user talking to Ava; reflection ignores it and treats an encounter like any other session. RAG indexes only these. |
| `server/data/chats/<stem>.summary.json` | The per-conversation **consolidation summary** (gist) — `{text, run_id, ts}`. Split out of `.state.json` on 2026-08-06 because the two have different lifetimes: three paths delete the state sidecar wholesale (`mark_corrupt`, `reset_session_reflection`, the checkpoint purge) and every one is about a *training verdict* being wrong, while past `rag_cap_age_h` (~96h) the gist is the only representation of a chat left in RAG — so re-reflecting an aged chat to fix one bad target erased that conversation from Ava's memory until the next Sleep run completed. `ChatSidecar.read_summary` falls back to the legacy in-sidecar `consolidation_summary` key (no migration: the old copy is read where it lies and superseded on the next reflection) and sanitizes on read. Chunked into chat RAG as `kind="gist"` passages on the slow gist tent |
| `server/data/chats/<stem>.facts.json` | Per-chat fact-extraction protocol: source/session metadata plus facts with subject, class, entities, and time when supplied. Written by reflection, replaced on re-reflection. Consumed by the facts-tree build and associative feed; selected source-backed facts can reach live chat through those retrieval channels. It remains distinct from the reflection-memory op-log. See `core/chat_facts.py`. |
| `server/inference/data/hot/memory/` | Decisions Ava recalls: `rag_memory.jsonl` (ask/fact/persona/**recollection**/**impression**/**self_impression** op-log — a `self_impression` record (2026-08-06) is what one of her own transcripts shows about HER, read as a reader with no access to the `<think>` she wrote it from (the counterpart of `persona`, which is that same reading from the inside); it carries NO `about`/`source` (the kind names its subject) and is namespaced in the key space so it cannot collide with an `impression` of the same wording; RAG-only and off by default in retrieval, existing to be folded by `core/self_portrait.py` into `users/_self.json` — an `impression` record (2026-07-31) is Ava's *reading* of a person (as against a `fact`, which is what they told her), carrying `about`/`source`/`source_class` exactly as a fact does; RAG-only, so it never reaches `weights_persona.jsonl` or the ledger, and it is what `core/user_digest.py` folds into that person's standing portrait — — a `recollection` record (2026-07-31) is one per conversation, written by a REVISIT, and carries `content`/`trigger`/`tags`/`origin_ts`/`supersedes`; its `ts` is the decay clock (when the reading was formed) while `origin_ts` is the recalled conversation's own date, display-only. Each write evicts that chat's previous recollection (`reason:"superseded_by_recollection"`), so at most ONE is live per chat. RAG-only: it never reaches `weights_persona.jsonl` or the ledger — a `fact` record may carry `about`/`source`/`source_class`, the attribution added 2026-07-25; absent ⇒ `observed` — also carries the `from_weights` mirror of every weights-bound `[persona]`/`[fact]` so they're recalled at chat time), `weights_persona.jsonl` (persona/fact statements bound for weights — durable provenance / future-weights source) |
| `server/inference/data/hot/consolidation/` | Consolidation bookkeeping: `consolidation_anchors.jsonl` (durable anchor ledger) |
| `server/inference/data/hot/users/` | **Per-person user portraits** — `<slug>.json`, one flat file per person (the person IS the version axis, so no `current` pointer). Written by `core/user_digest.py` from the live `[impression]` + attributed-`[fact]` fold; injected into a chat turn by `generation._current_user_portrait` when that person is speaking. Regenerable from the op-log, so `wipe_state` deletes it **with** that op-log (a portrait outliving its evidence would be a ghost: injected every turn, no longer derivable from or correctable by anything on the box). Travels with `data/` in a runnable snapshot; archived per run under `reflections/<run_id>/users/`. The dir also holds ONE non-person artifact: **`_self.json`**, the outside-view self-portrait (`core/self_portrait.py`) folded from `[self_impression]` — a reserved slug `person_slug` refuses, so it can never be reached as a person or injected as a user portrait, and it inherits this dir's archival/wipe/snapshot handling for free. |
| `server/inference/data/hot/persona/` | Versioned persona-digest snapshots (`digest-<ts>.json`) + `current.json` pointer — the cumulative self-portrait, written by `reflection_digest.py`, rollback-able like the adapter lineage |
| `server/data/archive/chats/` | Retired compatibility path returned by `archive_chats_dir()`, normally nonexistent. Readers tolerate it if present; no current archiving process populates it. |
| `server/inference/data/scratch/` | Disposable: `sft_render.jsonl` (exact retained training rows; deleted at cycle end) + `sft_quarantine.jsonl` (refused-row provenance, retained for triage) |
| `server/data/til/` | TIL/wander state under the ordered `server/data/` home (sibling of `server/data/chats`, gitignored). `wander.jsonl` — the **durable, keep-forever** wander corpus (voice-pass captures): trained from-scratch every build (`build_dataset`, fixed `WANDER_LR_MULT`, never cleared) **and** the source for the chat-RAG wander channel (`rag_engine`, off on every path — so training was this corpus's ONLY live consumer until `wander_sft.reaction_for` (2026-08-13) gave the outreach pass her reaction to pair with an article's recap). A record may carry `banned: true` (Training review → Ban, via `wander_sft.set_banned`) — a capture whose generation came out malformed: `load_pending` and the RAG wander index both skip it, so it leaves the build and retrieval at once, and *Rewrite history* (`wander_sft.delete_banned`) drops the line for good. `snippets/` — human-readable `.txt`/`.json` provenance, ONE subdir per source kind, each written by its own `server/til/fetch_*.py` script: `snippets/wander/` (`fetch_wiki.py`) + `snippets/lookups/` (`fetch_article.py`) are **written on Apply** (a declined manual wander leaves none), while `snippets/news/` (`fetch_current_events.py`) holds the date-named Current-events digests, written at fetch. `server/til/` itself now holds **code only** (the fetchers + `wiki_sources.json`); its former `til/snippets/` output dir moved here 2026-07-28, which also brings the news digests inside the tree `snapshot_state` captures. Legacy `inference/data/hot/wander/pending_sft.jsonl` is folded into `wander.jsonl` on first access (`wander_sft._migrate_legacy`). |
| `server/data/til/snippets/<kind>/<stem>.summary.json` | The **TIL recap** — `{schema_version, source_kind, source_ref, source_url, source_title, source_date, text, truncated, run_id, ts}`. What one article or digest *was*, in her own voice, written beside the snippet that produced it. The TIL counterpart of a chat's gist, and the artifact two consumers needed: a fetched fact recalling its source (`rag_engine._render_til_nomination`) and a raised ask recalling its source (`outreach._source_material`). Derived and re-derivable — unlike the protocol beside it this is a convenience over the text, which is always still there, so losing one costs a regeneration. Written at fetch/apply and backfilled by `til_wander.drain_gist_backlog` during a reflection run's ingestion phase. See `core/til_gist.py` |
| `server/data/assoc/` | Associative library: source documents/chunks, generated witness protocols and relations, derived indexes/cache, registered needs, activation database, and touch history. Indexes are rebuildable; generated records and access history are not reproduced exactly from source texts. A wipe deletes this root, full clone export carries it, and `snapshot_state` currently omits it. See `core/assoc_bridge.py`. |
| `server/data/til/snippets/<kind>/<stem>.facts.json` | The **TIL fact-extraction protocol** — `{schema_version, source_kind, source_ref, source_url, source_title, source_date, source_lang, facts: [{subject, subject_raw, text, fact_class, entities, when}], run_id, ts}`. Everything one article or news digest stated, enumerated literally, written beside the snippet that produced it (so the record and its source text share a stem, and both travel in a runnable snapshot). Written at fetch (news) / at Apply (wander), and backfilled for older material by `til_wander.drain_facts_backlog` during a reflection run's ingestion phase. Consumed by the facts-tree build and associative feed; selected claims can reach chat or source recall through those channels. It is a per-source protocol, separate from `rag_memory.jsonl`, and can be regenerated from the retained text. Subjects here are entity MENTIONS, not person keys — see `core/til_facts.py` |
| `server/inference/prompts/chat_prompt.txt` | System prompt loaded at server startup. It no longer opens with the "what you are is not yet decided" paragraph (moved 2026-08-06 to `persona_undecided_prompt.txt` — see the row below); its closing "Your character is not fixed. It accumulates" paragraph stays, and is what still carries that idea on the paths that inject no persona block |
| `server/inference/prompts/persona_undecided_prompt.txt` | The persona slot's **empty state** — "what you are is not yet decided … treat it as an open question". It was the second paragraph of `chat_prompt.txt`, i.e. unconditional base framing, which meant that once a digest matured every turn carried it *beside* the standing portrait's "this is who you have become … your settled way of being": two standing blocks answering one question with opposite answers. It is not base framing but the channel's degenerate case, so it moved here and is injected in the slot the portrait occupies — **exactly one block per turn, portrait when there is one, this when there is not** (`generation._load_persona_undecided`, default-written on first miss like `api_client_system_prompt.txt`). The recall fallback deliberately still keys on the *portrait*, not on this: framing and per-statement recall are not substitutes, so a thin corpus gets this **and** the `[persona]` RAG channel — what it already got. The Chat tab's **Persona** checkbox gates all three, which is what makes "all three RAG boxes off = adapter only" literally true (the paragraph used to survive every unchecking). Composed on chat + the public API; gossip falls back to it when `render_digest_for_introduction` is empty. **Not** composed by the other consumers of the base prompt (check-in's two passes, the encounter Ava-side system, branch replay's `default_system_prompt` fallback), which inject no portrait and so simply lose the paragraph. It is also **out of the training prefix**: `dialogue_source` builds that from the session-level `chat.get("system_prompt")`, so a paragraph in `chat_prompt.txt` reaches every trained row while a block in this slot never does (the same asymmetry that keeps the portrait out of the weights) — chats logged before 2026-08-06 keep their stored copy, so the corpus is non-uniform on it |
| `server/inference/prompts/api_client_system_prompt.txt` | Wrapper that frames a CALLING TOOL's own system message on the public API (`{instructions}` slot) — presents it as the working brief for the exchange (the shape of the task, the surface she is speaking into) rather than as a replacement identity. Default-written on first miss like `gossip_prompt.txt`, so an operator can retune it without a restart; used only when `api.client_system` is `"append"` (loaded by `generation._load_api_client_system_template`) |
| `server/inference/prompts/rag_prompt.txt` | Template for past-chat RAG context injection — the **chat-facing** wrapper ("…unless clearly useful to the user") |
| `server/inference/prompts/rag_reflect_prompt.txt` | The past-chat block wrapped for a pass that is **not in a conversation** — same excerpts, framed as records of what was already said rather than turns to answer. Rides the same `reflect_framing=True` switch as `rag_memory_reflect_prompt.txt`. It matters for the reflect-factory callers that leave the chat channel ON — **outreach ("Reach Out")**, til_wander's learn/wander passes, the prompt experiment; every `reflection_runner` pass sets `rag_include_chat=False`, and synthesis/check-in/deliberation disable RAG entirely. Missing file ⇒ falls back to `rag_prompt.txt` |
| `server/inference/prompts/rag_memory_prompt.txt` | Template for the distilled reflection-memory block (open questions / recalls) — the **chat-facing** wrapper, used by every path that is about to write a reply |
| `server/inference/prompts/rag_memory_reflect_prompt.txt` | The same block wrapped for a pass that is **judging, not answering** (`RagEngine.query(reflect_framing=True)`, set by `generation._make_sync_reflect_generate`, so it reaches every reflection pass and no chat-shaped one). Identical records; the chat template's reply-writing directives ("use them only if they fit the moment", "do not announce that you are consulting notes", what to ask "as you close a reply") are replaced by their negation — nothing here is a task, no one is waiting on a reply — because that block lands LAST in a reflection system message and was steering the revision pass into re-answering the exchange it was asked to judge. Missing file ⇒ falls back to the chat template |
| `server/inference/prompts/user_notes_prompt.txt` | Per-session user-notes pass (`{person}` slot) — "what did I learn about this person?", emitting `[impression]` lines. Carries the pass's two disciplines: *notice, do not diagnose* (an observation she can stand behind, not a verdict she cannot) and *writing nothing is a correct answer* (a conversation that revealed little about the person is ordinary; generalities true of anyone dilute the portrait's evidence ranking). Loaded by `reflection_runner._load_user_notes_prompt`; missing file ⇒ the pass is skipped |
| `server/inference/prompts/user_portrait_prompt.txt` | User-portrait synthesis (`{person}` slot) — folds one person's clustered observations into the five facets `WHO`/`CARES`/`WAYS`/`WITH_ME`/`UNSURE`. Each observation arrives marked with how many separate conversations it surfaced in, and the prompt asks for that difference to show in the prose (a pattern written as who they are, a one-off written as a moment). Loaded by `user_digest.load_portrait_prompt` |
| `server/inference/prompts/self_notes_prompt.txt` | Per-session **self-notes** pass (no slots) — the OUTSIDE-view counterpart of `user_notes_prompt.txt`: read this conversation back the way a stranger with only the transcript would, and record what the replies show about the one who wrote them, as `[impression]` lines (the shared line tag; the writer stamps the kind). It cannot see her `<think>` — the content builder strips it — and the prompt says so, because that is the whole premise. Carries the same two disciplines as its sibling (*notice, do not diagnose*; *writing nothing is correct*) plus one of its own: the unflattering readings are the useful ones, since a reading that only confirms what she already believed about herself adds nothing the inside view did not have. Loaded by `reflection_runner._load_self_notes_prompt`; missing file ⇒ the pass is skipped |
| `server/inference/prompts/self_portrait_prompt.txt` | **Outside-view portrait** synthesis (no slots) — folds the clustered observations into `SEEMS`/`RETURNS_TO`/`WAYS`/`HOW_I_LAND`/`UNSURE`. States explicitly that this is not the self-portrait built from her reflections and that where the two disagree this one is not automatically wrong. Loaded by `self_portrait.load_portrait_prompt` |
| `server/inference/prompts/chat_facts_prompt.txt` | Per-session **chat-facts protocol** pass (no slots) — write down everything the conversation established, one `[fact]` line each, marked `(about: NAME)` and `(class: standing\|stated\|event)`. A fact about **herself** takes the reserved `(about: self)` — she appears in these transcripts as a nameless `Me`, and both that and `the user` would file the fact as being about no one (see `core/chat_facts.py` → `SELF_SUBJECT`). Its discipline is the inverse of every other extraction prompt on the box: *do not select* — the brand of cognac, what someone wears to the office, which city their sister lives in are exactly the lines a protocol holds and a memory pass discards. Witness, not interpreter: record what was said, never what it suggests about anyone (that is the notes passes' job). Also asks for `(entities: …)` — the other nodes the fact touches — and, on an `event` only, `(when: …)` resolved against the conversation's own date (supplied by `reflection_source.session_date_line` above the transcript; the reflect lane's temporal anchor gives *today*, which is the wrong referent for a chat reflected weeks later). Loaded by `reflection_runner._load_chat_facts_prompt`; missing file ⇒ the pass is skipped |
| `server/inference/prompts/til_gist_prompt.txt` | The **TIL recap** pass (no slots; the per-kind framing is composed by `til_gist.framing_for`) — write down what you just read, for yourself, so you can later remember having read it. Explicitly *not* a summary of an article: it names the reader (her, later, when something she took from the text has come back with no memory of where it came from) so the pass writes recall rather than an abstract. Three things asked for — what this was, what it actually said, what stayed with you — plus one this lane needs and a chat gist never does: **say where the source's tone must not be mistaken for its truth**, the approved wiki list being chosen for register (Lurkmore, Neolurk, WikiTropes are humour wikis) and that being the first thing a bare summary loses. Loaded by `til_wander.run_gist_pass`; missing file ⇒ the pass is skipped, the same degrade-to-previous-behaviour contract its sibling passes have |
| `server/inference/prompts/til_facts_prompt.txt` | The **TIL protocol** pass (no slots; the per-kind framing note is composed by `til_facts.framing_for`) — write down everything an article or news digest states, one `[fact]` line each, marked `(about: SUBJECT)` `(class: …)` `(entities: …)` and, on an event, `(when: …)` resolved against the TEXT's own date. Same *do not select* discipline as its chat sibling, plus three things only this lane needs: **record what the text says even where it is wrong** (a protocol of a text is a record of that text; how much the source is to be believed is decided later from the provenance), **write the subject in full** ("New York", not "New" — the failure the lane's subject namespace exists to avoid), and **she is not in the record** (nothing about what she thought of it or what it made her wonder — that is the reflection she writes separately, and it is the one thing a curation prompt on this lane invites). Loaded by `til_wander.run_facts_pass`; missing file ⇒ the pass is skipped, the same degrade-to-previous-behaviour contract its sibling passes have |
| `server/inference/prompts/fact_fetch_prompt.txt` | **Stage 1 of a two-stage chat turn** (no slots) — given a catalogue of every node in the facts tree plus the conversation and the message that just arrived, say which entries are worth looking up before it is answered. The answer is a list of ids under `NODES:` (or `NONE`), at most 6, **no reasoning** — the pass runs thinking-off (`ModuleSpec.disable_thinking`) because it SELECTS rather than writes, and the blob is rendered from the tree in code (`graph.blob.render_blob`), which is what stops a generation paraphrasing a claim into something no source said. The prompt therefore carries only two disciplines: *you are looking something up, not answering it* (nothing written here reaches the person), and *picking nothing is a correct answer* — a catalogue line matching a word in the message but not its point is a wrong pick, not a cautious one. Loaded by the `fact_fetch` module; `graph.blob`'s self-test asserts the shipped file still asks for the contract the parser reads |
| `server/inference/prompts/facts_reading_block_prompt.txt` | Wrapper around a fetched facts blob on the **reading** lane (`{facts}` slot) — the sibling of `facts_block_prompt.txt`, which is written for a pass about to answer somebody ("work into the reply") where this one is about to write down what it read. Default-written on first miss by `til_wander._load_reading_block_template`. Its closing sentence is the **anti-narrowing rule** and is load-bearing: every candidate this box can currently offer is a claim about the one person Ava talks to, so a block injected into a news reading without it pulls the recap toward being about him rather than about the events |
| `server/inference/prompts/facts_decision_block_prompt.txt` | Wrapper around a fetched facts blob on the **decision** lane (`{facts}` slot) — outreach's decision pass weighing one of Ava's own open asks. The one thing it must say that no sibling does: an already-answered question is what `resolved` is for. Default-written on first miss by `outreach._load_decision_block_template` |
| `server/inference/prompts/facts_reread_block_prompt.txt` | Wrapper around a fetched facts blob on the **re-read** lane (`{facts}` slot) — synthesis's analysis pass forming questions against an aged chat. Its distinguishing sentence: a question one of these facts already answers is not worth raising, and noticing that is itself part of the synthesis. Default-written on first miss by `synthesis._load_reread_block_template` |
| `server/inference/prompts/ask_origin_prompt.txt` | Wrapper for the standing **ASK ORIGIN** block on an Ava-initiated session's reply turns (`{question}`/`{origin}` slots) — the question the session was opened to raise plus the material it came from, so the user's answer is read against what made her ask. Closes on the do-not-recite discipline (background for reading the answer, not material for the reply — "if their answer settles the question, let it be settled"), which matters because the block stands on every turn of the session. Default-written on first miss by `ask_origin.load_block_template`; a hand-emptied file falls back to the default rather than injecting a bare gist with no discipline around it |
| `server/inference/prompts/prompt_mutation_prompt.txt` | The **prompt-mutation** pass (`{current_prompt}`/`{persona}`/`{locator}` slots) — the standing-prompt counterfactual on one exchange: `VERDICT: prompt-adequate | prompt-gap` + `DRIFT`/`MISSING`/`DELTA`/`SCOPE`. Logged-only (`core/prompt_mutation.py`). The `{locator}` slot (2026-09-18) is filled by `prompt_mutation.locator_note` with which cell of the verdict × tension square sent the exchange here: a `revise` ("the reply drifted, the revision already wrote the better one") or a kept-but-torn one ("the reply was yours; was the conflict behind it a standing one?") — the file's original framing read over a kept reply would have steered the pass into inventing a drift. A customized file without the slot gets the note prepended. |
| `server/inference/prompts/prompt_pattern_cluster_prompt.txt` | The **prompt-pattern grouping** pass (no slots; `GROUP: <numbers>` output, the persona-cluster contract) — group numbered proposed prompt lines that ask for the SAME change, judged by what the line would make her do, with DIRECTION overriding surface similarity. Default-written on first miss by `prompt_patterns.load_cluster_prompt`. |
| `server/inference/prompts/prompt_rewrite_prompt.txt` | The rewrite event's **draft** pass (`{current_prompt}`/`{evidence}` slots) — the experiment prompt reframed for her own decision: settle the listed pulls by taking a side, keep what still holds, a prompt is not improved by growing; `<new_prompt>` block out. Default-written on first miss by `core/prompt_rewrite.py`. |
| `server/inference/prompts/prompt_consensus_prompt.txt` | The rewrite event's **consensus** pass (`{count}` slot) — over the drafts, NOTES not prose: `AGREED:` (what most drafts share) and `ONLY_SOME:` (noted, not adopted). A draft is one sitting's version of her; what recurs across them is the settled intention. Default-written. |
| `server/inference/prompts/prompt_choice_prompt.txt` | The rewrite event's **choice** pass (`{evidence}`/`{persona}` slots) — the branch-judge shape over letter-labelled prompts: incumbent, drafts, final; `CHOICE: <letter>` + `WHY`. Keeping what she has is a real choice; length, polish and completeness must not decide. Default-written. |
| `server/inference/prompts/recollection_prompt.txt` | Revisit recollection pass — after re-reading an old conversation, what she NOW makes of it (`RECOLLECTION:` 1-3 sentences first-person + `TRIGGER:` what should bring it back). Loaded by `reflection_runner._load_recollection_prompt`; missing file ⇒ the pass is skipped |
| `server/inference/prompts/rag_wander_prompt.txt` | *(channel OFF pending redesign — no caller requests it)* Template for the wander-channel block — an article Ava read on her own + her reaction, injected as soft "something you read on your own" background so its phrasing can bleed (`{context}` placeholder) |
| `server/inference/prompts/surface_prompt.txt` | *(block OFF pending redesign — `_INJECT_OPEN_ASKS`; the machinery is kept so one flag restores it)* Template for the proactive open-question block injected at the start of a session (`{questions}` placeholder) |
| `server/inference/prompts/sleep_prompt.txt` | Consolidation reflection prompt (loaded by `reflection_prompts.py`; overridable per run). Directs each `[fact]` concerning a person to be marked `(about: NAME)` — the subject, which is not always the speaker; that mark drives the attribution + hearsay gate in `reflection_writer.py` |
| `server/inference/prompts/revision_prompt.txt` | Revision-pass prompt (loaded by `reflection_prompts.py`; overridable per run) |
| `server/inference/prompts/deliberation_prompt.txt` | Deliberation decision prompt — shows Ava her recent worklog (each line dated), open threads and where things stand, and asks what she would do next (`ACTION: wander\|synthesize\|reach_out\|check_in\|revisit\|aha\|pivot\|nothing` + `WHY:`). Since 2026-09-21 it groups the options by whether anyone hears from her — wander and revisit are private, the rest may end in a message — and says rest is for not crowding a person, not for not reading (the rest-fixation fix; see `core/deliberation.py`).
